## CE-11b unit U2: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass (`5da0e4ec` and `e2a64e51`) at `e2a64e51` found that the Lean diff of the four modules from `091b9b48` changes docstrings only (the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` and the docstrings of `idealSplit` and `ideal_to_split` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer`): no statement, definition or proof changed. It rechecked `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer` and the root import with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper without `-o` against the fix pass's prefix (exit 0, empty logs). It compared the receipt with the branch head: the file hashes, the theorem and definition lists, the counts (77, 10, 44 and 55 theorems with 186 exact guards, each naming its own theorem and allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), the `axiom_profile` entries (keyed exactly by the theorems), the module commits, the final-check records and artifact hashes, and the inventory records and rows. It checked this guide, the analysis, the registry descriptions of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase`, the working plan's section 58 and the CE-11b design's section 22 against the findings, and that central `07551131` contains unit U5 (`db7bda73`). It found every finding of the review of `091b9b48` resolved and raised no new finding.

This commit records the review in this section and the status paragraph, the Review paragraph of the U2 section of the analysis (whose first paragraph no longer says that the work is not integrated), the receipt (`review.confirming_review` and the plan record) and the committed plan snapshot, refreshed from the working plan through section 58 (sections 52 to 58, with the confirming-review paragraph at the end of section 58; sections 52 to 55 are the ones that central received with unit U5). No Lean file, inventory row or check changes; the integration's own checks run on the topic tip after this commit.

## CE-11b unit U6: what is proved

This first part of unit U6 adds three modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 72 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains three imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch` | 44 | The key match of the ideal registration (design-v2 section 7.2, item 2, `ideal_keymatch`; listed under U4 in the design, built here because U6's decision step needs it and U4 is not on central). The trace invariant `KeyTrace` relates the secrecy ledger's transcript to the deployment state with the ideal backend: the session counter equals the raw-accept counter; every accept entry that returned a session returned one below the counter; an accept entry that returned the selected ordinal has bytes that pass the native verification prefix and, when they verify to the selected PQ key, the selected session is marked; every owner that still holds a KEM key pair holds the challenger's pool entry at its owner index (`PoolOwners`); every published bundle is the Phase 1 writer output of a candidate whose PQ key is the pool entry at its index (`BundleKey`). Every request of the native interface keeps it (`handler_trace`), and so does every query of the complete interface, the secrecy commands included (`coinSend_keeps`, `query_trace` on `ledgerHandler`, the secrecy layer composed with the handler); it holds at the start (`keyTrace_initial`) and on every outcome of `gameWith` with the ideal registration (`game_trace`). The per-command facts are stated on the slot program (`prog_create`, `prog_bundle`, `prog_accept`, `prog_build`, `prog_finish`, `prog_hmac`, through `slotCore_support`, `idealReg_support`, `realRegAt_support`, `onNative_native`, `onBase_support`, `createPool_facts` and the native facts `reg_accept`, `reg_sessions`, `reg_bundle`, `reg_finish`, `reg_shared_owners`, `nativeOwner_kem` with `PoolOwners`, `poolOwners_alike`, `poolOwners_set`, `poolOwners_append`, `terminal_kem`) and cover the CE-native hidden steps: the selected accept (`acceptExposed_facts`, from `acceptInactive_facts`, `acceptActive_facts`, `tailCE_tail`, `tailCE_client`, `program_marker`: a returned session is the session counter before the call and, on the active path, follows the hidden root read that marks it), the marked build (`buildHidden_sessions`) and owner k's challenge finish (`finishChallenge_facts`, `groupCE_sessions`). `ideal_keymatch`: under `Phase1ReadBack`, on every outcome, a designation of the target's raw-accept ordinal and KEM creation index on the final transcript implies that the selected session is marked; `target_marked` is the same for a CE designation `g` through `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.target`. Further: the mark changes only at the selected accept (`handler_core`), no run beyond the selected ordinal changes it (`run_settled`), and the selected accept on bytes that do not verify to the selected PQ key leaves none (`handler_accept_inactive`); `keeps_any`, `quiet_entry`, `keyTrace_quiet` are tools. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBranch` | 17 | The branches of the second compile lemma at the cut. The CE decision reads the mark: with `target_marked`, the CE decision on the experiment with the ideal registration (`ceDecide` on `classicalOf`) equals the decision together with the mark (`markedDecision`, `game_marked`); `ideal_cutMarked` and `ideal_prefixMarked` are U5's `ideal_cut` and `ideal_prefix` for a decision that also reads the mark (`marked`, `resumeMarked`). Item 2's side at the cut: `slotGameAt_cut` writes item 2's two-slot world at the challenger's draws as the Server construction, the common prefix and `itemRest` (the rest of item 2's opaque scan under the stopped router, then item 2's post-challenge phase `itemPost`). The branches without an active cut accept nothing on both sides: a failed construction (`itemPost_failed`), a completed cut and a pending cut whose raw bytes do not verify to the selected PQ key (`itemRest_dead`, through `opaqueRest_dead`, `rejectRun_complete`, `optionRun_support`, `itemPost_dead`; `resumeMarked_dead`, through `map_const_lawEq`). The CE budget at the cut: `scan_certified`, `transported_unread`, `transported_pool`. `CutRelIndexed` strengthens U5's `CutRel` by the owner positions (owner `j`'s active KEM key pair is pool entry `j`) and the remaining creations of the continuation; `cut_relIndexed` proves it on every outcome of the common prefix from a source budget of the whole compiled program. `ideal_compile_of_selected`: under the approved codec contracts and the budget, `ideal_compile` (in the design's form, over `CE.idealGame g`) holds once the selected-key branch `SelectedBranch` holds at every draw of the challenger. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealActive` | 11 | The selected accept at the cut. Item 2's steps before encapsulation issue no static DH request (`beforeLogged_plain`), so under the stopped router they run as coins with the router state kept (`stopped_coins`, `stopped_plain`, `stopped_program`) and are the native steps before encapsulation of any seed with the logged scalar forgotten (`stopped_before`); hence `opaqueRest_selected` and, in law, `itemRest_selected` (with the compiled continuation `compiledNext`). On the CE side the selected accept at the canonical state at the cut is `selectedStep` on `acceptActive`, whose steps before encapsulation pass through the first-touch allocator as coins (`acceptStep_selected`); `resumeMarked_selected` writes the CE side in law as the same native steps followed by nothing accepted (a rejection before encapsulation leaves no mark, which no later step changes) or `activeCE` (the selected accept from its steps after encapsulation, `acceptAfter`, then the continuation). `selected_of_active` and `ideal_compile_of_active` reduce the selected-key branch, and `ideal_compile`, to the active branch `ActiveBranch`. |

## CE-11b unit U6: decisions and deviations

- `ideal_keymatch` is built in U6 (design-v2 section 11 placed it in U4, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`, which is not on central), in its own module. Its conclusion is that the selected session is marked (`marks.isSome` on the final ideal backend). That a mark implies an active selected accept is proved, in the form the branches use: nothing is marked before the cut or at the canonical state at the cut (`ideal_cutMarked`), the mark changes only at the selected ordinal (`handler_core`, `run_settled`), and an inactive selected accept or a rejection before encapsulation leaves no mark (`handler_accept_inactive`, `resumeMarked_dead`, `resumeMarked_selected`). That on the active path the mark is set exactly when the selected accept returned a session is not proved yet: `tailCE_tail` (with `tailCE_client`, `acceptActive_facts` and `acceptExposed_facts`) gives only one direction, that a returned session is marked; the converse, that a mark set by the selected accept comes with a returned session, is a lemma of the decision step (open point (5) below). The decision step needs both directions, because item 2's decision counts a run only when the selected accept returned a session (`PqxdhRegistrationJointAcceptCutTag.decideTag`, the decision and `accepted`), while the CE decision reads the mark (`markedDecision`, the decision and the mark). The invariant is proved on the whole game, because the ledger that the designation reads is a value inside the wrapped adversary at the cut.
- The CE decision is rewritten once on the whole game to the decision together with the mark (`game_marked`). The CE sides of the completed and inactive branches are then false without any transcript reasoning after the cut: the canonical state at the cut marks nothing and only an active selected accept marks a session. Because U5's `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` is published (frozen), `ideal_cut` and `ideal_prefix` are re-proved for a decision that reads the mark (`ideal_cutMarked`, `ideal_prefixMarked`) instead of being generalized in place; the decision reads only the mark, not the whole backend, because at a completed cut the backend's accept counter differs from the canonical state's.
- The owner-entry index invariant ("owner `j` holds pool entry `j`") is a field of the new relation `CutRelIndexed` beside U5's `CutRel` (frozen), together with the remaining creations (`cut_residual`); `cut_relIndexed` takes the source budget of the whole compiled program, which implies the scan's.
- The comparison is split into explicit obligations instead of one theorem: `SelectedBranch` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealBranch`) and `ActiveBranch` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealActive`) are `Prop` definitions, and `ideal_compile_of_selected`, `selected_of_active` and `ideal_compile_of_active` are reductions. They are intermediate results of the unit, not assumptions: nothing outside these reductions takes them as premises, and `ideal_compile` itself is not claimed.
- Modules: the design's U6 modules were `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile`. This part publishes `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBranch` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealActive` (all registered); `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` (the active branch) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` (the final `ideal_compile` from `ideal_compile_of_active`) remain.
- The steps before encapsulation (U5's open accept step) are compared at the program level: they issue no static DH request, so the private identity service is never consulted, the stopped router passes them through as coins, and the comparison holds for any seed; the Server's identity slot (`cut_owned`) is needed only after encapsulation (the static DH of the suffix).
- Publication: three Lean commits (one per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block) and one documentation commit, on branch `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut` over central `959b7154`; not reviewed, not integrated.

## CE-11b unit U6: names for consumers

- For the rest of U6 (the active branch and `ideal_compile`): `ActiveBranch`, `activeCE`, `acceptAfter`, `selectedStep`, `compiledNext`, `ideal_compile_of_active` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealActive`); `CutRelIndexed`, `cut_relIndexed`, `itemPost`, `itemRest`, `markedDecision`, `marked`, `resumeMarked`, `ideal_compile_of_selected` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealBranch`).
- For U4 and CE-12: `ideal_keymatch`, `target_marked`, `KeyTrace`, `game_trace`, `ledgerHandler`, `query_trace`, `handler_core`, `run_settled` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch`); design-v2 section 7.2 lists `ideal_keymatch` as the basis of U4's `ideal_trace` (L3, L4).
- Generic tools: `slotCore_support`, `keeps_any`, `onBase_support`, `optionRun_support`, `map_const_lawEq`, `stopped_coins`, `stopped_program`.

## CE-11b unit U6: contracts used

The approved codec contracts `Phase1ReadBack` (`ideal_keymatch`, `game_marked`, the reductions) and `Phase2ReadBack` (through U5's prefix lemmas), and the CE budget (`budget.Respects adversary`, through CE-9's transported caps). No new assumption or contract. `SelectedBranch` and `ActiveBranch` are obligations of this unit, not premises of any published claim.

## CE-11b unit U6: open points

- The active branch `ActiveBranch` (module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost`, registered): item 2's post-challenge phase on the active cut against `activeCE`. Plan, in order: (1) the selected accept after encapsulation: the compiled suffix (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueSuffix.compiled` under the skeleton's resumption interpreter `resumeImpl`, the actual encapsulator, the post router `PqxdhDynamicKemJointRouter.post` and the CCA evaluation `evalLayer`) against `acceptAfter`'s suffix under the native callbacks, where the static DH reads the Server's seed at its identity slot (`cut_owned`), and the marker tail (`resumeTail` against `tailCE`, U1's `tailCE_challenge`); (2) the post router never stops: the creations after the cut fit the reservation (`CutRelIndexed.residual` with the router state of the cut, in the form of `PqxdhDynamicKemStopping.normal_capacity`); (3) the flattening of item 2's post stack (grouped client, skeleton, actual encapsulator, post router, CCA evaluation, public table, first-touch allocator) into one stateful handler on the product state, and the extraction of the CE side's per-step allocator into one outer allocator run (`slotCore` against an outer `slotImpl`); (4) the post-challenge relation `PostRel` (CE-3's merge invariant at the source state, item 2's `SplitN`/`join` up to `eraseObservers`, the marks as coordinates against values read from the table, the allocator and table equal, the router state, the install ghosts ignored) and the per-command simulation (registration commands through CE-3's `step_merge` and item 2's split lemmas `groupedImpl_split`, `split_build`, `split_group`; the hidden steps through `tailCE_challenge`, `groupCE_challenge`, `groupCE_known`, `preparationCE_challenge`, `preparationCE_eval`; records through CE-3's `server_record`, `beacon_record` and `reveal_step` of the increment 2 spike; every case is listed in the checklist below); (5) the decision step (item 2's `skelDecide` with `decideTag` against `markedDecision`: the result and ledger agree, CE-3's merge gives the produced frames, and on the active path the mark is set exactly when the selected accept returned a session). For the last part, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch.tailCE_tail` proves only one direction: a returned session is marked. The converse is to be proved in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost`: from an unmarked state, a mark set by `tailCE` on the marker client comes with a successful reply (the converse of `tailCE_client`: `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueClient.client` returns `ok (some ...)` on the only path that issues the root request), and the selected accept's tail then returns the session `some state.sessions` (the converse of `tailCE_tail`: `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueTail.tail` publishes the token and returns `some index` on that reply); lifted to the steps after encapsulation (`acceptAfter`) and, by `run_settled`, to the end of the run, it gives that the final mark is set exactly when the selected accept's public reply is a session, which the per-command simulation relates to item 2's `accepted` tag.
- Checklist for the totality of step (4): `PostRel` must be kept by every constructor of `PqxdhDeploymentInterface.Spec`, that is the coins and the eleven commands that `handler_core` distinguishes, on every branch, the failure branches included. The CE side is `handlerWith` with `idealReg` (the slot program `idealProg` for the registration commands, the base handler for the others); item 2's side is CE-9's `compileImpl`, then the grouped client `groupedClient` and the skeleton `skelImpl` under the actual encapsulator, the post router and `evalLayer`. The secrecy commands `test` and `fresh` reach both sides only inside the wrapped adversary (`PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.wrap`), as coins and `serverSend` or `beaconSend` (`ledgerImpl`, `send`), and the observation mode only maps the raw replies to a success bit (`asModeO`), so they need no case of their own.
  - Coins: the CE handler lifts the coin; item 2's side forwards it (`compileImpl` as a source coin, then the grouped client and the skeleton). No failure branch.
  - `create`: `createReg` with `createPool` at pool entry `owners.length` against the forwarded source create, whose KEM key pair the post router takes from its reservation (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool.step_merge_pool`, `cut_entry_key`). Branches: a pin that does not parse (`parsePin`; no source request and the reply `none` on both sides); a creation beyond the reservation, which step (2) excludes (on the CE side `createPool_beyond`).
  - `bundle`: the real bundle (`realRegAt`, `PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.bundle`) against the forwarded source bundle (`forwardLogged`). Branches: an absent owner (reply `none`, no draw); a failed bundle, which keeps the owner returned by the generation (`PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.bundle` sets it even on failure).
  - `accept` at the ordinals after the selected one: the real accept (`acceptStep_other`, `PqxdhServerAuthNative.nativeAccept`) against the skeleton's accept (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemPostSkeletonServices.acceptStep`, whose encapsulation goes through `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemEncapsulation.prefixImpl` and the actual encapsulator). Branches: a rejection before encapsulation, a rejection after it (no root request), and a returned session. On every branch the advanced session counter and the consumed IDs are installed before the client runs (`PqxdhRegistrationJointServerAccept.tail`, and `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueTail.tail` for the selected accept, issue the install before their match), so a rejected accept still advances the session counter (`acceptStep_accepts`). Raw bytes that verify to the selected PQ key at a later ordinal are encapsulated afresh on both sides; their ciphertexts reach owner `k`'s finish as non-challenge ciphertexts (below).
  - `build`: the marked session with a pending token runs `buildHidden` after a read of the allocator at its coordinates, against item 2's `buildStep` with the hidden service of the marked session (`buildService`; marks as coordinates against marks as values, `buildHidden_native`); every other build is the real build (`buildStep_unmarked`, `buildStep_other`, `buildStep_real`) against item 2's `nativeResponse`. Branches: no pending token (reply `none`, state unchanged on both sides; `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.buildStep_idle` in the environment form); a failed build, which still consumes the token (`PqxdhRegistrationScheduleState.build_consumes`, `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.buildStep_consumes`; item 2's `buildStep` clears the pending token on every outcome); the Server install ghost of a published peer, which `PostRel` ignores.
  - `finish`: owner `k` in the `initSent` phase after the active selected accept takes `finishChallenge` (`groupCE`, `groupedOwnerCE`, `preparationCE`) against the grouped client and `groupStep`: the challenge ciphertext reads the hidden value (`groupCE_challenge`, `preparationCE_challenge`), and any other ciphertext is decapsulated by the router through `evalLayer` and reads its root from the table (`groupCE_known`, `preparationCE_eval`); every other finish is the real finish (`finishStep_real`) against a grouped finish with a known secret. Branches: a DH output rejected by `groupStep`'s nonzero check (reply `none`); a rejected frame or decapsulation, after which the owner is aborted (a finish from `initSent` always installs a terminal phase, `PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.finish_terminal`, `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.finishStep_terminal`); a finish at a terminal owner, which has no effect (`PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.no_retry`, `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.finishStep_retry`); an absent owner or an owner in another phase; the Beacon install ghost and the LQ-F snapshot of owner `k`'s challenge finish, which `PostRel` ignores.
  - `hmac`: the native HMAC (`PqxdhServerAuthNative.nativeHmac` through `realRegAt`) against item 2's `hmacStep`. Branches: the zero-key 192-byte slice, which both sides answer from the restricted Extract table (item 2 by a direct table read; the CE side also records an observer touch, which `PostRel` erases with the other observers), and every other input (the real function on both sides).
  - `hash`: the native BLAKE2b oracle on the CTX cache (`PqxdhServerAuthNative.nativeHash`, through the base handler) against the forwarded source hash, which the skeleton answers by `hashGame` on its own CTX cache. No failure branch.
  - `serverSend` and `serverRaw`: the native Server record step (`PqxdhDeploymentNative.serverStep`) against `compileImpl`'s `serverLocal` on the local copies, which issues at most one `.reveal` (`peerKernel`), answered by the skeleton's `revealKernel` (CE-3's `server_record`), including the selected session's peer after its hidden build. Branches: a raw frame that routes to no peer (`serverTarget` is `none`), a key ID without a live kernel, a rejected frame (reply `none` with the raw-attempt counter updated) and a success with its produced frames.
  - `beaconSend` and `beaconRaw`: the native Beacon record step (`PqxdhDeploymentNative.beaconStep`) against `beaconLocal`, which issues at most one `.kernel` read (`ownerKernel`), answered by the grouped owners (CE-3's `beacon_record`), including owner `k` after its hidden establishment. Branches: an uncreated handle (`PqxdhDeploymentNative.absent`, no source request), an owner that is not established, a rejected frame (with the owner's raw-attempt counter updated) and a success with its produced frames.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` (registered): `ideal_compile` from `ideal_compile_of_active` and the active branch.
- U4's `ideal_trace` (L3, L4) can reuse `ideal_keymatch` and `KeyTrace` from this unit.
- Integration (PROTOCOL section 6): hot spots as for U5 (this guide, the root imports, the inventory rows, the check-inventory blocks, the analysis); the u3 track (`codex/deployment-ce11b`) may add sections and rows in the same places. At the review fix pass central was at `5ba38799`: a read-only simulated merge of the topic with it conflicts only in `check-inventory.sh` and `reviewed-inventory.txt` (the root imports and the analysis merge without conflict), and the merge needed `inv recount` (1720 handwritten Lean files on central plus this unit's three, 1723) before `inv rehash`. Central is now at `194016ff` (status paragraph), with eleven more handwritten Lean files at the same anchors, so the count after the merge is 1734. The committed plan snapshot is refreshed through section 64 by the commit that records the confirming review; central's snapshot is the one this branch started from, so the merge keeps this branch's version.

## CE-11b unit U6: re-estimate

Figures in nominal agent-days. U6 was 6.75-11.5. This first part (the key match, which the design counted under U4, the dead branches, the reduction and the steps before encapsulation) took about 1-1.5; the key match moves 1-1.5 from U4 to U6 (U4 becomes 3.5-6.5). Remaining in U6: the active branch, 4.5-8 (the selected accept after encapsulation 0.5-1, the no-stop and flattening 1.5-2.5, `PostRel` and the per-command simulation 2-3.5, the decision 0.5-1), and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` about 0.25.

## CE-11b unit U6: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `f7f0baaa` found no error in the Lean statements or proofs. The reviewers rechecked the three modules and the root import at `f7f0baaa` with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper without `-o` (exit 0, empty logs); counted 44, 17 and 11 theorems with as many exact guards allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound` and no forbidden token; read `ideal_compile_of_selected` against the design's statement of `ideal_compile`, and `SelectedBranch`, `ActiveBranch`, `activeCE`, `acceptAfter`, `KeyTrace`, `ideal_keymatch`, `target_marked`, `handler_core`, `run_settled` and `handler_accept_inactive`; checked against the Rust reference (`get_shared_secret` in `beaconcrypt/src/server.rs`) that the Server's steps before encapsulation (status, reservation, prekey and one-time parsing, ephemeral, KEM key parsing) use no identity key, as `beforeLogged` and `stopped_before` model them; compared every row of the reviewed inventory with the committed files at each commit of the unit (2126 to 2129 rows, handwritten Lean files 1706 to 1709, no mismatch, missing path, duplicate or unlisted Lean file); evaluated the unit's `check-inventory` blocks on an extract of each Lean commit (91, 128 and 153 checks, no failure); reran the focused `check-inventory` on `f7f0baaa` under a `nix` lease with a clean tree (exit 0); compared the receipt with the branch; and checked that the track's prefix `ce11b/u6/olean` resolves like the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `959b7154` apart from the root olean and the three new oleans. Their findings and the fixes:

- Minor: the decisions above said that the key match implies, in the active branch, that the selected accept returned a session, and open point (5) said that the mark is set exactly when the selected accept returned a session, citing `tailCE_tail`. `tailCE_tail` proves only one direction (a returned session is marked); nothing in the three modules proves the converse, which the decision step needs because item 2's `decideTag` reads whether the selected accept returned a session while `markedDecision` reads the mark. The converse holds by inspection of `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueClient.client` and `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueTail.tail`. Fixed in the decisions above (a mark implies an active selected accept is proved, with the lemmas that prove it; the equivalence of the mark and a returned session on the active path is not proved yet) and in open point (5), which now lists the converses of `tailCE_client` and `tailCE_tail` as lemmas of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` and how they reach the decision.

## CE-11b unit U3: what is proved

Unit U3 adds six modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 224 guarded theorems (217 at the review; the review fix pass added seven); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains six imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvReal` | 42 | CE-11a's real registration commands on any backend `Bk` with `n` slots (`realRegAt` and the per-command laws and admissibility of `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentReal`, generalized from the unit backend and zero slots), with the pool create in capability form (`createPoolStep`, `createPool_law`, `createPool_keeps`, `createPool_splits`) and `real_splitsAt`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvStep` | 37 | The bridge from the final split form `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer.idealSplit` to the real commands per command (`idealSplit_create`, `idealSplit_bundle`, `idealSplit_hmac`, `idealSplit_accept_other`, `idealSplit_build_real`, `idealSplit_finish_real`), the slot frames of the record coordinates, the selected raw accept in capability form (`acceptSelectedEnv`, exact law `acceptSelected_law`, `acceptSelected_keeps`) and the splits of every command without hidden install (`create_splits` to `finish_real_splits`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvBuild` | 27 | The marked build in capability form with a lazy material read (`buildHiddenEnv`): the override and shape of the generic factored build, the World's hidden Server install with its counts forgotten, the exact law `buildHidden_law`, `buildHidden_outcome` and `buildHidden_keeps`. The ideal step runs the form that reads the cached material (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.buildCachedEnv`), which equals `buildHiddenEnv` at a cached slot. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFinish` | 33 | Owner k's challenge finish in capability form (`finishChallengeEnv`): the World's hidden Beacon install, the forwarded known tail, the exact law `finishChallenge_law`, `commit_outcome`, `hiddenFinish_outcome`, `finishChallenge_outcome`, `finishChallenge_keeps` and `finishChallenge_splits`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFacts` | 53 | Facts for the admissibility predicate: World runs without hidden install keep the used markers and the seed table (`run_keep`, `realStep_free`, `createPool_free`, `acceptSelected_free`); every real command and the pool create move the Server's sessions and keep owner k terminal (`RealMove`, `realStep_move`, `createPool_move`); the selected accept reads at most one slot and its mark is at the old session counter, below the new one, at coordinates holding a slot unless the allocator is full (`tail_root`, `tailSlots_step`, `acceptExposed_facts`, `acceptSelected_move`); the hidden commands' outcomes with the seed table unchanged outside the slot (`buildHidden_exact`, `hiddenFinish_exact`) and their moves (`buildHidden_move`, `finishChallenge_move`); every allocated slot's material stays in the material cache (`AllocCached`; `material_grows`, `slotLazy_cached`, `slotsLazy_cached`, and a conjunct of `acceptSelected_move` and of `finishChallenge_move`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv` | 32 | `idealStep` (the capability form of `idealSplit t p`) and its branches, the marked build reading its slot's cached material (`buildCachedEnv`, `buildCached_hit`, `buildCached_cache`) with the build class's bound at every state (`buildCached_bound`, `idealStep_build_bound`), the admissibility predicate `IdealInv` (`idealInv_stable`, `idealInv_start`, the guards `idealInv_build_ready` and `idealInv_finish_ready`, preservation `idealInv_move`, `idealInv_realStep`, `idealInv_selected`, `idealInv_build`, `idealInv_finish`), `ideal_splits`; the environments `envG` and `envGD`; `c1_split`, `c1_ideal`, `c1_idealGame`, `c3_ideal`, `ideal_reveal`; `world_keeps`, `env_admitted`, `server_once`, `beacon_once`, `hidden_server_once`, `hidden_beacon_once`, `seed_at_install`. |

In plain terms: the ideal environment runs every registration command of the ideal registration as a record-world environment, with the hidden values it holds back from the World read lazily from its own material cache by the selected accept and owner k's challenge finish and read back from that cache without a draw by the marked build, and with the World's hidden Server and Beacon installs issued at the slots the allocator assigned. `ideal_splits` shows that, from every admissible joint state, each command run against the World has exactly the law of the final split form of U2 on the reassembled state, and keeps admissibility. `IdealInv` is the admissibility: the World's used markers are exactly the slots of the environment's install ghosts, the World's seed table holds exactly the seeds of those slots, the material cache holds the material of every allocated slot, and the allocator, marks and ghosts satisfy the guards that the marked build (an unused Server marker at an allocated slot whose material is cached) and owner k's challenge finish (room in the allocator, no Beacon marker) need. With CE-11a's `join_param`, the draw of the challenger's parameters followed by the final split game and the record world with `envGD` have the same law of the full reassembled outcome (`c1_split`); with U2's `ideal_to_split`, the ideal game and the record world with `envGD` have the same law of the adversary's output, ledger and produced frames (`c1_ideal`, and `c1_idealGame` for `CE.idealGame g`). `c3_ideal` (the final CTX cache) and `ideal_reveal` relate the record world to the draw followed by the final split game, not to the ideal game itself: `ideal_to_split` reads both games through `splitView`, which empties the CTX cache (design-v2 section 22). The build class of the ideal step stays within the planned first-touch allowance from every environment state (`idealStep_build_bound`), the build-class part of the frame `frame_idealD`.

## CE-11b unit U3: decisions and deviations

- Six modules instead of the two of design-v2 section 11 (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvReal`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv`): the selected accept, the marked build, owner k's challenge finish and the facts for the admissibility predicate each got their own registered module, so that every module compiles within the 180-second limit of a check and can be reviewed alone.
- `IdealInv` keeps design-v2 section 6.2's I1-I4 and changes I5: the marked coordinates hold an allocated slot (`markedSession`, not "slot 0"), and the marked session lies strictly below the session counter (needed to keep I3 when a later accept publishes a token); I5's first clause also says that nothing is marked, active or installed before the selected accept (`before`); the allocator's well-formedness is added (`allocBound`, `allocBelow`); the last clause says the Server ghost is at the marked coordinates (`peerMarked`, design: the ghost implies a mark); F4 is a conjunct (`seeds`), as U2 moved `seed_at_install` here; and the material cache holds every allocated slot's material (`allocCached`, added by the review fix pass). "Slot 0" is not a conjunct because no U3 step needs it: the marked build and the preservation proofs need only some allocated slot, while slot 0 would need the extra fact that the selected accept's read is the first allocation of the run. It holds on reachable states (the selected accept starts from the empty allocator, `before`, and allocates at most one slot), but `IdealInv` does not provide it; unit U4 needs it (open points).
- The marked build reads its slot's material from the environment's material cache without a draw (`buildCachedEnv`, review fix pass). The first version read it lazily (`buildHiddenEnv`, coin effects on a cache miss), which is a hit on every reachable state but made the build class draw 44 coins from states whose cache lacks the slot, so the planned frame failed (review finding). Of the review's two options this one keeps the allowance of plan sections 31, 33 and 35 (`allowanceG (baseAllowance caps) ceFirstTouch`, first-touch classes accept and finish), the CE-11a decision that the build is not a first-touch class (CE-11a's reason is that item 2's skeleton keeps the material in its marks; CE-11b's marks hold coordinates, so the build reads the material again, now from the cache without a draw), and the record-lift line's coin bills; the other (adding the build class to the first-touch set) would raise those bills by 44 per build. `IdealInv`'s new conjunct `allocCached` gives the cache hit (`idealInv_build_ready`), and at a hit the two forms are equal (`buildCached_hit`), so every law and outcome lemma of `buildHiddenEnv` applies unchanged. This is an implementation-level change: no assumption or contract is added and the allowance is unchanged.
- The strictness of the mark's session uses the marker tail's install-first structure: the active selected accept's tail is split into its first step (the install of the incremented session counter) and the rest, which issues no install (`acceptActive_facts`).
- `envGD` is `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw.paramEnv` with `drawParams` (design-v2 section 21's replacement of `bindStart`); `c1_split` is `join_param`'s instance and `c1_ideal` composes it with `ideal_to_split` pointwise under the draw (as anticipated by the U2 semantic review); `c3_ideal` and `ideal_reveal` are maps of `c1_split` (the reveal through `revealOf_fill`).
- `env_admitted`, `hidden_server_once` and `hidden_beacon_once` hold on every outcome of the record world with `envGD`, through the generic `world_keeps` (CE-11a's `run_keeps` after the counted startup). `env_admitted` states admissibility on every outcome; its `serverMarks` and `beaconMarks` say that the World's markers are exactly the install ghosts' slots. The per-step admission (each hidden install is issued from a state whose marker of the slot is unused) is not a separate theorem: it is `idealInv_build_ready` and `idealInv_finish_ready` composed with `buildHidden_outcome` and `hiddenFinish_outcome` inside `ideal_splits`.
- `seed_at_install` is proved at the reassembled state of every outcome of the record world with `envGD`, which `c1_split` relates in law to the final split game's final state. The same statement on the support of `gameWith .. splitInit (idealSplit t p)` itself needs a transfer of supports along `LawEq` (open point).
- `buildHidden_exact` and `hiddenFinish_exact` repeat the proofs of the published `buildHidden_outcome` and `hiddenFinish_outcome` with one more conjunct (the seed table is unchanged outside the slot), because published modules are not edited.
- Central moved three times during the unit (`959b7154`, `84a07b15`, `dad157b1`, the last with U6's first part); each was merged into the topic (`b17cbc8b`, `b952a6a5`, `59adae12`), and every module was checked again against the last. The review fix pass merged central `78fb56cd` (`a20610f4`, Codex's record-facts modules only).

## CE-11b unit U3: names for consumers

- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv`: `idealStep`, `IdealInv`, `ideal_splits`, `envG`, `envGD`, `c1_split`, `c1_ideal`, `c1_idealGame`, `c3_ideal`, `ideal_reveal`, `world_keeps`, `env_admitted`, `hidden_server_once`, `hidden_beacon_once`, `seed_at_install`, `idealStep_build_bound`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFacts`: `terminalAt`, `SessionMove`, `AllocMove`, `RealMove`, `AllocCached`, `realStep_move`, `acceptSelected_move`, `buildHidden_move`, `finishChallenge_move`, `run_keep`, `world_support`, `run_keepsGood`, `slotsLazy_cached`.
- Capability forms: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvStep.acceptSelectedEnv`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.buildCachedEnv` (the marked build the step runs; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvBuild.buildHiddenEnv` is its lazy-read form, equal at a cached slot by `buildCached_hit`), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFinish.finishChallengeEnv`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvReal.createPoolStep`.

## CE-11b unit U3: contracts used

No new assumption and no new contract. The theorems are equalities of programs, equalities in law and support facts; their premises are the admissibility predicates and the guards of the hidden commands, which `ideal_splits` discharges from `IdealInv`.

## CE-11b unit U3: open points

- The frames `frame_ideal` and `frame_idealD` and C4 `c4_ideal` are built only for the build class: `idealStep_build_bound` bounds the build command from every environment state by `allowanceG (baseAllowance caps) ceFirstTouch` (design-v2 section 6.3's allowance), after the review fix pass made the marked build read its slot's cached material (decisions above). The frame of the record world with `envGD` runs the ideal step at the drawn parameters (`paramEnv`), so the frame proofs also carry the bound through `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw.paramStep`. The remaining classes must also cover the allocator's no-room branch at the accept and finish classes (classes 3 and 5): with a full allocator a slot read draws a fresh `PqxdhServerAuthKeySchedule.Output` (108 bytes) instead of the 44-byte material, so the active selected accept costs at most 32 + 108 = 140 coins at class 3 against 96 + encapsCost + 44 (it fits, with encapsCost to spare), and owner k's finish one 108-byte draw at class 5 beside its preparation.
- Remaining cost bounds for the frames: the pool create (at most 64 coins plus the interpreted key generation beyond the pool), the selected accept (the ephemeral and one slot's material, no encapsulation and no Extract value) and owner k's challenge finish (preparation, one slot's material at class 5, `finishSplit_env_cost`, the known tail) under `KemBound`, the real commands (`realStep_bound`), and the startup (`qInit + 32` plus the draw of the parameters).
- `seed_at_install` on the support of the final split game itself, pointwise in the parameters `p` (design-v2 section 5.2). A support transfer along `c1_split` (`join_param`) gives it only for `p` in the support of `drawParams`. Route (from the review): for every `p`, CE-11a's `join` at `envG t p` (its `born` premise is `rfl`; `idealInv_start`, `idealInv_stable`, `ideal_splits`), `world_keeps` for `envG t p`, then a transfer of supports along `LawEq`, which is equality of every continued distribution, so supports agree. About 0.25.
- Unit U4 (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`) is not started; `ideal_keymatch` is already in U6's `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch`. U4 needs slot 0 at the marked coordinates (the Server restriction of `IdealSlots`, `slot = 0`, design-v2 section 7.1, and L1 of `ideal_trace`, `alloc.1 c = some 0`, section 7.2), which `IdealInv` does not give (`markedSession` says only that the marked coordinates hold a slot; decisions above). U4 gets it from its own invariant, from `before` together with the first allocation from an empty allocator, or from a conjunct added to `IdealInv`.
- Reviewed (findings fixed in "CE-11b unit U3: review fix pass"), and the fix pass confirmed by a second review ("CE-11b unit U3: confirming review"). Integration (PROTOCOL section 6): central is at `1f6ec6b8` (status paragraph); a read-only simulated merge of the topic with it conflicts only in `check-inventory.sh` and `reviewed-inventory.txt` (the root imports and the analysis merge without conflict), and the merge needs `inv recount` (1769 handwritten Lean files on central plus this unit's six, 1775) before `inv rehash`. The committed plan snapshot is refreshed through section 69 by the commit that records the confirming review; central's snapshot ends at section 64 and is unchanged since the merge base, so the merge keeps this branch's version.

## CE-11b unit U3: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `94a9cd4a` rechecked every module once without `-o` (exit 0, empty logs; 42, 37, 27, 33, 50 and 28 theorems, each with one exact guard allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), found that `ideal_splits`, `c1_split`, `c1_ideal`, `c1_idealGame`, `c3_ideal`, `ideal_reveal`, `hidden_server_once`, `hidden_beacon_once` and `seed_at_install` have no premise beyond their parameters, and reported one major finding, one minor finding and eleven nits, three of them records without action. The fixes are one Lean commit (`ddb249ba`), one merge of central (`a20610f4`) and the documentation commit that adds this section.

- Major (the frame of the build class): the review confirmed that `frame_idealD` and `frame_ideal` with `allowanceG (baseAllowance caps) ceFirstTouch` failed for the first `idealStep`. `Frame` bounds every step from every environment state, and the marked build began with a lazy material read, 44 coins at the build class from a legal state whose material cache lacks the slot, where the class has none. The review judged the decision implementation-level and preferred the cached read, which is taken (decisions above). `buildCachedEnv` reads the slot's material from the cache without a draw and does nothing on a miss. `IdealInv` gains `allocCached` (every allocated slot's material is cached, which implies the review's form for the marked coordinates), kept by every command: the allocator in front of the lazy service fills each slot it allocates and keeps the cache (`material_grows`, `slotLazy_cached`, `slotsLazy_cached`, conjuncts of `acceptSelected_move` and `finishChallenge_move`), the marked build leaves the cache unchanged (`buildCached_cache`), and the real commands keep the backend. `buildCached_hit` turns the build back into `buildHiddenEnv` at the hit, so `buildHidden_law`, `buildHidden_keeps` and `buildHidden_move` apply unchanged, and `idealStep_build_bound` proves the build class within the allowance from every state. The allocator's no-room branch at classes 3 and 5, which the remaining frames must cover, is in the open points.
- Minor (overclaim): the analysis and the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv` read as if `c3_ideal` and `ideal_reveal` were statements about the ideal game; both relate the record world to the draw followed by the final split game. Both texts are reworded (the docstring is the only Lean change for this finding), and "what is proved" says the same.
- Nit (I5 without "slot 0"): the decisions bullet gives the reason, and the U4 open point says where unit U4 gets slot 0.
- Nit (`env_admitted` read more than it states): the docstring and the analysis now say what the theorem states and where the per-step admission is proved (inside `ideal_splits`); no corollary was added.
- Nit (native `seed_at_install`): the review's route is in the open points.
- Nit (order of the inventory blocks) and nit (block comment style): at the merge of central `78fb56cd` the four earlier U3 blocks of `check-inventory.sh` moved after central's U6 blocks, so the six U3 blocks are contiguous before the primitive-fidelity anchor, and the first one's comment reads "CE-11b unit U3"; the count was recounted and the digests rehashed afterwards, `check-inventory.sh` last.
- Nit (no focused inventory before the merge commit `59adae12`): no change to that commit; this pass ran the focused inventory on the merge tree before committing `a20610f4`.
- Nit (`c1_shape`): the parenthetical that cited a scratch-only review probe now reads "as anticipated by the U2 semantic review".
- Nit (registry): the notes of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvStep` (it does not hold `idealStep`), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv` (the frames are open except the build class) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFacts` are refreshed.
- Nit (central moved past the review base): central `78fb56cd` (after `4761f182`; Codex's record-facts modules only) is merged as `a20610f4`, and every module and the root were checked against its published `LEAN_PATH`.
- Records (no action): the record of checks and the verified-clean summary.

Changed statements, before and after (every other statement is unchanged):

- `idealStep` (definition): the marked build was `buildHiddenEnv` (lazy read) and is `buildCachedEnv` (cached read). They agree at every state whose slot material is cached (`buildCached_hit`), which includes every state satisfying `IdealInv`, so `ideal_splits` keeps its form with no premise. The first version's `c1_split` and this one equate the same draw followed by the final split game with the record world, so the two environments' record worlds have the same law of joined outcomes; only the new one has the build-class bound.
- `IdealInv` (structure): one more field, `allocCached`. Every theorem that concludes `IdealInv` (`idealInv_start`, the preservation lemmas, `ideal_splits`, `env_admitted`) concludes more. The unconditional results downstream (`c1_split`, `c1_ideal`, `c1_idealGame`, `c3_ideal`, `ideal_reveal`, `env_admitted`, `hidden_server_once`, `hidden_beacon_once`, `seed_at_install`) keep their statements and have no premise.
- `idealInv_build_ready`: one more conclusion (the slot's material is cached).
- `idealInv_move` and `idealInv_build`: one more premise each, that the material cache is unchanged, needed for the new conjunct and discharged at every use (`RealMove.backend` for the real commands and the pool create, `bump` for the other accepts, `buildCached_cache` for the marked build). `idealInv_selected` and `idealInv_finish`: their move premise has one more conjunct (the cache keeps every allocated slot's material), supplied by `acceptSelected_move` and `finishChallenge_move`.
- `acceptSelected_move` and `finishChallenge_move`: one more conclusion each (the cache keeps every allocated slot's material if it did before).
- `idealStep_build`: its marked branch names `buildCachedEnv`, following the definition.
- The lemmas that take `IdealInv` only as a premise (`idealInv_stable`, `idealInv_finish_ready`, `idealInv_realStep`, `server_once`, `beacon_once`) read the stronger predicate; every outcome of the record world with `envGD` satisfies it (`env_admitted`).

New theorems (seven): `material_grows`, `slotLazy_cached`, `slotsLazy_cached` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFacts` (53 theorems) and `buildCached_hit`, `buildCached_cache`, `buildCached_bound`, `idealStep_build_bound` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv` (32 theorems); new definitions `AllocCached` and `buildCachedEnv`. `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvBuild` changed in its module docstring only. Each new theorem has an exact guard allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; the handwritten Lean count is 1761 after the merge. Checks: "CE-11b unit U3: checks".

## CE-11b unit U3: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass (`ddb249ba`, `a20610f4` and `bc0ca615`) at `bc0ca615` rechecked the six modules and the root import with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper, from the committed sources of `bc0ca615` and against its own prefix (a resolved mirror of the `BeaconcryptCore` part of the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `78fb56cd`), with empty logs. It compared the receipt with the branch head: the file hashes, and the theorem counts (42, 37, 27, 33, 53 and 32, 224 in all), equal to the exact guards, each allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; no theorem was deleted and seven were added. It checked every inventory row against the head (handwritten Lean count 1761), the position of the U3 blocks of `check-inventory.sh` (contiguous after central's U6 blocks, before the primitive-fidelity anchor) and the count after the merge `a20610f4` (central's 1755 plus six), and ran `check-inventory.sh` read-only on the clean worktree with host tools (it passed). A statement diff from `94a9cd4a` to `bc0ca615` changes exactly the statements listed under "Changed statements" in "CE-11b unit U3: review fix pass" and adds the seven new theorems; the downstream statements without premises are textually unchanged. A scratch Lean probe (not published) confirmed the major finding resolved: on the review's counterexample state (the marked slot's material not cached) the marked build is now a no-op, the review's proposed conjunct for the marked coordinates follows from `allocCached`, the build class of `Frame` holds literally for `envG` and for `envGD` (whose step is a map of the ideal step at the drawn parameters), and the build class's allowance is no coin, one BLAKE2b query and one administrative effect (the install). It found the minor overclaim and the nits resolved (the reason for I5 without slot 0 and U4's source for slot 0, the `env_admitted` docstring and analysis text, the route to the native `seed_at_install`, the order and comments of the inventory blocks), checked the analysis section, the module docstring, the guide sections, the working plan's section 66 (numbered uniquely) and the commit trailers, and found the branch head pushed. It approved with one nit: the status paragraph, the open points and the analysis still said that the fix pass had not been reviewed again.

This commit records the review in this section, the status paragraph and the open-points bullet, the Review paragraph of the U3 section of the analysis, the receipt (`review.status`, `review.confirming_review`, the plan record and `plan_snapshot`) and the committed plan snapshot, refreshed from the working plan through section 69 (sections 65 to 69; central's snapshot ended at section 64). No Lean file, inventory row or check changes; the integration's own checks run on the topic tip after this commit.

## CE-11b unit U6, second part: what is proved

This second part of unit U6 adds five modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 107 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains five imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostMark` | 4 | The decision step on the CE side. `tailCE_client_mark` and `tailCE_tail_mark` are the converses of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch.tailCE_client` and `tailCE_tail` (the first part's open point): under `tailCE`, item 2's marker client either leaves the mark unchanged or succeeds with a token, and the marker tail either leaves it unchanged or returns a session. `acceptAfter_mark`: the selected accept from the steps after encapsulation is active and sets the mark exactly when it returns a session. `activeCE_reply`: `activeCE` equals `activeReply`, the same computation with the decision reading whether the selected accept returned a session in place of the final mark (with `run_settled`), which is what item 2's `decideTag` reads. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSim` | 9 | Item 2's post-challenge stack as two layers. Generic: `optionLower` (an optional stateful layer followed by a stateful interpreter as one optional layer; `optionLower_run`, `optionLower_neverRead`), `compose_neverRead`, `fwdOption_neverRead`, and `sim_option`, a functional simulation through an upper stateful layer and a lower optional stateful layer for programs with only allowed requests and at most a room's number of costly requests: instantiated with the source creates as the costly class and the router's remaining reservation as the room (open point 2, not yet done), it will give the simulation and the absence of a router stop together. Item 2's stack: `upperLayer` (grouped client and skeleton), `serviceLayer` (actual encapsulator, post-challenge router, CCA evaluation, table layers `tableLayer`), `skelResume_upper`, `itemPost_active` (item 2's post-challenge phase on an active cut through `serviceLayer`), `serviceLayer_neverRead` and `itemPost_erased` (with the router transcripts erased, `eraseService`, from item 2's `post_neverRead`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSource` | 57 | The middle execution and its CE side. `Mid` (item 2's annotated source state, the ideal backend, the seed table), `sourceMid` (the selected raw accept, the marked build and owner k's challenge finish by the ideal registration's step on the middle state without copies, `idealMid` with `toX`, `fromX` and `rejoin`; every other request by item 2's annotated pool source `sourcePool` with the accept counter, `realMid`), `compiledMid`, the CE view `mergeX`. Real commands (`RealAt`, `postStep`, `step_real`) reduce to `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool.step_merge_pool` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource.programPool_native`. The hidden steps commute with CE-3's merge: the shared-state framework `ShComm` with `acceptExposed_comm` and `acceptAfter_comm` (through `shComm_tail`, `shComm_acceptLogged`, `tailCE_tail_comm`), `finishChallenge_merge` (owner k uncopied; `groupCE_comm`, `ownerImplCE_comm`), `buildHidden_comm` (every copied peer installed; `factored_override` from the generic builder's `genericBuild_mapPeers`), lifted to slot programs and the slot core (`acceptStep_selected_merge`, `buildStep_hidden_merge`, `finishStep_challenge_merge`, `selectedStep_merge`, `idealReg_merge`) and to `step_accept_selected`, `step_build_hidden`, `step_finish_hidden`. `step_mergeMid` (every command, under CE-3's `Inv`) and `compile_simulationMid` (the whole program, for any invariant that implies `Inv` and is kept by every middle step; the ghosts erased by `run_erase` with `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut.idealStep_unghost`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostItem` | 26 | Item 2's side, preparation. `service_coin`, `service_table` and `service_native`: every native skeleton step runs through the service layers as through the table layers alone, without stopping and with the router state kept (helpers `fwd_liftComp`, `tableLayer_lift`). `GhostFree` with `cleanSkel`: the skeleton never reads the analysis observers of its game state (`ghostFree_*` for its primitives and native steps, `skelImpl_ghostFree`), hence `upperLayer_neverRead` (`cleanUpper`). `itemPost_clean`: item 2's post-challenge phase on an active cut with both erasures, in the form the per-request simulation uses. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSteps` | 11 | Item 2's per-request steps against the middle source handler, first part. `encodeMid` (item 2's view of a middle state: encoded owners, the game state without observers with the mark and its table value `skelMarks`, the router state of the annotated pools without transcripts, the public table cache, the allocator and the table), `upperE` and `lowerE` (item 2's two layers with the erasures), `StepAt` (`sim_option`'s per-request obligation at this view). Helpers `lowerE_native`, `encodeMid_erased`, `encodeMid_clean`, `upperE_run`, `tableLayer_direct`. Steps: `step_coin`, `step_hash`, `step_reveal` (with `upperLayer_coin`, `upperLayer_hash`, `upperLayer_reveal`). |

## CE-11b unit U6, second part: decisions and deviations

- Architecture of the post-challenge simulation (the first part's open point (a)-(b)): two functional simulations into a common middle execution instead of one relation `PostRel`. The CE state does not determine item 2's state (item 2 keeps the source's stale kernels of the peers and owners that the compiled program copied, which CE-3's merge overwrites), and item 2's state does not determine the CE state (the CE side keeps the analysis observers and the LQ-F ghosts). The middle runs the compiled program (CE-3's `compileImpl`, shared by both sides) under a middle source handler on item 2's annotated source state; the CE side is its merge image (`compile_simulationMid`), item 2's side will be its projection (`sim_option` with the projection recorded in the open points), so item 2's simulation is per source request rather than per deployment command and CE-3's record lemmas are not repeated on item 2's side.
- The middle source handler answers the three hidden requests with the ideal registration's own steps (run on the middle state without copies and rejoined) and every other request with item 2's annotated pool source, so that each side meets definitions it already has: the CE side the ideal registration (whose real steps project to the native deployment handler, `postStep`), item 2's side its own source.
- Ghosts are erased, not related: the LQ-F snapshot and log on the CE side (`idealStep_unghost`), the router transcripts on item 2's side (`post_neverRead`), the analysis observers of the skeleton's game state (`skelImpl_ghostFree`, new). The decision reads none of them.
- The absence of a stop of the post-challenge router is to come from the simulation lemma (`sim_option`: a costly class with a room) instead of a separate total router; its instantiation for item 2 (the source creates against the router's remaining reservation) is part of open point 2.
- The decision step (the first part's open point (5) and finding 1 of its review): proved on the CE side as `activeCE_reply` (final mark = returned session); item 2's `decideTag` reads the returned session directly.
- `rejoin` keeps the indices of the old annotated state by position (`List.mapIdx`), so that erasing the annotations of a rejoined state gives the native state back without a length premise (`source_rejoin`).
- The invariant of the middle execution is a premise of `compile_simulationMid` (as in CE-3's `compile_simulation`), to be discharged by the next modules; it is not an assumption of any published claim.
- Modules: five new modules, all registered (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostMark`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSim`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSource`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostItem`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSteps`); `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` (the active branch) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` remain.
- Publication: five Lean commits (one per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block, `inv recount` before `inv rehash`) and two documentation commits on `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut` over central `dad157b1`; reviewed at `7d47499c`, with a review fix pass of one Lean commit (docstrings only) and one documentation commit; not integrated.

- Generic tools: `optionLower`, `optionLower_run`, `bound_or_zero`, `ShComm` with its closure lemmas, `mapPeersG` with `genericBuild_mapPeers`, `GhostFree` with its closure lemmas, `fwd_liftComp`.

## CE-11b unit U6, second part: contracts used

None. These modules use no codec contract and no new assumption; CE-3's merge invariant `Inv` and the invariant kept by the middle steps are premises of `step_mergeMid` and `compile_simulationMid`, discharged later in the unit.

## CE-11b unit U6, second part: open points

The active branch `ActiveBranch`, in module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` (registered), with the precise remaining steps:

1. The invariants of the middle execution. CE side: CE-3's `Good` on the local copies and the erased middle state, kept by `compiledMid` (real requests through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool.good_step_pool` and the erasure `programPool_native`; the selected accept, the marked build and owner k's challenge finish need their own preservation, the marked build through the footprint of the factored Server build, whose response the approved `Phase2ReadBack` must read back), giving `sound` and `preserved` of `compile_simulationMid`. Item 2 side: item 2's ownership of the annotated state (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource.PoolOwned`, the Server's identity slot), the owner positions (`PoolIndexed`; owner `j` holds pool entry `j`), the router's creation count equal to the KEM pool's size, the mark's value present in the two-slot table at its allocated slot, the allocator's well-formedness, and two facts of the ideal backend that `sourceMid`'s dispatch reads but `encodeMid` does not record: the selected accept is done (`t.accept < m.backend.2.2.accepts`, so that `sourceMid` answers every later raw accept with the real source, as item 2's skeleton answers it with its ordinary `acceptStep`), and it was active (`m.backend.2.2.active = true`, which `finishHiddenAt` requires for owner k's challenge finish, while item 2 recognizes that finish by the router's challenge handle). Both hold after `selectedStep (acceptAfter …)`, which adds one to the accept counter and, by `acceptAfter_mark`, sets the active flag, and they are to be shown kept by every later step (the counter only grows, and only the selected accept writes the flag).
2. Item 2's per-request steps: with the projection `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSteps.encodeMid` (the encoded owners, the game state without observers with the mark and its table value, the router state of the annotated pools without transcripts, the public table cache, the allocator and the table), prove `sim_option`'s `step` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSteps.StepAt`, done for the coins, BLAKE2b and the Server kernel reveal) and `keep` for `upper = eraseImpl cleanUpper upperLayer`, `lower = eraseOptionImpl eraseService serviceLayer`, `middle = sourceMid`, `costly = (sourceKind = .inl 1)` (creates), `allowed = (sourceKind ∉ {.inr 0, .inr 1, .inr 2, .inr 3})` (no identity, KEM, prekey or one-time field read: CE-9's transported caps `PqxdhDeploymentCompileCaps.transported` give these kinds 0, and `CutRelIndexed.residual` changes only the cap of `.inl 1`, so the continuation keeps them at 0), `room m = (p.pool t).length - m.joint.pool.2.keys.length` (`CutRelIndexed.residual`). Remaining requests: HMAC (the zero-key slice is a direct read, `tableLayer_direct`; a draft is in the track's notes), the kernel field read (`.inr 4`, a local read from the encoded owners; the other field reads cannot occur under `allowed`), create (the router's reservation against `createSourcePool`), bundle (the router's identity service against the annotated source), the real raw accept (the skeleton's `acceptStep` with the actual encapsulator against `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerAccept.source`), the real and the marked build (`buildService` with the mark's value against `buildHidden` after an allocator hit), the grouped finishes (`groupedFinish`, `markedPreparation` and `groupStep` against the annotated source's finish and, for owner k's challenge finish, against `finishChallenge`).
3. The selected accept's steps after encapsulation on item 2's side: the resumption (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueResume.resume` under `resumeImpl`) through the service layers against `selectedStep (acceptAfter …)` on the middle state at the cut (the static DH at the Server's identity slot through the router's identity pool, `tailCE_challenge` for the root request); with `selectedStep_merge` and `acceptAfter_comm` for the CE side.
4. Assembly: `activeCE_reply`, the CE side's middle form (`compile_simulationMid` after the selected accept), item 2's middle form (`itemPost_clean`, step 3, `sim_option`), and the decision (`markedDecision` on the merge image against `decideTag` on item 2's result: the produced frames are the copies' frames, the reply is the selected accept's reply); then `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` with `ideal_compile_of_active`.

## CE-11b unit U6, second part: review fix pass

An independent review of `7d47499c` in two parts (semantic and hygiene, each with its own Lean rechecks of the five modules and the root; the hygiene part also ran its own focused `check-inventory` on the clean tree, exit 0) found no error in the Lean statements or proofs; both parts approved with nits. It raised eleven findings (three minor, eight nits; some reported by both parts), all about documentation, records or docstrings. This fix pass resolves each of them with one Lean commit (`60721af7`, docstrings only) and one documentation commit. No statement, proof or guard changed, no theorem was added or removed, and the 107 theorems keep their exact guards.

- Minor (semantic) and nit (hygiene): the central moves. The guide said that central `78fb56cd` contains CE-11b unit U3, which it does not (it is Codex's structural origin publication), and central has since moved on to `1f6ec6b8` (Codex's global binding publication) and `760f056e` (U3 integrated). Fixed in the status paragraph and the integration open point, which now names the three moves and the hot spots central has touched since `dad157b1`, and gives the expected handwritten Lean count after the merge (1780) and the steps of the merge.
- Minor (semantic): conditional results read as unconditional. The status paragraph now says that the CE side's step commutation is complete under CE-3's merge invariant and that the whole-program form `compile_simulationMid` still needs the middle invariant, and that `sim_option` excludes a stop of the post-challenge router only once it is instantiated for item 2; the same qualifiers are in the `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSim` row of "what is proved", the decisions bullet on the router stop, the analysis paragraphs "The ideal side" and "Item 2's side", and the receipt's `scope`.
- Minor (hygiene) and nit (semantic): the description of the checks. The checks ran before each commit on a tree whose module source equals the committed one, not on the Lean commit with a clean tree; the checks paragraph now says so, and this fix pass rechecked all five modules and the root on its Lean commit with a clean tree (below).
- Nits (semantic and hygiene): module lists without `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSteps`. Design version 2, section 25, and the receipt's deviation entry now list all five modules; the working plan's section 67 points from its first bullet to its addendum.

CE-9's transported caps give the identity, KEM, prekey and one-time field reads (`.inr 0` to `.inr 3`) a cap of 0, and `CutRelIndexed.residual` changes only `.inl 1`, so `allowed` now excludes all four and the only field read left is the kernel read (`.inr 4`), local on the encoded owners.
