## CE-11b unit U3 remainder: what is proved

The remainder of unit U3 adds two modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 37 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root gains two imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvCost` | 29 | Effect bounds of every command of the ideal step from every environment state, under `KemBound setup caps`. Slot reads against the allocator in front of the lazy material service (`slotLazy_step`, `slotRun_total`): nothing at a cached slot, the 44-byte material at a fresh slot with room, a fresh 108-byte `PqxdhServerAuthKeySchedule.Output` (`output_bound`) when the allocator is full. The selected accept (`acceptSelected_bound`): at most `140 + encapsCost` coin effects and nothing else (32 coins before encapsulation, `beforeLogged_cost`; nothing after it, `suffix_cost`; one slot read for the root, `tailCE_cost`; the logged accept otherwise, `acceptInactive_cost`). The pool create (`createPoolStep_bound`) within the real create's allowance. Owner k's challenge finish (`finishChallenge_bound`): at most `decapsCost + 108` coin effects, one BLAKE2b effect and one administrative effect. The challenger's draws (`drawParams_uniform`): at most `(count + 1) * keygenCost + encapsCost` coins. `idealStep_bound`: every command stays within `allowanceG (baseAllowance caps) ceFirstTouch` of its class. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealFrame` | 8 | C2a: `frame_ideal` (`envG`, startup bound `qInit + 32`) and `frame_idealD` (`envGD`, startup bound `qInit + 32 + (count + 1) * keygenCost + encapsCost`, plan section 48, A2), both with the unchanged first-touch allowance. C4: `c4_ideal` and `c4_ideal_later`, the instances of `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.c4_closed` and `c4_later_closed` at `envGD`. F4 on the final split game's own support for every parameter (`seed_at_install_split`), through CE-11a's `join` at `envG t p` (`envG_admitted`) and the support transfer along `LawEq` (`lawEq_support_subset`, `lawEq_support`). |

## CE-11b unit U3 remainder: decisions and deviations

- The allocator's no-room branch at classes 3 and 5 (a 108-byte draw, open point of "CE-11b unit U3: open points") is bounded inside the unchanged allowance: the selected accept's 32 + 108 = 140 coins lie within the real accept's `96 + encapsCost` plus the 44 first-touch coins, and the finish's `decapsCost + 108` coins lie within class 5's allowance with its first touch (`idealStep_bound`). The allowance of plan sections 31, 33 and 35 and `ceFirstTouch` are unchanged; showing the branch unreachable under `IdealInv` was not needed.
- F4 on the final split game's own support is proved for every parameter `p` by the route of the U3 review (CE-11a's `join` at `envG t p`, whose `born` premise is `rfl`, and a support transfer along `LawEq`), not only for `p` in the support of `drawParams`.

## CE-11b unit U4: what is proved

The first part of unit U4 adds four modules with 96 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root gains four imports). The review fix pass adds the module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` (6 theorems) and three theorems to `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance` (now 42), so the first part has five modules with 105 guarded theorems (the root gains a fifth import); the table gives the current counts. Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace` | 16 | The public transcript functions of design-v2 section 7.1 (`acceptReplies`, `sessionAt`, `peerOf`, `FinishBefore`, `BeaconLinked`, `Anomaly`, `IdealSlots`) with their transcript lemmas; `KeyFacts`, the two transcript conjuncts of U6's `KeyTrace`, on every outcome of the final split game (`split_keyFacts`) and of the record world with `envGD` (`world_keyFacts`); `keyFacts_marked`, the key match of `ideal_keymatch` from the key facts alone (under `Phase1ReadBack`); the slot maps `originSlots` and `ghostOrigins`; `ctGuess`, its restriction `ctGuessPk` to guesses of the public key alone and `ctGuessPk_le` (both suprema over all guess functions, so information-theoretic; not the form CE-12a's IND-CCA reduction consumes, see `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins` | 31 | The provenance-annotated record world with the ledger, generic in the environment: the annotated joint step, `run_keeps_origins` and `world_keeps_origins` (they replace, on the annotated record world, design-v2's `ledger_keeps` and `run_keeps_origins` by one statement; design-v2 states `ledger_keeps` on the split game `gameWith`, and no `gameWith`-level `ledger_keeps` is proved: an invariant between the transcript, the environment state, the World and the annotation kept by every registration command holds on every outcome), `annotatedRun` with `annotatedRun_erase`. The annotation invariant `AnnInv` of the World (`Present`: hidden origins are at handles the World holds; `Covered`: every used marker has an origin; `Marked`): every World effect keeps it, and every effect that is not a hidden install keeps the annotation (`aEffects_keeps`, through `installServer_annInv` and `installBeacon_annInv`); it reads the World through its fixed view (`annInv_view`); program forms `aRun_rel`, `aRun_free`, `aRun_owner`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSessions` | 10 | The Server's session counter, pending tokens and replies: a real raw accept raises the counter by one and publishes at most one token at the old counter (`realStep_accept`); a real build consumes its session's token and returns a response only from one (`realStep_build`); the other real commands and the pool create keep both (`realStep_quiet`, `createPool_game`); under `tailCE` the selected accept either returns nothing and keeps the tokens or returns the old counter and publishes one token there (`tailCE_client_pending`, `tailCE_tail_pending`, `acceptActive_pending`, `acceptExposed_pending`, `acceptSelected_pending`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance` | 42 | The provenance invariant `Prov t` (the annotation invariant; every Server origin at the Server install ghost's key ID and every Beacon origin at owner `k`; the accept counter counts the raw accepts; up to the selected ordinal the session counter is the accept counter and every token and successful build is below it; the mark is the selected ordinal's session at coordinates holding slot 0, and while its token is pending the selected accept returned it and it has no successful build; every successful build of the marked session published the ghost's peer; the ghost's key ID is the target's peer). Preservation by every command of the ideal step (`ideal_rStep`, from `prov_realQuiet`, `prov_realBuild`, `prov_acceptOther`, `prov_selected`, `prov_markedBuild`, `prov_challenge`), startup (`prov_initial`), outcomes (`world_provG` for `envG`, `world_prov` for `envGD`). The annotation is the ghost slot map (`origins_eq_ghost`, `origins_ghost`). L1 and L3 (`trace_peer`, `trace_designated`) on the record world with `envGD` (`ideal_trace_world`) and on the final split game for every parameter (`ideal_trace`); both take `Phase1ReadBack` as a premise of the whole statement although only L3 uses it, and L1 alone holds without it (`ideal_trace_world_peer`, `ideal_trace_peer`, review fix pass). `ideal_slots_server`: the Server restriction (slot 0, the target's peer), the Beacon restriction (owner `k`) and the Server converse of `IdealSlots`, on every outcome of the annotated record world with `envGD`, stated under `Phase1ReadBack`, which only the Server converse uses; the two restrictions hold without it, for every Server key ID (`ideal_slots_restrictions`, review fix pass). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` | 6 | Review fix pass. The ciphertext guess of a guesser program, the form that CE-12a's IND-CCA reduction can consume (plan section 49, Q2): `ctGuessBy setup guess`, the probability that `guess`, an explicit program over the KEM's ambient oracles run on the public key of a fresh key pair before the encapsulation to it, outputs the encapsulation's ciphertext. `ctGuessBy_le` (at most `ctGuessPk`, through a best guess per public key, `encapsHit_max`), `ctGuessBy_le_ctGuess`, `ctGuessPk_eq_iSup` (`ctGuessPk` is the supremum of `ctGuessBy` over all guessers, including inefficient ones), `ctGuessBy_probComp` (the form for a `ProbComp` guesser) and `guess_preChallenge` (lifted by `ambientLift` into the IND-CCA oracle spec, the guesser runs unchanged under the pre-challenge decapsulation oracle). |

In plain terms: on every run of the record world with the ideal environment of a target, the World-level provenance annotation (`PqxdhRecordLiftOrigins`: which handle holds which hidden slot; design-v2's planned `run_origins`, unit U7, relates it to the record lift's own handle-to-slot map) is exactly the slot map of the environment's install ghosts; the Server peer that holds a hidden slot holds slot 0 and is the peer that the selected session built in the public transcript; only owner `k` can hold a hidden Beacon slot; and, when the codec reads Phase 1 back, every Server peer that the public designation assigns to the target holds slot 0. Whether owner `k` holds a slot when it designates the target (the Beacon converse) is not proved yet.

## CE-11b unit U4: decisions and deviations

- One invariant of the provenance-annotated record world instead of design-v2 section 7.2's route (the trace invariant on the final split game, then a transfer to the record world): `run_keeps_origins` replaces (on the annotated record world) design-v2's `ledger_keeps` and `run_keeps_origins` by one generic statement, and `Prov` is proved on the record world directly; design-v2 states `ledger_keeps` as an invariant of the split game `gameWith`, and no `gameWith`-level `ledger_keeps` is proved. The split-game form `ideal_trace` is derived from it through CE-11a's `join` at `envG t p` and a support transfer, for every parameter. This is an implementation-level choice; no assumption is added.
- Slot 0 (the U3 open point) comes from U4's own invariant: `Prov.marked` says that the mark's coordinates hold slot 0, established at the selected accept from `IdealInv.before` (empty allocator) and the selected accept's allocator move by at most one slot (`acceptSelected_move`).
- The annotation is tracked through a World-level invariant (`AnnInv`) and two facts about the hidden commands, not through every program trace: commands without a hidden install keep the annotation (`aRun_free`), the marked build changes it only at the key ID of the Server install ghost (`buildCached_origins`) and owner k's challenge finish only at owner `k` (`finishChallenge_owner` with `aRun_owner`). With `IdealInv`'s used-marker conjuncts this gives `origins_eq_ghost`.
- U6's key match is reused through its two transcript conjuncts (`KeyFacts`), carried along `ideal_to_split` and `c1_split` to the final split game and the record world; `keyFacts_marked` repeats the short argument of `ideal_keymatch` because that theorem is stated on the ideal game's outcomes.
- `ideal_slots` is delivered in part (`ideal_slots_server`: three of its four conjuncts, without the anomaly alternative, which only the Beacon converse needs). It is stated under `Phase1ReadBack`, the approved codec contract under which design-v2 section 7.2 states L3, which only its Server converse uses; design-v2's statement sketch `ideal_slots_stmt` listed no premise. The two restrictions hold without it (`ideal_slots_restrictions`, review fix pass).
- Four registered modules instead of one (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSessions`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`); the session facts are a new module of their own. The review fix pass adds `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly`, the module registered for `anomaly_le` (design-v2 section 28).

## CE-11b unit U4: names for consumers

- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace`: `sessionAt`, `peerOf`, `FinishBefore`, `BeaconLinked`, `Anomaly`, `IdealSlots`, `originSlots`, `ghostOrigins`, `ctGuess`, `ctGuessPk`, `ctGuessPk_le`, `KeyFacts`, `world_keyFacts`, `keyFacts_marked`. `ctGuess` and `ctGuessPk` are information-theoretic suprema; CE-12a's IND-CCA reduction consumes `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly.ctGuessBy` at a constructed guesser instead.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins`: `annotatedRun`, `annotatedRun_erase`, `run_keeps_origins`, `world_keeps_origins`, `AnnInv`, `aEffects_keeps`, `aRun_free`, `aRun_owner`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`: `Prov`, `world_prov`, `world_provG`, `origins_ghost`, `ideal_trace_world`, `ideal_trace`, `ideal_slots_server`, `annotated_cover`, and without `Phase1ReadBack` `ideal_trace_world_peer`, `ideal_trace_peer`, `ideal_slots_restrictions`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly`: `ctGuessBy`, `ctGuessBy_le`, `ctGuessBy_le_ctGuess`, `ctGuessPk_eq_iSup`, `ctGuessBy_probComp`, `ambientLift`, `guess_preChallenge`.

## CE-11b unit U4: contracts used

No new assumption and no new contract. `keyFacts_marked` (the key match, as in U6's `ideal_keymatch`), `trace_designated` (L3), `ideal_trace_world`, `ideal_trace` and `ideal_slots_server` take the approved codec contract `PqxdhServerAuthNegativeControl.Phase1ReadBack`, the last three as a premise of the whole statement although only L3 and the Server converse use it; `ideal_trace_world_peer`, `ideal_trace_peer`, `ideal_slots_restrictions` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` take no contract. Everything else is a support fact of the record world, an invariant of its outcomes or a definition.

## CE-11b unit U4: open points

- The Beacon converse of C5 (design-v2 section 7.2, L4): `BeaconLinked` implies that owner `k` holds a slot or `FinishBefore`, under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`; with it, the full `ideal_slots` (`IdealSlots ∨ Anomaly` on every outcome of the annotated record world with `envGD`). Route: the mark from `keyFacts_marked` with the owner handle; new conjuncts of the invariant (a mark implies `active`; the Beacon ghost's coordinates hold a slot; the marked session's pending token carries the challenge ciphertext; every successful build of the marked session wrote Phase 2 fields with that ciphertext); native facts needed: the selected accept's token carries the suffix's challenge ciphertext, the marked build's response is the Phase 2 writer output of `responseFields candidate encrypted` with `candidate.kem_ciphertext = pending.kem_ciphertext` (as `PqxdhServerAuthNegativeControl.build_published` and `prepare_commit_fields` give for the native build, here for the split build of `buildCachedEnv`), a successful challenge finish whose read ciphertext converts to `p.challenge` takes the hidden branch and sets the Beacon ghost (`preparationCE_challenge`, `hiddenBackend`), and a real finish of an owner not in `initSent` fails. About 1-2 agent-days.
- `ideal_slots_prefix` (the prefix-closed form: the designation facts of a prefix persist in the final transcript and the hidden origins persist), after the Beacon converse; about 0.25-0.5.
- L2 (the Beacon ghost implies that owner `k` is established in the transcript) needs the agreement of the public phases with the native ones for the ideal step (CE-5's `Agree`); `IdealSlots` does not use it. About 0.5-1 if a consumer needs it.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` (now holding `ctGuessBy` and its facts): `anomaly_le` (`Pr[Anomaly]` on the record world with `envGD` at most `ctGuess setup`, premises `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`; design-v2 section 7.2, item 6: `Anomaly` is public, so by `c1_ideal` it suffices on `CE.idealGame g`, where the challenge ciphertext is first read at the selected accept and owner k's earlier finish fixed its ciphertext before that read) and the form that CE-12a's IND-CCA reduction consumes (plan section 49, Q2), `anomaly_le_pk`: `Pr[Anomaly]` on the record world with `envGD` at most `ctGuessBy setup (anomalyGuesser …)`, same premises, at an explicit guesser program. Given the public key as the chosen pool entry's public key, `anomalyGuesser` draws the other pool entries, runs the record world with the adversary and an environment that holds neither the chosen secret key nor the challenge ciphertext, stops at the selected raw accept or at owner `k`'s first finish made in phase `InitSent`, whichever comes first, and returns the ciphertext read from that finish's Phase 2 input (a fixed ciphertext otherwise). The phase is public (`summarize`) and also visible in the simulated native state. That finish is the only one of owner `k` that can read the secret key: in `beacon.rs` `finish_registration`, a finish outside `InitSent` is rejected before the secret key is read and leaves the Beacon unchanged (lines 403-406), and the first finish in `InitSent` either establishes the Beacon or aborts it (`abort_registration` on every failure, lines 487-490), so no later finish of owner `k` is made in `InitSent`; on `Anomaly` that finish is the designating one (`finishOf`, the first successful finish). (Corrected after the confirming review: this text said owner `k`'s first finish, with the reason that a failed finish aborts the Beacon, which does not hold for a finish outside `InitSent`.) CE-12a packages it as a `PqxdhKemIndCca.OneKeyAdversary` whose pre-challenge phase runs it (`ambientLift`, `guess_preChallenge`) and makes one decapsulation query of the predicted ciphertext. `ctGuessPk` is not that form: it is a supremum over all guess functions (`ctGuessPk_eq_iSup`), which no approved assumption bounds (plan section 40), so a bound by `ctGuessPk` alone would bring back option (a) of Q2. Steps: (i) a draw swap that moves the challenge encapsulation behind the run (`drawParams` draws `chosen` first, and the encapsulation is not read before the selected accept); (ii) a cut of the record world's run at the first of the selected raw accept and owner `k`'s first finish in `InitSent`, like U5's cut at the selected accept; (iii) the environment without the chosen secret key and the challenge equals `envGD` up to the cut (there is no run-phase reveal command, `p.challenge` is first read at the selected accept and the chosen secret key only by owner `k`'s finish in `InitSent`); (iv) on `Anomaly` the finish's ciphertext is `p.challenge` (the Beacon converse's prerequisite: the selected session's build response carries `p.challenge`, read back under `Phase2ReadBack`); `anomaly_le` then follows by `ctGuessBy_le_ctGuess`. About 3.5-6 (re-estimated by the review fix pass from 2-3.5: the program-level factorization of the run prefix through the public key alone is more work than the semantic supremum route). Once this track is integrated, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` is a published module and is not edited again, so `anomaly_le` and `anomaly_le_pk` go into a new module.

## CE-11b unit U4: re-estimate

The U3 remainder took about 0.5-0.75 agent-days (planned 0.75-1.75 plus 0.25 for F4). This first part of U4 took about 1-1.5 (U4 was planned at 3.5-6.5 without `ideal_keymatch`). Remaining for U4: the Beacon converse and the full `ideal_slots` 1-2, `ideal_slots_prefix` 0.25-0.5, `anomaly_le` with its public-key-only form 2-3.5, L2 0.5-1 only if needed; together about 3.25-6 (without L2), so U4 now totals about 4.25-7.5: the Beacon converse needs native facts about the marked build's Phase 2 serialization that no published module provides for the split build. The review fix pass (about 0.25) re-estimates the anomaly item at 3.5-6 (open points), so the remaining U4 work is about 4.75-8.5 without L2 and U4 totals about 6-10.25.

The root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` of each Lean commit was checked on the committed sources just before the commit (source hashes in the receipt), and on each merge tree (the second against the published `3d212990` LEAN_PATH), with empty logs. The focused `make check-inventory` under the locked Nix shell passed on the merge tree of `38685f46`, at `90522530` with a clean tree, on the merge tree of `9e0002b4` and with this documentation in the tree; `git diff --check` is clean. Receipt: [proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11b-u4.json](proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11b-u4.json); the plain-English explanation is the section "Common deployment experiment, CE-11b unit U3 remainder and unit U4 (first part): frames and provenance of the ideal environment" of [the analysis](../formal-verification-analysis.md).

## CE-11b unit U4: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `9c477e45` recompiled the six modules and the root import (exit 0, empty logs), counted 133 theorems with 133 exact guards allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, found no `sorry`, `admit` or axiom, and found no wrong Lean statement. It reported one major finding and eight nits, three of them records without action. The fixes are one Lean commit (`5aff9fa8`) and the documentation commit that adds this section.

- Major (the public-key-only anomaly bound through `ctGuessPk` cannot be used by CE-12a's IND-CCA reduction, plan section 49, Q2, option (b)): `ctGuessPk` is a supremum over all functions of the public key, including inefficient ones, so from `Pr[Anomaly] ≤ ctGuessPk` CE-12a would only get a supremum of IND-CCA advantages of unconstructed guessers, which no assumption bounds; the predict-then-compare reduction needs the predictor as a program built from the adversary. `ctGuessPk` and `ctGuessPk_le` are kept. The new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` adds `ctGuessBy setup guess` for a guesser program `guess : MlKem768PublicKey → OracleComp (unifSpec + baseSpec) MlKem768Ciphertext` (the KEM's ambient oracles, which are the oracles of the pre-challenge phase of a `PqxdhKemIndCca.OneKeyAdversary` without decapsulation; more general than the review's `ProbComp` example, which `ctGuessBy_probComp` covers), with `ctGuessBy_le` (`ctGuessBy setup guess ≤ ctGuessPk setup` for every guesser, the information-theoretic corollary), `ctGuessBy_le_ctGuess`, `ctGuessPk_eq_iSup` (`ctGuessPk` is the supremum of `ctGuessBy` over all guessers) and `guess_preChallenge` (lifted by `ambientLift`, the guesser runs unchanged in the IND-CCA pre-challenge phase). The planned public-key-only form is now `anomaly_le_pk` at an explicit guesser that runs the game prefix up to owner `k`'s first finish made in `InitSent` (stopping point made precise by the confirming review) from the public key alone ("CE-11b unit U4: open points"). The docstrings of `ctGuessPk` and of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace`, this guide's table, names and open points, the working plan (section 73 and its new section 75), design version 2 (a pointer in section 26 and the new section 28), the receipt's mapping and the analysis now say that `ctGuessPk` is the information-theoretic form, not the one the reduction consumes. The anomaly item is re-estimated at 3.5-6 agent-days (from 2-3.5), so the remaining U4 work is about 4.75-8.5 without L2 ("CE-11b unit U4: re-estimate").
- Nit (the annotation "that the record lift reads"): the analysis, this guide's plain-terms paragraph and the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance` now say that the results are about the World-level provenance annotation (`PqxdhRecordLiftOrigins`) and that the planned `run_origins` (design-v2 section 9.1, unit U7), not yet proved, relates it to the record lift's handle-to-slot map.
- Nit (`run_keeps_origins` "covers" `ledger_keeps`): the decisions bullet and the table row of this guide, the module docstring and the docstring of `run_keeps_origins` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins`, the receipt, the plan and design version 2 now say that it replaces them on the annotated record world and that no `gameWith`-level `ledger_keeps` exists.
- Nit (`Phase1ReadBack` described per conjunct): both options are taken. The texts say that `ideal_trace_world`, `ideal_trace` and `ideal_slots_server` take `Phase1ReadBack` as a premise of the whole statement while only L3 and the Server converse use it (this guide, the analysis, the three theorems' docstrings and the module docstring), and three premise-free corollaries are added to `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`: `ideal_trace_world_peer` (L1 on the record world, for every Server key ID), `ideal_trace_peer` (L1 on the final split game, for every parameter) and `ideal_slots_restrictions` (the two restrictions of `IdealSlots` on the annotated record world, for every Server key ID).
- Nit (the root "checked after each Lean commit"): "CE-11b unit U4: checks" now says that the root of each Lean commit was checked on the committed sources just before the commit, with the source hashes in the receipt. The checks of this fix pass ran after the Lean commit, on its clean tree (below).
- Nit (registry notes): the notes of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins`, and those of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly`, are refreshed to the modules' content.
- Records (no action): the semantic lens's record of checks and confirmations, the hygiene lens's summary, and central's move to `3915c3f0` (a trial merge conflicts only in the handwritten Lean count and the digest rows of the root and `check-inventory.sh`; the integration merges central, then recounts and rehashes). Central has since moved on to `39fd1c50` (no CE-11b file); a trial merge of it with `5aff9fa8` (`git merge-tree`, old mode) shows conflicts in the same three places only (the hunk of the `check-inventory.sh` digest row also carries central's adjacent `trusted-boundary.md` row, as the confirming review recorded).

Changed statements, before and after: none. No existing theorem or definition changed its statement or its proof; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins` changed in docstrings only, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance` in docstrings and by three new theorems. New theorems (nine), each with an exact guard allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`: `encapsHit_max`, `ctGuessBy_le`, `ctGuessBy_le_ctGuess`, `ctGuessPk_eq_iSup`, `ctGuessBy_probComp` and `guess_preChallenge` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` (6 theorems; definitions `ctGuessBy`, `encapsHit` and `ambientLift`), and `ideal_trace_world_peer`, `ideal_trace_peer` and `ideal_slots_restrictions` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance` (42 theorems). Each corollary is at least as strong as the part of `ideal_trace_world`, `ideal_trace` or `ideal_slots_server` it restates: the same conclusion with one premise fewer, and `ideal_trace_world_peer` and `ideal_slots_restrictions` hold for every Server key ID instead of `setup.serverKeyId`. The track now has seven modules with 142 guarded theorems; the handwritten Lean count is 1828.

Checks of the fix pass, at the Lean commit `5aff9fa8` with a clean tree: the changed modules `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`, the new `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` and the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean`, in import order, each with one `coord.sh lean` check (Lean 4.34, `-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) with `-o` into the fresh prefix `ce11b/u4/fix/olean` (a resolved mirror of `ce11b/u4/olean` without the three changed modules' oleans, in front of the published `3d212990` LEAN_PATH; the oleans of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvCost`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealFrame` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSessions`, whose sources and imports did not change, are reused), each exit 0 with an empty log. `coord.sh inv recount` (1827 to 1828) and `coord.sh inv rehash` (the three changed modules, the new one, the root and `check-inventory.sh` last) ran before the commit. The focused `make check-inventory` under the locked Nix shell passed at `5aff9fa8` with a clean tree and with this documentation in the tree; `git diff --check` is clean. Receipt: the key `fix_pass` of [proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11b-u4.json](proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11b-u4.json).

## CE-11b unit U4: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass (`5aff9fa8` and `c02d3caf`) at `c02d3caf` found all nine findings of the review of `9c477e45` resolved. It read the Lean diff (docstrings only in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins`; docstrings and the three new corollaries in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`, exact restatements without `Phase1ReadBack` with the Server key ID generalized; the new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly`) and recompiled the four changed or new modules and the root import independently from the committed sources of `c02d3caf` with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper, in import order and with `-o` into its own prefix (a resolved mirror of this track's prefix without those artifacts, in front of the published `3d212990` LEAN_PATH): exit 0, empty logs, artifacts byte-identical to the fix pass's final checks. It counted 142 theorems with 142 exact guards in the track's seven modules, each allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, found no forbidden token and no changed or removed existing theorem, matched every inventory digest under `beaconcrypt-core` (handwritten Lean count 1828), ran `check-inventory.sh` on the clean worktree (it passed), and checked the receipt (74 hash and content checks: file hashes, the modules' theorem lists, definitions and axiom profiles, the final checks with their logs, artifacts and LEAN_PATH files, the focused inventory logs, the plan and design copies and the scratch development files; the root checks ran on the committed root of each commit, seconds before it). A scratch Lean probe (not published) packaged a guesser and one decapsulation query of its prediction as a `PqxdhKemIndCca.OneKeyAdversary` and showed that the three corollaries are exactly (`rfl`) the L1 and restriction parts of `ideal_trace_world`, `ideal_trace` and `ideal_slots_server`; its negative control (a different Server key ID) fails. The commit footers are exact, the head is pushed, and a trial merge with central `39fd1c50` conflicts only in the handwritten Lean count, the root's digest row and the digest row of `check-inventory.sh`, whose hunk also carries central's adjacent `trusted-boundary.md` row.

It approved with one minor documentation finding and two nits, all addressed by the documentation commit that adds this section:

- Minor (the stopping point of the planned `anomalyGuesser`): the open points, the working plan's section 75 and design version 2's section 28 said that the guesser stops at owner `k`'s first finish, "the only finish of owner `k` that reads the secret key: a failed finish aborts the Beacon". That reason is false for a finish outside `InitSent`: `finish_registration` rejects it before reading the secret key and without aborting, and `summarize` leaves the phase unchanged. The stopping point is now owner `k`'s first finish made in `InitSent` (a public phase, also visible in the simulated native state); that finish either establishes or aborts the Beacon, so it is the only finish of owner `k` that reads the secret key, and on `Anomaly` it is the designating finish (`finishOf`). Corrected in "CE-11b unit U4: open points" (the guesser and steps (ii) and (iii)), the major bullet of "CE-11b unit U4: review fix pass", plan section 75, design version 2 section 28 and the analysis's wording. The module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` names "owner `k`'s first finish" without the false reason and refers to the open points; it is left unchanged, so that the reviewed Lean files stay byte-identical (the review marked that change optional).
- Nit (`keyFacts_marked` missing from "contracts used"): it also takes `Phase1ReadBack`; it is added there, to its table row and to the receipt's premises.
- Nit (scratch records): plan section 75 listed the definition `ctGuessBy` among the six theorems and omitted `encapsHit_max`, and its record of the trial merge omitted the `trusted-boundary.md` row; both are corrected, the integration notes record the row, and the records bullet of "CE-11b unit U4: review fix pass" now names it.
- Record (no action): the nine findings of the review of `9c477e45` are resolved at `c02d3caf`, and the checks pass.

This commit changes no Lean file, inventory row or check. It records the review in this section, the status paragraph, the analysis's Review paragraph, the receipt (`review.status`, `review.confirming_review`, the premises, the plan, design and `plan_snapshot` records) and the committed plan snapshot, refreshed from the working plan through its new section 77 (sections 72 to 77 and the integration bullet of section 71; central's snapshot ended at section 71). The integration's own checks run on the merged topic tip. Once this track is integrated, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` is a published module and is not edited again, so `anomaly_le` and `anomaly_le_pk` go into a new module.

## CE-11b unit U6, third part: what is proved

This third part of unit U6 adds four modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 94 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains four imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`. Every step below is an instance of `sim_option`'s per-request obligation: a request of the middle source handler `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSource.sourceMid`, projected to item 2's view, is answered by item 2's upper layers (grouped client and skeleton) and erased service layers (actual encapsulator, post-challenge router, CCA evaluation, table layers) without stopping, with item 2's view of the middle's poststate.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostRequests` | 22 | `step_hmac` (the skeleton's HMAC step, a direct read on the zero-key 192-byte slice, against the source's native HMAC through the ROM family); `step_read` (a field read without a private-service call, `LocalField`: prekey, one-time prekey and the established kernel, with `localValue_encode`); the post-challenge router away from the selected challenge decapsulation (`challengeDecaps`; `postDecapsulation_fresh`, `kemStep_fresh`, `jointStep_fresh` and `jointProgram_fresh` are item 2's `PqxdhDynamicKemPost.decapsulation_source`, `step_pre`, `PqxdhDynamicKemJointRouter.post_step` and `post_program` without the agreement premise, which only that decapsulation uses; `fresh_of_noDecaps`); private-service programs through item 2's service layers (`service_pool`, `service_poolProgram`, and `lowerE_pool`: with creations within the challenger's pool, no KEM reveal and no challenge decapsulation, through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource.stopped_program` the erased service layers act as the reserved services); `step_create` (item 2's constructor client reads the challenger's pool at the router's creation count, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource.client_reserved`, as the middle's pool create does) and `step_bundle` (the bundle program runs as under the actual services and is the annotated bundle trace by item 2's `PqxdhRegistrationJointBundleCompiler.bundle_source`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostServer` | 24 | `SplitT`: a native program and a skeleton program at the factored value type split when the skeleton run from the game state without observers through item 2's table layers from the native table cache is the native run with observers and family history dropped, the marks and slot state kept (`splitT_pure`, `splitT_bind`, `splitT_set`, `splitT_modify`, `splitT_consume`, `splitT_loggedRoot` with `tableRead_run`, `splitT_client`, `splitT_tail`); programs of the exposed encapsulation interface through the service layers (`fwdOption_liftComp`, `service_enc`, `service_encProgram`, `lowerE_enc`); `step_accept` (a raw accept other than the selected one: the exposed prefix runs as item 2's annotated source prefix, `prefix_chain`, the tail splits, together the middle's annotated source accept, `sourceMid_accept`, `source_accept_run`, `upperLayer_accept`); `step_build` (at a session that the marks do not designate the response tail is the source's own, `buildService_real`, `build_real`; `skelMarks_real`, `sourceMid_build`, `upperLayer_build`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostHidden` | 19 | The table counters are never read (`table_neverRead`, `run_neverRead`, `slot_neverRead`, `tableLayer_run`, `tableLayer_neverRead`, `lower_neverRead`, `optionLower_neverRead_both`); item 2's view without them (`eraseServiceC`, `eraseServiceC_idem`, `eraseServiceC_eraseService`, `serviceLayer_neverReadC`, `lowerEC`, `encodeMidC`, `StepAtC`, `stepAtC_of_stepAt` carrying every earlier step over, `itemPost_erasedC`); the marked build (`idealMid_run`, `buildStep_hidden_run`, `rejoin_source`, `afterBuild`, `skelMarks_afterBuild`, `sourceMid_build_hidden`, `lowerEC_native`, and `step_build_hidden`: item 2's build with the mark's value runs the factored response tail as the ideal registration's hidden build does after its allocator hit, when the allocator and the table hold the mark's value). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostFinish` | 29 | Naturality of item 2's opaque Beacon preparation in its handle type (`mapSecrets`, `mapInputs`, `mapPrepared`, `MapCalls`, `collect_map`, `admitted_map`, `prepare_map`) and its key use (`collect_free`, `prepare_free`: it decapsulates only with the owner's key); the grouping of the opaque owner (`groupH`, `owner_grouped`); known handles (`preparationKnown`, `prepSecret`, `targetPrep`, `markedPrep_known`, `markedPrep_bounds`, `known_calls`, `handlesKnown`, `onPool_map`, `known_fullCalls`: the full callback conditions of item 2's `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Shared.owner_realization` for the source's native finish callbacks, `tailKnown`, `tail_known`, `nativeCtx_pure`, `nativeCtx_bind`, `simulate_nativeCtx`) and `middle_finish` (the annotated source finish at a present owner is the grouped owner under `groupH handlesKnown`, by `PqxdhRegistrationJointServiceSchedule.finish_source` from item 2's ownership invariant); the grouped owner through item 2's layers (`stackRun`, `stackRun_pure`, `stackRun_bind`, `skel_groupedPool`, `lowerEC_run`, `stack_prep`, `splitT_ctx`, `splitT_group_known`, `stack_known`, and `owner_stack`: an owner that is not waiting for Phase 2 with the selected KEM key runs through item 2's layers as under `groupH handlesKnown` on the source state); `step_finish` (`StepAtC` for a finish that is not owner k's challenge finish, with `sourceMid_finish`, `upperLayer_finish`, `upperLayer_finish_none`). |

## CE-11b unit U6, third part: decisions and deviations

- Table counters (a finding of this part): the ideal registration's marked build (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps.buildStep`) re-reads the mark's value through the allocator and item 2's persistent lazy table, an allocator hit, and `PqxdhRecordLiftLazyTable.run_hit` charges one lookup for it; item 2's skeleton reads the value from its marks. Item 2's view `encodeMid` (second part) keeps the table state with its counters, so the marked build cannot satisfy `StepAt`. The counters are accounting observers that no step reads, so item 2's view drops them (`encodeMidC`, `lowerEC`, `StepAtC`), every step at the earlier view carries over (`stepAtC_of_stepAt`), and the assembly is to instantiate `sim_option` at the new view (open point 4). The selected accept and owner k's challenge finish read the slot once on both sides; only the marked build differs. This is the "lookup counts" item of design-v2 section 8.4, settled by erasure as the other ghosts were in the second part.
- The post-challenge router holds the placeholder candidate; item 2's comparison of that router with the stopped source router (`PqxdhDynamicKemJointRouter.post_step`) needs the candidate to agree with the decapsulation of the challenge. Only that decapsulation uses the premise, so this part re-proves the comparison away from it (`jointProgram_fresh`) and keeps the challenge decapsulation for owner k's challenge finish; no correctness assumption is used.
- Splitting at the factored value type: item 2's `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemPostSkeleton.SplitN` is stated at the native value type with the candidate's table. The real Server requests read the table only directly, and a small parallel framework at the factored value type with item 2's table layers (`SplitT`) is shorter than transporting `SplitN` through item 2's relabelling (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemEntryFactor.RelC`), whose projection also requires marks that are images of roots.
- Premises of the step lemmas, to be discharged by the middle invariant (open point 1): `step_create` (the KEM pool's size is the owner count, the KEM pool is a prefix of the challenger's pool, room for one creation), `step_bundle` (item 2's ownership invariant `PqxdhRegistrationJointSchedule.Invariant`, prefix pool), `step_accept` (the accept is not the selected one, the Server's identity slot holds its seed, the constructed Server's public identity is its seed's, prefix pool), `step_build` (the build is not hidden in the middle), `step_build_hidden` (the mark's coordinates are allocated and the table holds a value there), `step_finish` (the finish is not owner k's challenge finish, item 2's ownership invariant, prefix pool, and the addressed owner, if its KEM index is the selected one, is not in the `initSent` phase; the last premise, the hypothesis `other`, reads only the owner at the finish's address, and a form about every owner would be false in reachable states, for example with owner k in `initSent` after the selected accept while another owner's finish arrives; after the cut the active flag is set, so with `finishHiddenAt = false` at the addressed owner the premise follows when the owner holding the selected KEM key is owner k, a position fact of open point 1). None is an assumption of a published claim.
- `step_read` covers the prekey and the one-time read as well as the kernel read; `sim_option`'s allowed class admits only the kernel read (second part, review fix pass, finding 5), so the superset costs nothing.
- Real finishes without a support argument: item 2's grouped owner takes its decapsulation result as an opaque handle, and the source's finish uses the secret. The opaque preparation is natural in the handle type (`prepare_map`), so with every decapsulation answered by a known handle (`handlesKnown`) the grouped request's handle is syntactically known; item 2's `owner_realization` (with `known_fullCalls`) and `finish_source` then give the middle's annotated finish, and the known grouped request splits against the native root and continuation as in `SplitT`. `handlesKnown` carries an unused placeholder for the challenge handle; `step_finish` fixes it to the zero vector, and neither side depends on it.
- Modules: four new modules, all registered (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostRequests`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostServer`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostHidden`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostFinish`); owner k's challenge finish goes to a further module, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostChallenge` (registered by the review fix pass, not yet created), and the registry entry of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostFinish`, which named that finish, was corrected; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` (the active branch) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` remain.
- Publication: four Lean commits (one per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block, `inv recount` before `inv rehash`) and two documentation commits on `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut` over central `564dc4d6`, then the review fix pass (one Lean commit, docstrings only, and one documentation commit); reviewed, not integrated.

## CE-11b unit U6, third part: names for consumers

- Per-request steps: `step_hmac`, `step_read`, `step_create`, `step_bundle` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostRequests`); `step_accept`, `step_build` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostServer`); `step_build_hidden`, and for the assembly `lowerEC`, `encodeMidC`, `StepAtC`, `stepAtC_of_stepAt`, `itemPost_erasedC` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostHidden`); `step_finish` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostFinish`).
- Router and service tools: `challengeDecaps`, `jointProgram_fresh`, `fresh_of_noDecaps`, `postRouter`, `poolUp`, `service_poolProgram`, `lowerE_pool` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostRequests`); `service_encProgram`, `lowerE_enc`, `SplitT` with its closure lemmas, `prefix_chain`, `source_accept_run` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostServer`); `run_neverRead`, `optionLower_neverRead_both`, `idealMid_run`, `lowerEC_native` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostHidden`); for owner k's challenge finish `prepare_map`, `MapCalls`, `groupH`, `owner_grouped`, `stackRun`, `stackRun_bind`, `stack_prep`, `lowerEC_run`, `owner_stack`, `upperLayer_finish` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostFinish`).

## CE-11b unit U6, third part: contracts used

None. These modules use no codec contract and no new assumption. The facts of the middle state that the step lemmas take as premises are intermediate results of the unit, listed under "decisions and deviations".

## CE-11b unit U6, third part: open points

The active branch `ActiveBranch`, in module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost` (registered); every step below is to be stated at the view without table counters (`StepAtC`).

1. The invariants of the middle execution, as in the second part's open point 1, now also supplying the premises of this part's steps: the KEM pool is a prefix of the challenger's pool with one key pair per owner and its size within the room (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource.PoolPrefix`, `PoolOwned`), item 2's ownership invariant and the Server's identity slot, the constructed Server's public identity (from its birth), the mark's coordinates allocated with a stored value, the accept counter past the selected ordinal and the active flag set, the owner holding the selected KEM key being owner k (each owner's KEM index is its position; with the active flag set and `finishHiddenAt = false` at the addressed owner this gives `step_finish`'s premise, which concerns the addressed owner only: if its KEM index is the selected one, it is not in the `initSent` phase; the invariant must not require that no owner is in `initSent` with the selected key, since owner k may be in that phase while another owner's finish arrives); on the CE side CE-3's `Good` kept by `compiledMid`.
2. Owner k's challenge finish against `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps.finishChallenge` (the real finishes are `step_finish`), in a new module, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostChallenge` (registered, not yet created). Plan: (a) the middle side, `sourceMid` at `finishHiddenAt = true`, is the ideal registration's finish (an `rfl` helper as `sourceMid_build_hidden`) and, through `idealMid_run`, `finishStep`'s challenge branch: `finishChallenge` from no recorded coordinates under the allocator and item 2's lazy table, the LQ-F snapshot and the install ghost; (b) on item 2's side `upperLayer_finish` at owner k and `stackRun_bind` split the grouped owner; the preparation runs as in `stack_prep` except at the selected challenge decapsulation, where the post-challenge router answers from its placeholder without a CCA query and item 2's marked preparation returns the challenge handle, so a variant of `owner_stack` with a handle that is known or the challenge marker is needed; (c) the grouped request with the challenge marker is `groupCE_challenge`, whose comparison is under `publicLocalV`, to be carried to item 2's table layers (the allocator read of the four DH coordinates is the same on both sides); (d) `groupedOwnerCE` runs at the native owner type (identity seed, key pair) and `groupedOwner` at item 2's encoded type (identity index, public key with KEM index): a naturality lemma of `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Shared.prepare` in its identity, key and secret-conversion types, as `prepare_map` is in the handle type, relates them, with `privateConversion` and `dhIdentity` at the index against the seed.
3. The selected accept's steps after encapsulation on item 2's side (unchanged from the second part's open point 3).
4. Assembly: `sim_option` with `lower := lowerEC`, `project := encodeMidC`, the steps carried by `stepAtC_of_stepAt`, `itemPost_erasedC`, the CE side's `compile_simulationMid`, `activeCE_reply` and the decision; then `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` with `ideal_compile_of_active`.
5. Reviewed (findings fixed in "CE-11b unit U6, third part: review fix pass"), and the fix pass confirmed by a second review ("CE-11b unit U6, third part: confirming review"). Integration (PROTOCOL section 6). Central moved from `564dc4d6` to `39fd1c50` before this part's documentation commits (Codex's `3d212990`, `d281c804`, `3915c3f0` and `39fd1c50`: 81 files, 35 new Lean modules, no CE-11b file; 1855 handwritten Lean files), overlapping this part only in the analysis and the three inventory places (the root imports, `check-inventory.sh`, `reviewed-inventory.txt`); a trial merge of the topic with `39fd1c50` conflicts only in the handwritten Lean count of `check-inventory.sh` and in two hunks of `reviewed-inventory.txt` (the root's digest row, and the digest row of `check-inventory.sh` with the adjacent row of `trusted-boundary.md`, which central changed). ## CE-11b unit U6, third part: review fix pass

An independent review of `71fa9922` in two parts (semantic and hygiene) found no error in the Lean statements or proofs; both parts approved with nits. The semantic part rechecked the four modules, and the hygiene part rechecked the four modules and the root and ran its own focused `check-inventory` on the clean tree (exit 0). No statement, proof or guard changed, no theorem was added or removed, and the 94 theorems keep their exact guards.

- Minor (semantic): the wording of `step_finish`'s last premise. The status paragraph, the decisions bullet on the premises, open point 1 and the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostFinish` stated it as a condition on every owner ("no owner holding the selected KEM key waiting for Phase 2", "an owner at the selected KEM index is not in the `initSent` phase"). Read that way it is false in reachable states (owner k in `initSent` after the selected accept while another owner's finish arrives), and it would not follow from `finishHiddenAt = false`, which speaks about the addressed owner only. The Lean hypothesis `other` is about the addressed owner, as were the analysis, the docstring of `step_finish` and the receipt. The four texts now say that the addressed owner, if its KEM index is the selected one, is not in the `initSent` phase, and open point 1 says that the middle invariant must not require the form about every owner. No Lean statement changes.

## CE-11b unit U4, remainder: what is proved

The remainder of unit U4 (track u4b) adds four modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 77 guarded theorems (73 from the first pass and 4 from the review fix pass); no existing proof module changed (the root gains four imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeaconFacts` | 20 | The native facts the Beacon converse needs, read off the extracted Rust through the existing source correspondences. A real Beacon finish of an owner that is not in the `initSent` phase returns nothing (`realFinish_none`; `beacon.rs`, `finish_registration`, rejects such a finish before reading the secret key) and a Phase 2 finish returns bytes only when it establishes the owner (`finish_some`, `finish_none`). Owner k's preparation with the challenge handle answers the decapsulation of a ciphertext whose bytes are the challenge ciphertext by the challenge marker (`collect_answers`, `prepare_challenge`), so a challenge finish on such an input that returns bytes sets the Beacon install ghost at coordinates that hold a slot (`finishChallenge_beacon`, with `commitEnv_none`). The selected raw accept marks a session only when it is active, and when it marks one it publishes there one token whose pending registration carries the challenge ciphertext (`acceptSelected_token`, through `program_ciphertext`, `accept_ciphertext` on the extracted `server_accept` via `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueCore.accepted_original` and `PqxdhRegistrationInputs.accepted_used`, `tailCE_client_token`, `tailCE_tail_token`, `acceptActive_token`, `acceptExposed_token`). Every published response of the generic Server build is the Phase 2 writer's output on the fields of a candidate that carries the pending registration's KEM ciphertext (`GWritten`, `afterInitialization_written`, `candidate_written`, `genericBuild_written`, with `PqxdhServerAuthNegativeControl.prepare_commit_fields`); for the forwarded split build the writer is the codec's (`writeImpl`, `tailFwd_writeImpl`, `buildSplitFwd_written`), and so for the ideal environment's marked build at the session's token (`buildCached_written`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon` | 23 | The Beacon provenance invariant `BeaconProv` between the transcript and the environment state, beside `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance.Prov`: a mark comes only with the active flag; the marked session's pending token carries the challenge ciphertext; every successful build of the marked session is written with it (`ChallengeWritten`); the Beacon install ghost's coordinates hold a slot; after every late finish of owner `k` (`LateFinish`: made after the selected raw accept) whose input reads the challenge ciphertext (`ChallengeRead`), either no session is marked or the Beacon ghost is set. Kept by every command of the ideal step (`beacon_rStep`, from `beacon_realQuiet`, `beacon_acceptOther`, `beacon_selected`, `beacon_realBuild`, `beacon_markedBuild`, `beacon_challenge`) and by every other entry (`beacon_quiet`), from the startup (`beacon_initial`); on every outcome of the annotated record world with `envGD`, together with `Prov` (`world_beacon`). At a state, under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`: the key match and L3 for designations on any prefix of the transcript (`keyFacts_marked_prefix`, `trace_designated_prefix`) and L4 (`beacon_converse`: when owner `k` designates the target on a prefix, the Beacon ghost is set at coordinates holding a slot or the designating finish was made before the selected raw accept; through `written_read`, `finishOf_mem`, `finish_split`). On every outcome of the annotated record world with `envGD`: `ideal_slots_prefix`, `ideal_beacon` (L4) and `ideal_slots` (`IdealSlots ∨ Anomaly`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess` | 10 | A generic coupling of two stateful simulations of one program that are identical until a stop and keep a frozen observable once stopped (`UntilStop`, `relTriple_until_stop`, the variant of `PqxdhServerAuthEntryFactor.relTriple_until_bad` that carries the observable), with the equality in law of every view that agrees on such coupled final states (`lawEq_until_stop`). The transcript guess: `cutAt` (the prefix before the raw accept attempt of an ordinal; `cutAt_prefix`, `acceptCount_cons`, `cutAt_append`, `cutAt_keeps`), `earlyFinish` (the input of owner `k`'s first successful finish made before the selected raw accept; `earlyFinish_eq` under `FinishBefore`), `guessCt` (the bytes of that input's Phase 2 ciphertext; `guessCt_append`). `anomaly_guess`: on every outcome of the record world with `envGD`, under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, the anomaly implies that the transcript guess is the drawn challenge ciphertext. `anomaly_prefix_guess` (review fix pass): the same for the anomaly on any prefix `pre` of the transcript (`Anomaly … pre`, that is `BeaconLinked … pre ∧ FinishBefore t pre`, the exceptional alternative of `ideal_slots_prefix`); the designating finish on the prefix is owner `k`'s first successful finish in the final transcript too, and the build response it is linked to stays in the transcript. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound` | 24 | The final split game does not read the challenge ciphertext before the selected raw accept (it is first read there, and owner `k`'s hidden challenge finish reads it again afterwards): while the selected accept was not active and marked no session, every command other than the selected raw accept is the same at every challenge (`idealSplit_challenge`, `handler_challenge`, `coinSend_same`). On the game flattened with the secrecy layer (`splitHandler`, `splitHandler_run`), the joint states before the selected raw accept (`Early`) are kept by every other step (`handler_backend`, `splitHandler_transcript`, `early_step`, `early_initial`), the selected raw accept stops both runs with the guess of the same transcript (`SelectedQ`, `cutAt_selected`, `stopGuess`, `selected_stop`) and the guess is frozen after it (`stopGuess_keeps`), so the transcript guess has the same law at every challenge (`split_step`, `split_guess_law`, `guessView`, `game_guess`, `game_guess_law`). With the encapsulation drawn after the guess and the best guess of each public key (`pairGuess_le`, `zeroCt`, `split_guess_le`), and the guess event transferred to the split game by `c1_split`: `guess_le_pk_sup` (review fix pass; the transcript guess event on the record world with `envGD` has probability at most `ctGuessPk`, without a read-back premise). Through the anomaly's implications (`evalDist_decide_mono`): `anomaly_le_pk_sup` (the anomaly on the record world with `envGD` has probability at most `ctGuessPk`) and `anomaly_le` (at most `ctGuess`), and, from the review fix pass, `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup` and `anomaly_prefix_le` (the anomaly on some prefix of the final transcript, at most `ctGuessPk` and `ctGuess`), under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`. |

In plain terms: on every run of the record world with the ideal environment of a target, if the selected Beacon owner designates the target in the public transcript, then either it holds a hidden slot in the World-level provenance annotation, or its designating finish happened before the selected raw accept; with the Server-side facts of the first part this gives C5 restated, `IdealSlots ∨ Anomaly`. The anomaly, a public event, has probability at most the best chance of predicting an encapsulation's ciphertext from the public key, because the transcript before the selected raw accept does not depend on the challenge ciphertext and the designating finish's ciphertext must equal it. The same bound holds for the anomaly computed on an earlier part of the transcript, which a consumer that designates at an intermediate time needs.

## CE-11b unit U4, remainder: decisions and deviations

- A second invariant of the annotated record world (`BeaconProv`) is kept beside `Prov` (published and frozen) instead of new conjuncts of `Prov`; `world_beacon` instantiates `PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins.world_keeps_origins` with their conjunction. This is an implementation-level choice; no assumption is added.
- `ideal_slots_prefix` is stated for designations computed on any prefix of the final transcript, with the final provenance annotation. Designation is not monotone in the transcript (a smaller owner or session can become eligible later), but the designation's accept, bundle, build and finish entries persist, which is what the proof uses. Whether a designated handle holds its slot already at the prefix's time (the annotation at intermediate states) is not stated; it follows the same route once a consumer needs it.
- `ideal_slots`, `ideal_beacon` and `ideal_slots_prefix` take `Phase2ReadBack` besides `Phase1ReadBack` (design-v2 section 7.2 named both for L4); the Server-side conjuncts still need only `Phase1ReadBack` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance.ideal_slots_restrictions` and `ideal_slots_server`).
- `anomaly_le` is proved through `c1_split` (whose projection keeps the drawn parameters) and an identical-until-selected-accept coupling of the final split game over the challenge, followed by a draw swap, not through `c1_ideal` and a one-point lazy cache as design-v2 section 7.2 item 6 planned. The route gives the stronger bound `ctGuessPk` (`anomaly_le_pk_sup`); `anomaly_le` with `ctGuess` follows by `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace.ctGuessPk_le`.
- Four registered modules (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeaconFacts`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound`) instead of one module for the converse and one for the anomaly; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly` stays frozen.
- The bound for the anomaly on a prefix (review fix pass) is stated for the event that some prefix of the final transcript has the anomaly, the exceptional alternative of `ideal_slots_prefix` as it stands; it contains the anomaly of the final transcript (empty rest) but is not contained in it, since designation is not monotone. The common transfer of both bounds is `guess_le_pk_sup`, factored out of `anomaly_le_pk_sup`, whose statement is unchanged.
- The name `anomaly_le_pk_sup` is the public-key supremum (`ctGuessPk`); it is not the planned `anomaly_le_pk` (`ctGuessBy` at a constructed guesser).
- Not delivered in this track: `anomaly_le_pk` (open points) and unit U8.

- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon`: `BeaconProv`, `world_beacon`, `beacon_converse`, `ideal_beacon`, `ideal_slots`, `ideal_slots_prefix`, `LateFinish`, `ChallengeRead`, `ChallengeWritten`, `written_read`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess`: `UntilStop`, `relTriple_until_stop`, `lawEq_until_stop`, `cutAt`, `earlyFinish`, `guessCt`, `anomaly_guess`, `anomaly_prefix_guess`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound`: `anomaly_le`, `anomaly_le_pk_sup`, `anomaly_prefix_le`, `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup`, `guess_le_pk_sup`, `split_guess_le`, `game_guess_law`, `pairGuess_le`, `splitHandler`.

## CE-11b unit U4, remainder: contracts used

No new assumption and no new contract. `keyFacts_marked_prefix`, `trace_designated_prefix`, `beacon_converse`, `ideal_slots_prefix`, `ideal_beacon`, `ideal_slots`, `anomaly_guess`, `anomaly_prefix_guess`, `anomaly_le_pk_sup`, `anomaly_le`, `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup` and `anomaly_prefix_le` take the approved codec contracts `PqxdhServerAuthNegativeControl.Phase1ReadBack` and (all but the first two) `Phase2ReadBack`; `written_read` takes `Phase2ReadBack` only (thirteen theorems in all, as in the receipt's premises). `guess_le_pk_sup` takes neither. Everything else is a support fact of programs, an invariant of outcomes, an equality in law or a bound by a definition (`ctGuessPk`, `ctGuess`).

## CE-11b unit U4, remainder: open points

- `anomaly_le_pk` (plan section 75, design-v2 section 28) is this track's next step; unit U4 is not complete without it. Statement: `𝒟[Anomaly … on World.run (envGD t)] {true} ≤ PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly.ctGuessBy setup (anomalyGuesser …)` under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, where `anomalyGuesser` is an explicit program over `unifSpec + baseSpec`, built from the adversary, that takes the public key and never takes the selected secret key (`chosen.2`) or the challenge ciphertext (`p.challenge`) as input; this is the form CE-12a's predict-then-compare IND-CCA reduction consumes. With it comes `anomaly_prefix_le_pk`, the same bound for the event of `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup`; both events imply the transcript guess event, so one bound of the guess event serves both, as `guess_le_pk_sup` does here. Module: the new `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` (registered, not yet created), since the four modules of this track are frozen once integrated; integrating them first is fine. The published `anomaly_le_pk_sup` bounds by the public-key supremum `ctGuessPk`, which no approved assumption bounds, so it does not stand in for this form. The guess of `anomaly_le` (owner `k`'s first successful finish before the selected raw accept) is a random variable of the whole selected key pair: owner `k`'s finishes before the selected raw accept decapsulate with its secret key. Route, reusing this track's machinery: (a) fix the guess at owner `k`'s first finish made in `initSent` before the selected raw accept, by a ghost component of the backend (with a projection lemma for `gameWith` with the extended backend) or by the public phase with CE-5's agreement for owner `k`. On the anomaly the designating finish is that finish, by two facts. First, a finish outside `initSent` is rejected before the secret key is read and without a state change (`realFinish_none`; `beacon.rs`, `finish_registration`, lines 402-406), so every successful finish is made in `initSent`. Second, the first finish made in `initSent` leaves `initSent`: it establishes the Beacon (`finish_some`) or aborts it (`beacon.rs` lines 487-490: a staged failure calls `abort_registration`, as do the later identity checks; neither `Established` nor `Aborted` holds the KEM secret key; the abort side is still to be stated from `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Publication.finish`). So owner `k`'s first successful finish is its first finish made in `initSent`, the only finish that reads its secret key. (b) Couple the final split game at the drawn key pair and at the same public key with another secret key, identical until that stop or the selected raw accept (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess.relTriple_until_stop`), with states related by replacing owner `k`'s KEM secret key in its Beacon phase and in the pool; every command before the stop must keep this relation (the pool create, owner `k`'s bundle, which carries the key pair unchanged, a finish of owner `k` outside `initSent`, which is rejected, and every command of another owner, of the Server, and every send and raw receive). (c) `anomalyGuesser` draws a dummy key pair and the other pool entries, runs the split game at the public key with the dummy secret key, and returns the guess. (d) Unfold `ctGuessBy` against `drawParams` with `LazyOracleDiscard.lawEq_swap` as in `split_guess_le`. About 2.5-5 agent-days.
- L2 (the Beacon ghost implies that owner `k` is established in the transcript) needs CE-5's agreement for the ideal step; `IdealSlots` does not use it. About 0.5-1 if a consumer needs it.
- The slot of a designated handle at the time of a prefix (the annotation at intermediate states, `ideal_slots_prefix` with the prefix's annotation): the same route on the annotated run's intermediate states, about 0.25-0.5 if a consumer needs it. The probability of the prefix form's exceptional alternative is bounded since the review fix pass (`anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup`, `anomaly_prefix_le`).
- Unit U8 (CE key-first and key-last, plan section 49 Q3; design version 1 section 7) is a step of its own, after `anomaly_le_pk`; plan section 14's row stays open until it is proved. It proves `idealGame_key_first`, `idealGame_key_last` and `split_key_first` in the new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeyFirst` (registered, not yet created). Route: (0) first replace item 2's persistent lazy table (`PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.tableService`, which counts lookups and fresh draws) by VCVio's lazily sampled cache (`lazyService`) through the slot-naturality projection, `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.represent` at `table_service_proj` with `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSlots.slotCore_natural`, as in `represent_split`; this comes first because the table's counters break the exact commutation of the steps with completions (a completion of slot 0 at the startup charges no fresh draw, and the later query at slot 0 is then a hit, which charges none either, while without the completion it is a miss, which charges one, so the draw counts of the final states differ); (1) complete slot 0 of the lazy cache (the material cache for the split world) at the startup (`LazyOracleDiscard.commutes_simulateQ`; the allocator assigns slot 0 first, so the later query at slot 0 is a hit); (2) drop the final completion of an untouched slot (`LazyOracleDiscard.lawEq_drop`); (3) split the 108 bytes (`PqxdhServerAuthCtxMaterial.key_first`); (4) swap the draws for key-last. Not started. Estimate rechecked with the projection step: about 1.25-2.25 (1-2 before; the projection is an instance of existing lemmas, about 0.25).
- Integration: central is at `dd681f34`, not an ancestor of this branch (status paragraph); the integration merges it (or a later central) first, with central's side first at every conflicting anchor, then recounts (1866 to 1870 handwritten Lean files) and rehashes the inventory and reruns the focused checks. In `check-inventory.sh` only the module blocks conflict: the handwritten Lean count changed identically on both sides and merges silently to 1866, so the recount to 1870 is required (`check-inventory.sh` fails otherwise); the conflicting digest rows, of the root and of `check-inventory.sh`, are in `reviewed-inventory.txt` (corrected after the confirming review).

## CE-11b unit U4, remainder: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `codex/deployment-ce11b` at `1217aa58` found no wrong statement, proof or hidden assumption. The four modules and the root recompiled from the committed sources (exit 0, empty logs; the hygiene part also from a `git archive` against its own mirror, with oleans byte-identical to the track's); 73 theorems with 73 exact guards within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; no forbidden token; footers, pushed head, receipt and inventory rows checked; the focused inventory passed. Probes (not published) showed that `ideal_slots`, `annotatedRun_erase` and `anomaly_le` compose to the bound on the failure of C5 restated that design-v2 section 9.1 needs, and that the anomaly on a prefix implies the transcript guess event; a negative control (`ideal_slots` does not give `IdealSlots` alone) failed as expected. Both parts approved with findings, resolved as follows.

- Major, scope gap (disclosed): `anomaly_le_pk` and the constructed `anomalyGuesser` are not delivered, so CE-12a's Q2(b) reduction cannot use anything yet. Unit U4 is not recorded as complete (status paragraph, open points, plan sections 80 and 82, receipt). `anomaly_le_pk` and its prefix form are this track's next step, in the new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` (registered, not yet created), with a guesser that never takes `chosen.2` or `p.challenge` as input ("open points").

- Minor: no bound covered the exceptional alternative of `ideal_slots_prefix`, the anomaly on a prefix, which is not contained in the anomaly of the final transcript because designation is not monotone. New theorems: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess.anomaly_prefix_guess` (on every outcome of the record world with `envGD`, under both read-backs, `Anomaly … pre` for a prefix `pre` of the final transcript implies that the transcript guess of the final transcript is the drawn challenge ciphertext; the reviewer's probe as a theorem), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound.guess_le_pk_sup` (the transcript guess event on the record world with `envGD` at most `ctGuessPk`, without a read-back premise: the `c1_split` transfer factored out of `anomaly_le_pk_sup`), `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup` (the event `∃ pre rest, transcript = pre ++ rest ∧ Anomaly … pre`, that is `BeaconLinked … pre ∧ FinishBefore t pre`, at most `ctGuessPk`) and `anomaly_prefix_le` (at most `ctGuess`). The bound by `ctGuessBy` for this event comes with `anomaly_le_pk`.

The status paragraph and the open points now record it, with the trial merge's conflict set and the handwritten Lean count of 1870 after the merge; plan section 80 is corrected; the integration merges `dd681f34` first.

The optional rewrite with measure-level lemmas was not done.

## CE-11b unit U6, fourth part: what is proved

This fourth part of unit U6 adds ten modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 101 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains ten imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`. As in the third part, item 2's steps are stated at item 2's view without table counters (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostHidden.StepAtC`, `encodeMidC`, `lowerEC`). The final statement is `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile.ideal_compile`: under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, for every adversary that respects its budget, `𝒟[slotGame setup codec checks ensureInit none (decision setup.source.crypto codec setup.serverKeyId budget event claim g) (compileFull setup codec route bounds mode adversary) runtime budget.create (selectedKem budget g) g.1.val 2] {true}` equals `𝒟[(fun outcome => decide (happens event (classicalOf outcome) = true ∧ designated setup.source.crypto codec setup.serverKeyId budget claim (classicalOf outcome) = some g)) <$> (idealGame setup codec route checks budget g ensureInit bounds mode adversary).run runtime] {true}`, the design's second compile lemma `ideal_compile` in the form of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealActive.ideal_compile_of_active`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostChallenge` | 25 | Owner k's challenge finish. Relabelling: the opaque Beacon preparation is natural in the owner's identity, KEM key and identity-conversion types for one owner (`RelabelCalls`, `collect_relabel`, `prepare_relabel`), which relates item 2's encoded owner (identity index, public key with KEM index) to the native owner (identity seed, key pair). The preparation through item 2's layers: the post-challenge router answers the decapsulation of the challenge ciphertext at the created selected index from its placeholder candidate without a CCA query (`reservation_selected`, `post_target`) and item 2's marked preparation returns the challenge marker (`stack_target`); every other preparation request is item 2's marked preparation with known handles under the actual services, whose pool changes item 2's view erases (`prepItem`, `prepKnown_erase`, `stack_prepQuery`, `stack_prepProgram`); item 2's answers relabel the native answers with the challenge handle (`prep_calls`), which keep the CE-native state (`slot_prep`). The grouped request: a known handle on both sides is item 2's native grouped answer (`slot_known` with the third part's `stack_known`); the challenge marker is `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps.groupCE_challenge` carried through item 2's table layers to the allocator (`tableLayer_flat`, `stack_challenge`, `groupCE_keep`, `stack_challengeC`). `owner_challenge`: owner k's grouped owner through item 2's layers is the CE-native grouped owner `groupedOwnerCE` under `ownerImplCE` past the allocator, projected. The allocator keeps a good state and every value it represents (`slot_keep`, `slot_keepProgram`). The middle side and the step: `rejoin_set`, `afterChallenge`, `finishStep_challenge`, `finishChallenge_found`, `sourceMid_challenge`, `step_challenge`. `finish_other` derives the third part's premise of `step_finish` about the addressed owner from the finish not being owner k's challenge finish, the active flag and the owner's KEM index being its position, and `step_finishActive` gives the step of every finish after an active selected accept. |

## CE-11b unit U6, fourth part: decisions and deviations

- Relabelling for one owner: the third part's handle naturality (`prepare_map`) relabels by a function. The identity conversions of the encoded and the native owner (an identity index against a seed) are not functions of each other on every identity, so `RelabelCalls` relates the two interpretations only at the owner's own identity, key and converted identity, with the private conversion answered purely on both sides; the collection uses no other identity.
- The preparation per request: the third part's `stack_prep` needs a preparation without the selected challenge decapsulation, which owner k's challenge finish makes. This part computes item 2's layers on each preparation request (`stack_prepQuery`): the selected challenge decapsulation through the post-challenge router directly (the reservation holds the selected public key without a secret, the router answers the placeholder candidate, the marked preparation returns the challenge marker), every other request through `stack_prep` on a one-request program, whose pool changes are transcripts that item 2's view erases (`prepKnown_erase`).
- Item 2's view of the mark after a hidden read: owner k's challenge finish reads the hidden value at the four DH coordinates through the allocator. Item 2's view computes the mark's value from the allocator and the table, so the step needs that the read keeps the mark's stored value: a good allocator state (`SlotGood`) and a stored value at the mark's coordinates are premises of `step_challenge` and fields of `ItemInv`, kept by every middle step (`slot_keep`, `slot_keepProgram`).
- `step_finish`'s premise (the third part's open point 1, in the addressed-owner form): `finish_other` derives it from `finishHiddenAt = false` at the addressed owner, the active flag and the owner positions; no condition on other owners is used.
- The simulated requests: `Allowed` excludes only identity and KEM field reads, a weaker exclusion than the second part's open point 2 (which excluded the prekey and one-time prekey reads too), since `step_read` covers every field read without a private service. The assembly derives `onlyAllowed` from the transported caps, which give both excluded kinds the cap 0.
- The selected accept: the CE-native tail runs on the native state with a recorded mark through the allocator, item 2's skeleton on the game state without observers with a mark that holds the value; `SplitC` relates them with item 2's mark read off the table (`marksOf`). Its premise of a free slot is needed only by the one hidden read of the challenge-marked root request, and at the cut the allocator is empty. `resume_accept` is stated from the game state without observers; `resume_clean` connects it to item 2's post-challenge phase.
- Olean prefix: the task named `ce11b/u6b/olean` as the fresh prefix, but that path is the second part's existing prefix; this part used a new registered prefix `ce11b/u6b/p4/olean` (a resolved symlink mirror of the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `dd681f34`, `ce11b/u6b/p3/int/olean`, without the root olean).
- One middle run, two views: item 2's side of an active cut is the selected accept through the allocator followed by the projection of the tagged continuation's middle run (`itemPost_middle`), and the CE side is the same selected accept followed by the merge image of the same middle run (`activeCE_middle`); the decisions agree on the merge image (`decision_merge`). `activeBranch_of_middle` leaves exactly the CE side of the middle invariant as an obligation (`MiddleInv`, `MiddleStart`), as the second part's architecture planned.
- The CE side of the middle invariant is CE-3's full invariant `Good` with the selected accept behind the accept counter (`GoodMid`). It needs neither item 2's invariant nor the creation room: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool.good_step_pool` holds for any pool, and the accept counter only grows, so the selected raw accept is never reached again in the middle execution.
