The writer fact is proved for the generic build with a writer callback that answers the writer's output (`genericBuildW_shape`, a strengthening of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvBuild.genericBuild_shape`), and the logged factored build is reduced to it by answering the Phase 2 writer before the native tail runs (`writeImpl`, `tailImpl_write`, `factored_logged`), so that VCVio's `support_simulateQ_run'_subset` keeps the writer's answers. `continueRust_shape` restates `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvBuild.continueRustG_shape` because that module is not in the import closure; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostGoodBuild` imports `PqxdhDeploymentSplitFactor` for `freeBuild` and `factored_free` (seven further modules in its closure).
- Owner k's challenge finish: the preparation's facts are proved at the probabilistic level (`collectCE_inputs`, `prepareCE_facts`) and carried through the allocator by the fourth part's `slot_prep`, because `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Preparation.prepare_origin` needs a handler in `StateT A ProbComp`; the staged registration's facts come from the opening of the metadata-cleared candidate on both branches of `groupCE` (`afterRoot_opening`, `hiddenAfter_opening`).

## CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: what is proved

Track u4c adds three modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 78 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root gains three imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeySwap` | 31 | Owner `k`'s KEM secret key replaced in the final split game. The replacement (`withSecret`, `swapOwner`, `swapSched`, `swapNative`, `swapX`) changes the secret half of owner `index`'s KEM key pair, the only KEM secret an owner state holds (in the `fresh`, `freshWithCoins` and `initSent` phases), through `PqxdhBeaconPhase1Publication.mapState`; `Holds`/`HoldsX` say that owner `index` holds a given secret key; `Swaps swap inv first second` relates two stateful steps: from a state satisfying the invariant, the second step at the replaced state is the first step with the replacement applied to its final state, and the first step keeps the invariant. Per command: steps on the shared native state and the Server's record steps are frames of the owners (`onShared_swaps`, `onShared_owners`, `serverStep_swaps`, `realAccept_swaps`); the pool create installs the replaced key pair at owner `index` from the pool with that entry replaced (`createPool_swaps`, `createReg_swaps`, `pool_withChosen`); the bundle is natural in the key pair through the public projection (`bundle_swaps`, from `PqxdhLivePhase1Bundle.bundle_map`); the Beacon record interpreter is natural in it (`beaconImpl_map`: inactive phases reject without reading it, the established phase holds none; `beaconStep_swaps`, with `record_map` and `logged_map`); a finish of another owner is a frame (`finish_other_swaps`); the LQ finish snapshot reads only scalars (`value_swap`, `snapshotAt_swap`); `onNative_swaps`, `onBase_swaps`, `realRegAt_swaps`, `nativeHandler_swaps` lift these through the deployment state. `split_swaps`: before the selected raw accept (the selected accept neither active nor marking, the query not the selected raw accept), every query of the final split game other than a finish of owner `k` commutes with replacing owner `k`'s secret key in the state and in the challenger's parameters (`withChosen`), and keeps owner `k`'s secret key; `swapX_initial`. The native Beacon finish decapsulates at most once (`isDecapsulate`, `collect_decapsulate`, `prepare_decapsulate`, `isDecapsulateNative`, `nativeOwner_decapsulate`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` | 31 | `ctGuessByCca setup guess`: the probability that a guesser over the KEM's IND-CCA oracle interface (`MlKemCCAOracleSpec`), run on the public key of a fresh key pair with the pre-challenge decapsulation oracle of its secret key (`KEMScheme.IND_CCA_preChallengeImpl`) and before the encapsulation, outputs the encapsulation's ciphertext (`ctGuessByCca_evalKem`; at most `ctGuessPk`, `ctGuessByCca_le_pk`; for a guesser without decapsulation queries it is `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly.ctGuessBy`, `ctGuessByCca_ambient`). The oracle finish: owner `k`'s registration finish with the decapsulation callback answered by the oracle at the ciphertext's bytes (`prepOracle`, `oracleCallbacks`, `oracleFinish`); interpreted by the pre-challenge oracle of the selected secret key (`interp`, `simulateQ_interp`, `interp_coins`) and with any key pair in the owner's state, it is the real finish (`oracleCallbacks_interp`, `oracleFinish_interp`, through `PqxdhPhase2KemHandles.native_owner_run`). The guesser's native handler `guessReg`: the final split game's step on the guesser's own coins, except a finish of owner `k` while the selected accept is not active, which is the oracle finish (`guessReg_lift`, `guessReg_oracle`); interpreted, at the state with owner `k`'s key replaced, it is the split game's step before the selected raw accept (`guessReg_interp`, `coinSend_swaps`). The flattened games (`flat`, `splitHandler_flat`, `mapRun_flat`, `flat_transcript`, `flat_keeps`, `flat_selected`, `simulate_swaps`, `flat_step`) are identical until the selected raw accept up to the replacement, which stops both with the guess of the same transcript, so the transcript guess has the same law (`guess_lawEq`, through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess.lawEq_until_stop`). `anomalyGuesser`: draws a key pair of its own, the other pool entries and the Server construction on its own coins, runs the split game at the selected public key with its own secret key and the all-zero challenge under `guessReg`, and outputs the transcript guess. `guesser_eval`, `pair_lawEq`, `split_guess_cca` (on the challenger's draws followed by the final split game, the transcript guess equals the challenge ciphertext with probability exactly `ctGuessByCca` of the guesser), `guess_le_cca` (the guess event on the record world with `envGD`, through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.c1_split`, no read-back premise), `anomaly_le_pk` and `anomaly_prefix_le_pk` (under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess.anomaly_guess` and `anomaly_prefix_guess`). Query bound: `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations` (at most `budget.finish` decapsulation queries against an adversary that respects the budget; `ccaCoins_decapsulate`, `oracleFinish_decapsulate`, `guessReg_decapsulate`, `guessReg_decapsulate_other`, `flat_decapsulate`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeyFirst` | 16 | Unit U8. Generic eager sampling of one point of a lazy cache in the backend (`cacheLens`, `eraseService`, `outcomeMap_erase`, `game_eager`): when every step of the deployment handler commutes with completions of the cache (`LazyOracleDiscard.Commutes`), the game whose cache starts with the point holding a uniform answer drawn before the game has the law of the game with the empty cache, for every view of the outcome that erases the service state (`LazyOracleDiscard.commutes_simulateQ`, then `lawEq_drop` for the completion moved to the end). The first-touch allocator in front of a slot service commutes with completions of a lazy cache inside the service state when the service does (`serviceLens`, `slotImpl_commutes`, `slotImpl_commutes_of`), so do slot cores over the lazy slot cache (`slotCore_commutes`, `handler_commutes`). `idealGame_lazy`: `CE.idealGame g` over item 2's counted table has the law of its lazy representation over `lazyService` for every such view (`PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.represent` with `table_service_proj` and `idealCore_natural`). `idealGame_key_first`: the game that draws slot 0's 32-byte message key before everything else, then its 76-byte residual and the challenger's draws, and starts with slot 0 holding the reassembled output (`idealGameKeyFirst`, `keyInit`, `slot0`, `lazyInit`, `output_key_first` from `PqxdhServerAuthCtxMaterial.key_first`); `idealGame_keyFirst_keyLast`, `idealGame_key_last` (`idealGameKeyLast`). The final split form: its slot steps and hidden installs commute with completions of the material cache (`materialLens`, `slotRunX_commutes`, `installStep_commutes`, `split_handler_commutes`), so `split_key_first`: the split game at given parameters has the law of the game that draws slot 0's `key32` and then its `nonce12` before the game and starts with slot 0's material in the cache (`materialInit`, with `PqxdhFullSecretInputKdf.pairSample`); `split_key_last` swaps the two draws. |

In plain terms: the Beacon-side anomaly, which the record lift cannot exclude, is now bounded by the chance that a program that knows only the selected public key and can ask for decapsulations under the selected secret key predicts the ciphertext of a later encapsulation to that key. The program runs the whole ideal game itself; where the game would decapsulate under the selected key (owner `k`'s Phase 2 finishes before the selected accept), it asks the oracle, and everywhere else the selected secret key is never read, so a key of the program's own can stand in for it. This is the quantity that CE-12a's predict-then-compare reduction turns into an IND-CCA advantage. Unit U8 states the ideal game with slot 0's message key as a separate first (or last) draw, the analogue of item 2's stage S6; no CE-12 headline consumes it (design-v2 section 10.2: no headline reads slot 0's `key32` or `nonce12`), and it is built because plan section 49 Q3 keeps plan section 14's row.

## CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: decisions and deviations

- Route (plan section 84, Claude's decision): the guesser has oracle access to the decapsulation of the selected key. The proof couples the final split game at the selected key pair with the guesser's game, interpreted by the selected key's pre-challenge oracle. In the guesser's game owner `k`'s state holds a secret key of the guesser's own (a value of the secret key type is needed there), but no step reads it before the selected raw accept: every step other than owner `k`'s finish is natural in it (`split_swaps`), and owner `k`'s finish goes through the oracle, which decapsulates under the selected secret key. So the two runs are identical until the selected raw accept up to that replacement. This is not the coupling at another secret key that plan section 84 rejects: there owner `k`'s finishes would decapsulate under the other key; here every decapsulation of owner `k` before the stop is the oracle's.
- The relation of the coupling also carries the invariant that owner `k` holds the selected secret key in the real run (`HoldsX`); with it the real finish and the oracle finish are related by the naturality of the native finish in its key pair (`PqxdhPhase2KemHandles.native_owner_run`), without a separate lemma that the finish decapsulates only with the owner's own key.
- The oracle finish answers every finish of owner `k` while the selected accept is not active, not only the first one made in `initSent`: a finish outside `initSent` makes no decapsulation request (`PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.terminal_native`, `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Publication.owner`), so this changes nothing and the guesser needs no stopping time. An oracle program cannot stop a simulation in the middle, so the guesser runs the whole game; the guess is frozen at the selected raw accept, and after it the guesser's steps, including owner `k`'s challenge finish, run on its own key pair and never on the selected secret key.
- Naming: the defined quantity is `ctGuessByCca`, not `ctGuessBy` as the brief wrote, because `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomaly.ctGuessBy` (published, frozen) is the guess without decapsulation access; `ctGuessByCca_ambient` shows that it is the special case of `ctGuessByCca` for guessers that make no decapsulation query, and `ctGuessByCca_le_pk` that `ctGuessByCca` is still at most `ctGuessPk`. The guesser outputs an optional ciphertext (none when the Server construction fails or the transcript holds no guess); the hit event is that its output is `some` of the encapsulation's ciphertext.
- `split_guess_cca` is an equality: on the split side, the transcript guess event has exactly the probability `ctGuessByCca` of the guesser. The inequalities come only from the anomaly's implication of the guess event and from `c1_split`'s join.
- Query accounting: `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations` is a static bound, one decapsulation query per finish query of the adversary at most (one per oracle finish, `nativeOwner_decapsulate`). The guesser makes no query to the base oracle (its ambient effects run on its own coins through the setup's fixed interpreter) and its uniform queries are those of its own draws and of the game; neither count is stated as a theorem here. Both are definite obligations of CE-12a in the existing cost model, from premises the CE chain already takes, not possible new contracts (open points, after the review).
- Two modules for `anomaly_le_pk`: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeySwap` (registered in this track) holds the program equalities of the replacement and the decapsulation count, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` the oracle finish, the guesser, the coupling and the bounds.
- Unit U8: the statements hold for every view of the outcome that erases the hidden-slot service state (`outcomeMap eraseService`), which keeps the Server's public data, the adversary's output, the ledger and every other coordinate of the final extended state (including the seed table of the split form). The key-first game runs over the lazy slot cache (`lazyService`), not over item 2's counted table, because the table's draw counter breaks the exact commutation with completions; `idealGame_lazy` is the step between them. A generic eager-sampling lemma (`game_eager`) serves both the ideal game and the split form. In the split form slot 0's material `(key32, nonce12)` is drawn early and its seed stays a world seed, drawn at its hidden install. `world_key_first` is not built (design-v2 section 10.2, S14).

## CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: names for consumers

- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser`: `ctGuessByCca`, `anomalyGuesser`, `anomaly_le_pk`, `anomaly_prefix_le_pk`, `guess_le_cca`, `split_guess_cca`, `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations`, `ctGuessByCca_le_pk`, `ctGuessByCca_ambient`, `oracleFinish`, `guessReg`, `interp`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeySwap`: `swapX`, `HoldsX`, `Swaps`, `split_swaps`, `withChosen`, `nativeOwner_decapsulate`.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeyFirst`: `idealGame_key_first`, `idealGame_key_last`, `idealGameKeyFirst`, `idealGameKeyLast`, `split_key_first`, `split_key_last`, `materialInit`, `game_eager`, `eraseService`, `idealGame_lazy`.

## CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: contracts used

No new assumption and no new contract. `anomaly_le_pk` and `anomaly_prefix_le_pk` take the approved codec contracts `PqxdhServerAuthNegativeControl.Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` (through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess.anomaly_guess` and `anomaly_prefix_guess`); `guess_le_cca` takes none. `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations` takes the budget premise `budget.Respects adversary`. Everything else is a program equality, a query count or an equality in law; the right-hand sides are the defined quantity `ctGuessByCca` at an explicit program.

## CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: open points

- CE-12a: the predict-then-compare IND-CCA adversary built from `anomalyGuesser` (its phase 1 runs the guesser with the phase-1 oracle and decapsulates the guess before the challenge; its phase 2 compares with the challenge), with the loss of plan section 84 (about 2·Adv^IND-CCA + δ_corr + 2^-256 under the existing assumption only).
- CE-12a obligation (definite; corrected after the review): CE-12a states the query caps of that adversary in the form `PqxdhKemIndCca.OneKeyAdversary.MakesAtMostQueries`, as the CE chain's KEM step already does (`PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.kem_transfer_bills`). Phase 2 makes no query; phase 1's caps are the guesser's plus the one decapsulation of the guess. (i) Logical decapsulations: at most `budget.finish + 1`, from `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations`. (ii) Base-oracle queries (`PqxdhKemIndCca.IsMlKemBaseQuery`): none. Every query of the guesser other than the oracle finish's decapsulation request is a coin of `ccaCoins`, inside which the base oracle is simulated by `kemAmbientImpl setup.baseImpl`, so the cap 0 follows from `PqxdhPrivateRootQueryBounds.ccaCoins_excluded` at `IsMlKemBaseQuery`, lifted through `oracleFinish`, `guessReg`, `flat` and `anomalyGuesser` exactly as `ccaCoins_decapsulate`, `oracleFinish_decapsulate`, `flat_decapsulate` and `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations` lift the decapsulation count; it needs no budget premise, since no step makes such a query. (iii) Uniform queries (`IsMlKemUniformQuery`): at most `(count + 1) · caps.keygenCost + (qInit + 32) + Σ_kind budget.cap kind · u kind`, counted from total query bounds of the guesser's probabilistic parts by `PqxdhPrivateRootQueryBounds.ccaCoins_uniform`. Here `(count + 1) · keygenCost` bills the guesser's own key pair and the `count` other pool entries (`PqxdhServerAuthSourceCosts.keygen_uniform`, with `keygenCost = keygenUniform + baseUniform · keygenBase` covering the simulated base work, and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvCost.sampleFamily_uniform`), `qInit + 32` bills the Server construction (`PqxdhLiveServerConstruction.construct_uniform` with no supplied seed), and `u kind`, a weight determined by `caps` and the fresh-message bounds, bills one step of class `kind` of the guesser game, summed over the budget by `FinitePartitionQueryBounds.weighted_bound` as in `anomalyGuesser_decapsulations`. The weights come from the existing per-command bills: the ideal environment's commands within `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.allowanceG (baseAllowance caps) ceFirstTouch` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvCost.idealStep_bound`, whose KEM terms `keygenCost`, `encapsCost` and `decapsCost` come from `PqxdhServerAuthSourceCosts.keygen_uniform`, `encaps_uniform` and `decaps_uniform`; the oracle finish draws at most what the real finish draws, its decapsulation being the oracle's), and the World's installs and the other classes as the real game's uniform bills charge them (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemPostBills.uniformCap` and `PqxdhRegistrationJointOpenUniform.total` in `kem_transfer_bills`). These bills give the weights' values, not the per-step bound itself (added after the confirming review): `idealStep_bound` bounds the ideal environment's step `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.idealStep`, which `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.ideal_splits` relates to the final split form only in law, while the guesser runs the split handler `PqxdhDeploymentBackend.handlerWith (PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer.idealSplit …)` (through `guessReg`, under `flat`). A per-step uniform bound for that handler is therefore itself a CE-12a lemma, proved from the same premises with the component bills (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvCost.acceptSelected_bound`, `finishChallenge_bound`, `createPool_uniform`, `lazyDummy_bound`, …); it needs no Lean change in this track. The premises are those that `kem_transfer_bills` already takes: `PqxdhServerAuthSourceCosts.KemBound setup caps`, the total query bound `qInit` of the runtime initializer (`initBound`) and `budget.Respects adversary`. Neither cap is a new contract or assumption, and the guesser needs no change for them.
- L2 (the Beacon ghost implies that owner `k` is established in the transcript) only if a consumer needs it, about 0.5-1; the slot of a designated handle at a prefix's time only if needed, about 0.25-0.5.
- Integration of this track (PROTOCOL section 6). The review of `c7f33970` and its fix pass are recorded in "CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: review fix pass", the confirming review of `47b32508` in "CE-11b unit U4 follow-up and unit U8: confirming review". These sections, the receipt and the docstrings of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeySwap` cite plan section 84 (the route), which this branch's committed plan snapshot lacked until the commit that records the confirming review (central `82b5fe43`'s snapshot has it, byte-identical to the working plan's). That commit refreshes the committed snapshot as a wholesale copy of the working plan through its newest section, 93: on this branch, whose snapshot was central `69a24703`'s through section 83, it adds sections 84 onward (85 to 87 included), with the pointer at the end of section 81 and the integration bullet of section 83; against central `82b5fe43`'s snapshot, through section 87, it adds section 87's integration bullet and sections 88 onward (corrected after the confirming review: this point first named sections 84 to 91 on this branch and 88 onward against `82b5fe43`). The integration merges `82b5fe43` (or a later central) first, keeps that snapshot and confirms that it contains section 84.

## CE-11b unit U7: what is proved

Unit U7 adds one module under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff`, with 23 guarded theorems and four definitions (`originMap`, `annotatedView`, `decisionOn`, `viewObserved`); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains one import). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound` (`originMap_two` only on `Quot.sound`), recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`. The main statement is `ideal_world`: under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, for every adversary that respects its budget, `𝒟[slotGame setup codec checks ensureInit none (decision setup.source.crypto codec setup.serverKeyId budget event claim g) (compileFull setup codec route bounds mode adversary) runtime budget.create (selectedKem budget g) g.1.val 2] {true}` equals `𝒟[(fun result => (PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.observed result).any (decisionOn setup codec budget event claim g)) <$> PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.run setup codec route (envGD setup codec checks (Target.ofCE budget g) ensureInit 0 runtime) setup.serverKeyId bounds mode adversary] {true}`: design-v2's `ideal_world_stmt` with `envGD` (the challenger's parameters drawn at the environment's startup, `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw.paramEnv`) in place of `bindStart`. The form that CE-12 composes with the record lifts is `ideal_world_view_slots`: the same left side is at most `𝒟[(fun view => decide ((viewObserved view).any (decisionOn setup codec budget event claim g) = true ∧ ∀ value, view.result = some value → IdealSlots setup.source.crypto codec setup.serverKeyId (Target.ofCE budget g) view.slots value.2.1.transcript)) <$> (chainView <$> realChains setup codec route (envGD …) setup.serverKeyId bounds mode adversary)] {true}` plus `𝒟[(fun result => decide (∃ value, result.1 = some value ∧ Anomaly setup.source.crypto codec setup.serverKeyId (Target.ofCE budget g) value.2.1.2.transcript)) <$> PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.run setup codec route (envGD …) setup.serverKeyId bounds mode adversary] {true}`. CE-12 bounds the second term by unit U4's `anomaly_le_pk` ("CE-11b unit U7: decisions and deviations"); `ideal_world_view_ct`, option (a)'s corollary, replaces it by `ctGuess setup`.

| Part | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The record lift's slot provenance | 10 | Generic in the environment and the slot count. `originMap` (the handle-to-slot map of a provenance annotation; `originMap_two`: at two slots it is `PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace.originSlots`) and `annotatedView` (the record lift's `PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal.View` read off an outcome of the provenance-annotated record world: output, ledger and environment state, the World's produced frames and raw-attempt counters, the annotation's handle-to-slot map; `annotatedView_analysis`: its analysis projection is `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.analysis` of the erased outcome). `handler_project` (one command of the all-real record compiler, projected by `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldCompiler.project` to the World state and the computed installation origins, is the annotated World command at the projected state, from every compiler state with `Good`: `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldCompiler.handler_erasure` for the World part and `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldProvenance.handler_origins` for the origins), `schedule_project` (every schedule), `run_origins` (`Prod.map id project <$> PqxdhRecordLiftWorldCompiler.run (realPolicy setup codec) … = PqxdhDeploymentIdealOrigins.annotatedRun …`, the annotation's extension of `run_erasure`). `chainView_split` (the view of a split compiler result is the annotated view of its projection: `PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal.handleSlot` of the record control is `originMap` of the computed origins), `protocol_view` (the view of `PqxdhRecordLiftProtocol.run` under the all-real policy has the law of the annotated view of `annotatedRun`, through `run_factor`), `realChains_view` (the same for `PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal.realChains`, through `PqxdhRecordLiftMixedWorld.policy_real`), `realChains_analysis_slots` (design-v2's form: the view's analysis projection and handle-to-slot map have the joint law of `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.analysis` and `originMap` on `annotatedRun`), `realChains_support` (every outcome of `realChains` has the view of an outcome of `annotatedRun`). |
| The per-target decision | 5 | `decisionOn` (design-v2 Appendix A: the event holds and the claim's budgeted designation is `g`); `decision_observe` (item 2's decision `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.decision` is `decisionOn` on the compiled program's observation, by definition); `ceDecide_observed` (the decision of `ideal_compile` on an outcome of CE's ideal game, `happens` and `designated` through `classicalOf` and `observeGame`, is `decisionOn` on the outcome's observed projection, false when no Server was constructed); `evalDist_le_add` (a Boolean observation that implies the disjunction of two others on the support is true with at most the sum of their probabilities); `viewObserved` (the observation of a view: output, ledger, produced frames) with `viewObserved_chainView` (it is `PqxdhRecordLiftProtocol.observed` of the run) and `viewObserved_annotated` (it is `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.observed` of the erased outcome). |
| The hand-off | 8 | `ideal_world` (from `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile.ideal_compile` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.c1_idealGame` mapped by the decision; the projection of `c1_idealGame` is all the decision reads, `ceDecide_observed`). On the provenance-annotated record world: `ideal_world_slots` (the equality with C5 restated conjoined, `IdealSlots` for `originSlots` of the annotation or `Anomaly`; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon.ideal_slots` holds on every outcome, and `annotatedRun_erase` relates the annotated and the plain record world), `ideal_world_slots_le` (at most the decision with `IdealSlots` plus the probability of `Anomaly` on the record world) and `ideal_world_slots_ct` (that probability bounded by `ctGuess`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound.anomaly_le`). On the record lift's view of the all-real chain world: `ideal_view_slots` (on every outcome of `realChains` with `envGD t`, the view's handle-to-slot map satisfies `IdealSlots` or the transcript has `Anomaly`; from `realChains_support` and `ideal_slots`), `ideal_world_view` (`ideal_world` on the view, through `PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal.realChains_game`), `ideal_world_view_slots` and `ideal_world_view_ct` (the bound forms on the view). |

## CE-11b unit U7: decisions and deviations

- `ideal_world` without `bindStart`: design-v2 section 9.1 states `ideal_world` from `ideal_compile`, `c1_ideal` and `run_bindStart`. Following the decision "`envGD` without `bindStart`" ("CE-11b increment 1: decisions and deviations") and the corrected open point 2 of "CE-11b unit U6, fourth part: open points", it is proved from `ideal_compile` and `c1_idealGame` (C1 for CE's ideal game at the observed projection; `c1_ideal` at `Target.ofCE budget g`, identity index 0), with no further lemma about the drawn parameters: `envGD` already draws them at its startup.
- `ideal_world_slots` in three forms. Design-v2 section 9.1 asks for "the same with `∧ IdealSlots`" and the bound `𝒟[D_g ∧ ¬IdealSlots] ≤ ctGuess setup`. With `∧ IdealSlots` alone the statement is not an equality (C5 restated fails on the anomaly), so the equality `ideal_world_slots` conjoins `IdealSlots ∨ Anomaly`, and the bound is stated as `ideal_world_slots_le` (decision with `IdealSlots`, plus the probability of `Anomaly` on the record world, which contains every outcome where the decision holds without `IdealSlots`) and `ideal_world_slots_ct` (with `anomaly_le`). The anomaly term of `ideal_world_slots_le` and `ideal_world_view_slots` is exactly the left side of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound.anomaly_le` (and of `anomaly_le_pk_sup`) at `Target.ofCE budget g`, identity index 0 and `setup.serverKeyId`, so every bound on that event composes by `add_le_add`. The bound CE-12 composes is unit U4's guesser bound `anomaly_le_pk` (track u4c on `codex/deployment-ce11b`, integrated into central at `66f425ef`, which this unit's integration merges), whose left side is the same expression at these arguments: by plan section 49, Q2, option (b), and section 84, CE-12a turns its guesser `anomalyGuesser` into a predict-then-compare IND-CCA reduction, and `ctGuess` does not appear as an unbounded headline term. `ideal_world_slots_ct` and `ideal_world_view_ct` are option (a)'s forms, kept as corollaries. The docstring of `ideal_world_slots_ct` ("in the form CE-12 composes") and the module docstring, which calls `ideal_world_view_slots` and `ideal_world_view_ct` "the bound forms that CE-12 composes with the record lifts", are to be read with this qualification: the `_ct` forms are the option (a) route. The review fix pass left both docstrings unchanged, so that the module stays byte-identical.
- `run_origins` at the compiler level. Design-v2 section 9.1 states `run_origins` on `PqxdhRecordLiftProtocol.run (realPolicy …)` as an extension of `run_factor` and `run_source` through `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldCompiler.origins` and `erase`. It is proved one level lower, as the extension of `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldCompiler.run_erasure` by the annotation (`project` is `erase` at the cached-seed interpretation, whose second component is `origins`), and lifted to the protocol (`protocol_view`, through `run_factor`) and to the all-real chain world (`realChains_view`); `realChains_analysis_slots` is the design's form. No new per-command proof was needed: `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldProvenance.handler_origins` (published by the items 4/5 track) already proves that the computed origins after every World command are the annotation of its reply, for every lookup policy; `handler_project` combines it with `handler_erasure`, which needs the compiler invariant `Good` (kept by `handler_good`, holding initially by `initial_good`).
- The view forms are integrated: `PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal` (the view, `realChains`, `chainView`, `realChains_game`) is on central, so design-v2's optional `ideal_world_view` is stated here (on `chainView <$> realChains`, the frozen record world by `realChains_game`), together with C5 restated on the view (`ideal_view_slots`) and the bound forms (`ideal_world_view_slots`, which CE-12 composes with `anomaly_le_pk`, and its option (a) corollary `ideal_world_view_ct`). Design-v2 section 7.4 needs C5 only on the real world; `ideal_view_slots` is that statement for the record lift's own handle-to-slot map.
- `originMap` is generic in the slot count (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealTrace.originSlots` is stated at two slots; `originMap_two`), so that `run_origins` and the view lemmas hold for every record world.
- `evalDist_le_add` is stated with `𝒟` only: VCVio's `probEvent` notation is deprecated, and its lemmas are used only inside the proof.
- One module: the generic part (record-lift objects and `annotatedRun`) and the CE-specific part are in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff`; its imports are `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound`, `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldProvenance` and `PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal`, all on central.
- Olean prefix: a fresh registered prefix `ce11b/u7/olean` (a resolved symlink mirror of the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `82b5fe43`, `ce11b/u6b/p4/int/olean`, without the root olean, 6339 links) receives the module's olean; the root was checked against it.
- Scratch: a temporary extract of this guide, written into the CE-11b scratch directory before the unit's own scratch directory `ce11b/u7` was registered, was moved into `ce11b/u7` after the registration. Nothing was deleted.
- Publication: one Lean commit (`f8730e3e`, with the root import, the inventory row and the check-inventory block; `inv recount` before `inv rehash`) and one documentation commit on `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut` over central `82b5fe43`, reviewed at `f23a204d` and followed by a review fix pass (one documentation commit, "CE-11b unit U7: review fix pass") and a commit that records its confirming review ("CE-11b unit U7: confirming review"); not integrated at that commit. The working plan records the unit in its section 90 (the fix pass in its section 92, the confirming review in its section 94) and the CE-11b design in its section 33.

## CE-11b unit U7: names for consumers

- The hand-off: `ideal_world`, `ideal_world_view`, `ideal_world_view_slots`, `ideal_world_view_ct`, `ideal_view_slots`; on the annotated world `ideal_world_slots`, `ideal_world_slots_le`, `ideal_world_slots_ct`. CE-12's route is `ideal_world_view_slots` with unit U4's `anomaly_le_pk`; the `_ct` forms are option (a)'s corollaries.
- The record lift's slot provenance: `run_origins`, `protocol_view`, `realChains_view`, `realChains_analysis_slots`, `realChains_support`, `chainView_split`, `handler_project`, `schedule_project`, `annotatedView`, `annotatedView_analysis`, `originMap`, `originMap_two`.
- The decision: `decisionOn`, `decision_observe`, `ceDecide_observed`, `viewObserved`, `viewObserved_chainView`, `viewObserved_annotated`; the tool `evalDist_le_add`.

## CE-11b unit U7: contracts used

The approved codec contracts `PqxdhServerAuthNegativeControl.Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, which the eight hand-off theorems take (through `ideal_compile`, `ideal_slots` and `anomaly_le`), and the adversary's budget (`budget.Respects`, through `ideal_compile`; `ideal_view_slots` is a support fact and does not take it). The generic part (`run_origins` and the view lemmas) and the decision lemmas take no contract or assumption premise. Their hypotheses are internal: `handler_project` and `schedule_project` assume the compiler invariant `Good` on the start state (`PqxdhRecordLiftWorldCompiler.Good setup.source state`), which `run_origins` discharges by `initial_good`; `evalDist_le_add` takes its implication hypothesis (on the support, the first observation implies the disjunction of the other two); `realChains_support` takes the outcome's membership in the support. No new contract and no new assumption.

The concurrent track on `codex/deployment-ce11b` (the anomaly guesser and unit U8) appends at the same anchors (the root import block, the inventory rows and module blocks, the end of the analysis, the guide's status paragraphs and section block, and the committed plan snapshot `doc/impl/common-deployment-experiment-plan.md`); whichever is integrated second merges the other first. (Added after the confirming review: this point first listed neither the plan snapshot among the anchors nor how to resolve it.)
2. For CE-12 (see "CE-11b: what is proved and what CE-12 consumes"): per target `g`, the step from item 2's two-slot world to the record lift's view of the all-real chain world with `envGD` is `ideal_world_view_slots`, with its anomaly term bounded by unit U4's `anomaly_le_pk` (plan section 49, Q2, option (b), and section 84; `ideal_world_view_ct` is option (a)'s corollary); the record lifts are then applied to the view event "decision and `IdealSlots`" with `Frame` from `PqxdhDeploymentIdealFrame.frame_idealD` and C4 from `c4_ideal`, and the final-world facts by the inclusions of design-v2 section 7.4, using `ideal_view_slots` on the real world only.
3. CE-11b's main line is complete with `anomaly_le_pk` (unit U4, track u4c on `codex/deployment-ce11b`), integrated into central at `66f425ef`, which this unit's integration merges (updated after track u4c's integration; this point first said that the main line is complete once `anomaly_le_pk` is integrated). This unit needs no change for it: its anomaly term is the left side of `anomaly_le_pk` at `Target.ofCE budget g`, identity index 0 and `setup.serverKeyId`, and `add_le_add` composes the two.

Figures in nominal agent-days. Design-v2 section 11 estimated U7 at 2-3; it took about 0.15, because `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldProvenance.handler_origins` already carried the per-command half of `run_origins` and `c1_idealGame` already carried C1 at the projection the decision reads. Unit U8 (the same track, also integrated) is outside the main line, and 11b-L (the LQ-F pieces, design-v2 section 10.1) stays deferred. The review fix pass took about 0.1.

## CE-11b unit U7: review fix pass

An independent review of `f23a204d` in two parts (semantic and hygiene) found no error in the Lean statements or proofs; both parts approved with nits. The semantic part recompiled the module against central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `82b5fe43` (exit 0, empty log), compared the composition with design-v2 section 9.1 as amended by sections 15 and 30, and checked, by `git show` of the concurrent branch at `c7f33970`, that the anomaly term of `ideal_world_slots_le` and `ideal_world_view_slots` is exactly the left side of `anomaly_le_pk` at `Target.ofCE budget g`, identity index 0 and `setup.serverKeyId`, in the same `open Classical in` context, so that `add_le_add` composes either bound. The hygiene part recompiled the committed module against the same `LEAN_PATH` (exit 0, empty log), scanned it (23 theorems with 23 exact guards in declaration order, within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; no `sorry`, `admit` or `axiom`), checked the inventory digests and counts at `82b5fe43`, `f8730e3e` and `f23a204d`, the receipt's hashes and lists, the identifiers in the added documentation, the footers and the registry, and ran its own focused `check-inventory` on the clean tree at `f23a204d` (exit 0). They raised five findings (one minor and four nits), all about documentation and records. This fix pass resolves them with one documentation commit. No Lean file, inventory row or check-inventory block changed, no theorem was added or removed, and the 23 theorems keep their exact guards.

- Minor (semantic): the documentation put `anomaly_le_pk` outside CE-11b's main line and offered `ctGuess` as an anomaly route for CE-12 (the status paragraph, the re-estimate, the closing section "CE-11b: what is proved and what CE-12 consumes", the analysis, design-v2 section 33 and working plan section 90). The recorded decisions say the opposite: plan section 49, Q2, chose option (b), by which CE-12a bounds the anomaly's probability by the predict-then-compare IND-CCA reduction and `ctGuess` does not appear as an unbounded headline term; plan section 75 and design-v2 section 31 put `anomaly_le_pk` into unit U4, plan section 84 fixes its route, and plan section 88 lists it among CE-11b's remaining work (wording corrected after the confirming review: this bullet first said that plan sections 75 and 84 put it into unit U4). No Lean statement is wrong. The status paragraph, the decisions, the names for consumers, the open points (new point 3), the re-estimate and the closing section now say that CE-11b's main line is complete once track u4c's `anomaly_le_pk` (unit U4) is integrated, that CE-12 composes `ideal_world_view_slots` with `anomaly_le_pk` for the predict-then-compare IND-CCA reduction, and that `ideal_world_view_ct` and `ideal_world_slots_ct` are option (a)'s forms, kept as corollaries; the closing section's list of what is not part of the main line no longer names `anomaly_le_pk`. The analysis says the same in plain words. The docstrings of `ideal_world_slots_ct` and of the module, which speak of the forms "CE-12 composes", are left unchanged (changing them would need a recompile and a new inventory digest); the decisions bullet on `ideal_world_slots` says that the `_ct` forms are the option (a) route. - Nits (semantic and hygiene): the receipt's `contracts_used.premise_free` lists `handler_project`, `schedule_project` and `evalDist_le_add`, and "contracts used" said that the generic part and the decision lemmas "take no premise", although `handler_project` and `schedule_project` assume the compiler invariant `Good` on the start state (`run_origins` discharges it by `initial_good`) and `evalDist_le_add` takes its implication hypothesis. This commit changes no Lean file, inventory row or check. It records the review in this section, the status paragraph, the open points, the checks, the analysis's Review paragraph, the receipt (`scope`, `review.status`, `review.docs_fix_commit`, `review.confirming_review`, the plan record, a new `plan_snapshot` record, the commits and notes) and the committed plan snapshot, refreshed as a wholesale copy of the working plan through its new section 94: on this branch, whose snapshot was the fix pass's through section 92, it adds the clause added to section 89 after track u4c's confirming review, section 93 with its integration bullet and section 94; against central `66f425ef`'s snapshot, through section 93, it adds section 93's integration bullet and section 94. The integration merges central `66f425ef` (or a later central) first, central's side first at every anchor, takes this branch's plan snapshot, then recounts the inventory (1884 handwritten Lean files after merging `66f425ef`) and rehashes it, and runs its checks on the merged topic tip. Once integrated, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff` is published and is not edited again.

## CE-11b: what is proved and what CE-12 consumes

CE-11b's main line (design-v2, units U1 to U7) is complete: unit U4's `anomaly_le_pk` (track u4c on `codex/deployment-ce11b`) is integrated into central at `66f425ef`, which this unit's integration merges, and its other statements are proved on this branch and on central, under the approved `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` contracts and the adversary's budget, without a new assumption or contract. For one target `g = (i, k)` it proves:

- The ideal registration and `CE.idealGame g` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealSlots`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealParams`): the challenger draws the selected KEM key pair, the other owners' pool and the challenge ciphertext; the selected raw accept, the marked build and owner k's challenge finish are CE-native hidden steps.
- C7, the split and the seed deferral (unit U2): `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase.erase_ideal`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer.ideal_to_split`.
- The ideal environment (units U3 and U4): `envG` and `envGD`, C1 (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.c1_split`, `c1_ideal`, `c1_idealGame`), C3 (`c3_ideal`), C2a and C4 (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealFrame.frame_ideal`, `frame_idealD`, `c4_ideal`), admissibility and F4 (`env_admitted`, `hidden_server_once`, `hidden_beacon_once`, `seed_at_install`, `seed_at_install_split`), and C5 restated on the provenance-annotated record world (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon.ideal_slots`, `ideal_slots_prefix`) except on the Beacon-side anomaly, bounded by `ctGuess` and `ctGuessPk` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound.anomaly_le`, `anomaly_le_pk_sup`, and the prefix forms) and, in the form CE-12 consumes (plan section 49, Q2, option (b), and section 84), by the decapsulation-access guess `ctGuessByCca` of the explicit guesser `anomalyGuesser` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser.anomaly_le_pk`, `anomaly_prefix_le_pk`; track u4c, integrated into central at `66f425ef`).
- The second compile lemma (units U5 and U6): `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile.ideal_compile`.
- The hand-off (unit U7): `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff.ideal_world` and its view and slot forms, with `run_origins`.

CE-12 consumes, per target `g`: `ideal_world_view_slots` (item 2's two-slot world is at most the view event "the decision holds and the view's handle-to-slot map satisfies `IdealSlots`" on the all-real chain world with `envGD` at `Target.ofCE budget g`, plus the anomaly's probability) with unit U4's guesser bound `anomaly_le_pk` (plan section 49, Q2, option (b), and section 84: CE-12a turns `anomalyGuesser` into the predict-then-compare IND-CCA reduction of design-v2 section 28; the anomaly term of `ideal_world_view_slots` is the left side of `anomaly_le_pk` at `Target.ofCE budget g`, identity index 0 and `setup.serverKeyId`, so the two compose by `add_le_add`; `ideal_world_view_ct`, with `ctGuess` through `anomaly_le`, is option (a)'s corollary, and `ctGuess` is not a headline term); `frame_idealD` (the `Frame` of `envGD`, under `KemBound` and the initialization bound) and `c4_ideal` (C4) for the premises of the record lifts; and `ideal_view_slots` for the inclusions of the final-world events (design-v2 section 7.4). It supplies itself the steps before item 2's two-slot world (CE-9's or CE-9b's KEM transfer and T7 through `opaqueBranch_slots_two`, with their premises) and after the record lift (the final-world facts and the sum over targets). Not part of CE-11b's main line: 11b-L (the later-quantum pieces, deferred, design-v2 section 10.1); unit U8 (concurrent track u4c, integrated into central at `66f425ef`; no CE-12 headline consumes it); any security claim (CE-11b proves equalities in law, support facts, query bounds and bounds by defined quantities); the smallness of `ctGuess` for ML-KEM (not needed on CE-12's route).

## CE-12: what is proved

Every headline below holds for every setup, codec, checks, router, runtime initializer `ensureInit`, fresh-message bounds, observation mode (mode P for CE-APP and CE-LOR, every mode for the others), adversary respecting a budget `B` and runtime state, in the deployment experiment `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game setup codec checks route ensureInit 0 bounds mode adversary` (Server identity index 0, generated seed, `supplied = none`), under the premises of "CE-12: premises and contracts used". Events read only the experiment's observation `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.Observed` (the adversary's output, the ledger with the transcript, the test session and the fresh messages, and the produced frames). These are the experiment's own records in plan section 9's sense, not a public view: the transcript stores mode-O replies (raw-receive plaintexts a mode-P adversary does not see) and `produced` includes frames whose writer failed and that were never published (`PqxdhDeploymentInterface.Logs`: "analysis logs rather than public views"); no event reads roots, keys, partnering or the registration ghost. The reductions behind `kemLoss`, `slotLoss`, `authLoss` and `recLoss` evaluate the event and the claim (and, for CE-LOR, the filter `F` and the adversary's guess), so these advantages are small under the contracts only when those functions are efficiently computable. A target is `g = (i, k)` with `i < qAccept` and `k < qCreate`, read in CE-9's domain `Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)` through `Fin.castSucc`; the claim of an event names a handle, and `D_g` says that the event holds and the claim's budgeted public designation `designateIn` is `g`. Every summed form is the exact split of the event over the targets (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineChain.split_named`, from CE-9b's `split_create`): coefficient one per target, no selection factor, the column `k = qCreate` dropped because it is never designated. Every headline also has a per-target form (`*_target`, or `ce_kt` and `ce_kt_close`).

- CE-KT (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineKeyTransfer`; VC rows A1-root, S3-server, S3-beacon): for an arbitrary event and claim, the probability of `D_g` in the experiment and the probability of the same decision in CE's un-compiled ideal game of `g` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps.idealGame`, in which item 2's two hidden entries, the target session's 108 bytes and owner `k`'s challenge finish, are uniform values of a lazily sampled two-entry table keyed by the 128-byte DH coordinates through item 2's first-touch allocator: one shared value when owner `k`'s finish reads at the target session's DH coordinates (the honest pairing), independent values otherwise) differ by at most `kemLoss_g + slotLoss_g` (`ce_kt_close`; the one-sided `ce_kt` is the probe `kt_le`). Summed: the probability that the experiment ends with the event and the claim designated at all, and the sum over `g` of the ideal-game probabilities, differ by at most `Σ_g (kemLoss_g + slotLoss_g)` (`ce_kt_sum`; one-sided `ce_kt_sum_le`). World forms with the record world of the ideal environment `envGD` of the target in place of the ideal game: `ce_kt_world`, `ce_kt_world_close`, `ce_kt_world_sum`.
- CE-OR (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineOrigin`; L2-origin, b2s-C2, b2s-C2-chosen-fs, b2s-C4, far-b2s-C4 for b2s, every position from 1; S2b-origin, s2b-C1, s2b-C4, far-s2b-C4 for s2b, positions from 2 only): the origin failure `OriginFailure dir` is an accepted raw receive, at any point of the transcript, in the direction (`.b2s`: a Server raw receive, its receiving handle the Server peer chosen by the public header decoder; `.s2b`: a Beacon owner's raw receive) on a handle designated `g`, whose decoded frame (`codec.frameRead`) the handle's CE counterpart `cpart` (owner `k` for a Server peer; the Server peer built by the target's session, `peerOf`, for owner `k`) did not return before that receive. `ce_or_b2s`: `Pr_CE[OriginFailure .b2s] ≤ Σ_g (kemLoss_g + slotLoss_g + authLoss_g(dec_g ∧ ISs_g) + publicationTerm B)`, with no anomaly term. `ce_or_s2b`: `Pr_CE[OriginFailure .s2b] ≤ Σ_g (kemLoss_g + slotLoss_g + β_g + authLoss_g(dec_g ∧ IS_g) + publicationTerm B)`. The claim is the receiving handle of the first failing entry (`orClaim`). Scope by direction: b2s covers every Server raw receive (positions from 1); s2b covers every Beacon raw receive, that is positions from 2; position 1, the sequence-one record inside the Phase 2 response, is decrypted inside `finish_registration` (Rust `beacon.rs`), not by a raw receive, and is CE-A1's (S2b-initial), not CE-OR's.
- CE-UKS (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineUks`; b2s-UKSr): `ce_uks_b2s`: the probability of an accepted Server raw receive on a peer designated `g` of a decoded frame that some handle returned anywhere in the transcript, where the two handles are not the CE partners of `g` (owner `k` and the target's Server peer, in either order; `CEPartners`), is at most `Σ_g (kemLoss_g + slotLoss_g + authLoss_g(dec_g ∧ ISs_g) + bindingTerm BI_g)`, with no anomaly term. `ce_uks_sender`: the probability that an owner designated `g` returned a record whose decoded frame some raw receive accepted on a handle that is not the owner's CE partner is at most the same sum with `β_g` added and `IS_g` in place of `ISs_g`.
- CE-APP (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineApp`; App-b2s, App-s2b, b2s-S2, s2b-S1, rows 163-166), mode P: `ce_app`: the probability that the adversary's output claims index `i` and guess `m`, the `i`-th admitted fresh message was sent on a designated handle, and it equals `m` (`AppWin`) is at most `Σ_g (kemLoss_g + slotLoss_g + β_g + recLoss_g(dec_g ∧ IS_g) + 2^-min)`, with `min` the shortest admitted fresh-message length in bytes (`FreshBounds.min`). `ce_app_server`: the same for a fresh message on a Server peer (`AppWinPeer`), with no anomaly term and `ISs_g`.
- CE-LOR (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineLor`; SSX-b2s, SSX-s2b, SS-b2s, SS-s2b, row 167), mode P: for every filter `F`, any function of the mode-P transcript view (`List (PublicEntry .P)`; the reductions evaluate it, see above), the eligibility `LorElig` (a test session exists, `F` holds, and every admitted test entry's handle designates the target of the first admitted test entry, `firstTest`) and the win `LorWin` (eligible and the adversary's guess equals the test bit) satisfy `ofReal |Pr_CE[win] - ½·Pr_CE[eligible]| ≤ Σ_g (kemLoss_g(W) + slotLoss_g(W) + recLoss_g(dec_g(W) ∧ IS_g) + ½·(kemLoss_g(E) + slotLoss_g(E) + recLoss_g(dec_g(E) ∧ IS_g)) + ½·β_g)` (`ce_lor`), two transfers per target, one for the win `W` and one for the eligibility `E`. `ce_lor_server`: the same with every admitted test handle a Server peer (`PeerTests`), `ISs_g` and no anomaly term.

## CE-12: the terms of the bounds

- `kemLoss_g` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineChain.kemLoss`): one ML-KEM IND-CCA advantage of CE-9's certified raw-ordinal adversary `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemRawCertified.adversary` for the decision `d_g` of the event and claim, plus the ML-KEM correctness error `correctnessError setup` (a definition); its query bills are CE-9's `kem_transfer_bills`.
- `slotLoss_g` (`slotLoss`): item 2's stage S7 with stage T at two slots for `d_g`, `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransfer.allIdealLoss` at the caps transported from `B`: the R1 statistical term `(qAccept + qFinish + qHmac)/2^256`, two HMAC-PRF advantages and the two key-schedule bounds (Extract-on-uniform32 and HMAC-PRF advantages); bills `allIdeal_bills`, `prf_bills`.
- `β_g` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineAnomaly.beta`): one ML-KEM IND-CCA advantage of the predict-then-compare adversary `ptc` built from CE-11b's anomaly guesser of the target, plus the correctness error, plus `2^-256`. Its phase 1 makes at most `qFinish + 1` logical decapsulation queries and its phase 2 makes no query (`beta_decapsulations`, `ptc_post`); its uniform and base-query bills are unit P2's `ptc_bills`.
- `authLoss_g e` and `recLoss_g e` (`authLoss`, `recLoss`): the losses of the explicit reductions of `PqxdhRecordLiftComposition.record_auth_lift` (chain hops: Extract-on-uniform32 plus HMAC-PRF per hybrid index; integrity: IND$ validation, `qHash/2^128`, twice the attempt-indexed INT-CTXT sum and `qRaw/2^512` per target key) and of `PqxdhRecordLift.record_lift` (the same plus, in mode P, the IND$ privacy loss), at the ideal environment of the target with the frame of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealFrame.frame_idealD`, for the view event `e`, with zero aliasing and refusal terms and the bill input `BI_g = inputG caps qInit B bounds` (startup `qInit + 32 + (qCreate + 1)·keygenCost + encapsCost`, the first-touch allowance, two slots); the AEAD forgery fallback is a parameter. The bill conjuncts of `record_auth_lift` and `record_lift` at the frame of `frame_idealD` give the bills at `BI_g`; P1 does not restate them (`auth_close` and `records_close` use only the bound conjunct; `HeadlineBills`, P2).
- Final terms: `publicationTerm B = 2·R·S/2^512` (CE-OR), `bindingTerm BI_g = (R + S + H)·S/2^512` with `H = sharedHashTotal BI_g` (CE-UKS), `2^-min` per target with `min` in bytes (CE-APP; the frozen `FreshGuessStatement` term charges one bit per byte of the shortest admitted fresh message; the sum carries `qAccept·qCreate·2^-min`), none for CE-LOR (an exact half in the final world) and CE-KT (the ideal probability stays on the right).

## CE-12: premises and contracts used

- CE-KT: `FunctionalDecapsulation setup.kem`, `Phase1ReadBack codec`, `Phase2ReadBack codec`, `SourceFullCorrect setup.source setup.hmac` and `B.Respects adversary`.
- CE-OR, CE-UKS, CE-APP, CE-LOR: CE-KT's premises plus `KemBound setup caps` and the total query bound `qInit` of the runtime initializer (they give `Frame (envGD …)` through `frame_idealD`); CE-OR and CE-UKS also `FrameReadBack codec`. CE-OR does not take `DecoderAgreement route codec.frameRead`: the frozen `OriginStatement` takes it, but `origin_statement` does not use it, and CE-OR uses the conjunct's own theorem `PqxdhRecordFactsOriginClosure.idealAuth_unpublished_bound`, which needs only `FrameReadBack` and the frame (review fix pass).
- `β_g`: `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` (through `anomaly_le_pk`) and `FunctionalDecapsulation` (through `ptc_bound`).
- Contracts entering only as advantage terms: ML-KEM IND-CCA, HMAC-PRF, Extract-on-uniform32, R1 Extract-ROM (statistical term), modified-nonce AEAD IND$ and INT-CTXT, BLAKE2b random-oracle model (world model), functional AEAD correctness. No new assumption or contract.

## CE-12: decisions and deviations

- The per-target chain is restated in U7's names (design-v2 section 3): C1-C2 `chain_slot`/`chain_slot_close`, C1-C3 `chain_ideal`/`chain_ideal_close`, C1-C5 `chain_view`/`chain_close`, C6 `chain_slots` (with the record-world anomaly), `chain_beta` (with `β_g`), `chain_server` (Server claims, no anomaly), `anomaly_view`, `real_slots_server`, `view_unslotted` (design-v2's `E ∧ ¬IS ⊆ Anom`), C7 `auth_close`/`auth_le`/`records_close`/`records_le`. Design-v2's `slots_close` and `slots_server` are realized by `view_unslotted` with `chance_split` (in the CE-LOR module) and by `chain_server` and `server_view`; the C6 combination is done per headline rather than as one generic lemma.
- World forms of CE-KT (`ce_kt_world*`) are included although design-v2 section 3 dropped them as having no consumer, because the P1 task names them.
- Events are typed with `g : Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)`, the codomain of `designateIn`; the sums run over `Fin qAccept × Fin qCreate` with `castSucc`.
- CE-OR: design-v2's `dirOf route entry` is `Direction.Receives` on the receiving handle (a Server raw receive routes to a Server peer, an owner's raw receive to that owner). Claims are first-entry claims (`firstHandle`).
- CE-UKS: design-v2's generic `side` with a witness type is realized as two event families, the receiver side `UksReceiverAt` (Server peer receiver, the claim the receiving handle of the first witness entry) and the sender side `UksSenderAt` (owner sender, the claim the publishing handle of the first witness entry).
- CE-LOR: the per-target events are the existential events (`lorEligEvent`, `lorWinEvent`) with the claim `lorClaim = firstTest`; by `lorElig_target`, `D_g` of them is exactly `LorElig g` (respectively `LorWin g`), so the transferred decisions equal design-v2's `d_g(LorElig g, firstTest)` pointwise. The bound is stated as `ofReal |advantage| ≤ Σ …` in extended non-negative reals, which needs no finiteness side condition on the losses. The final-world fact is applied with the public eligibility `hidingOf (dec_g(E) ∧ IS_g)` (some view with the same public hiding projection satisfies it) and the projected guess `decisionHiding guess`; `decided_view` shows the eligibility factors through the projection. The Server-side form puts `PeerTests` into the events rather than into the filter.
- `ptc`'s guesser is typed over `kem.IND_CCA_oracleSpec` (definitionally CE-11b's `MlKemCCAOracleSpec`), so that program rewrites stay type-correct; two proofs in the anomaly module use `set_option backward.isDefEq.respectTransparency false`, as central's `PqxdhDeploymentKemTwoSided` does.
- Three proofs use `set_option linter.deprecated false` for VCVio's deprecated `probEvent` (the `Pr[…]` notation) inside their proofs: `ptc_random_le` and `ptc_correct_draws` (anomaly module) and `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineLor.chance_split`. Copies with the suppression removed report only `probEvent` warnings (no other deprecated name); every statement is in `𝒟` form.
- Commit `68498a95` corrects three documentation strings of `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineEvents` (nonexistent `LorEligPeer`/`LorWinPeer`, and "pre" for "prefix"); statements, proofs and guards are unchanged.

## CE-12: review fix pass

The P1 review (three independent lenses at `4365e748`) reported no hop mismatch and no double-counted or missing term; its findings were fixed as follows.

- CE-OR premise (composition and semantic lenses). `unpublished_final`, `ce_or_b2s_target`, `ce_or_b2s`, `ce_or_s2b_target` and `ce_or_s2b` took `agreement : RawCtxServerRouting.DecoderAgreement route codec.frameRead` only to instantiate the frozen `OriginStatement`, whose proof `origin_statement` binds it as `_agreement`. `unpublished_final` now applies `PqxdhRecordFactsOriginClosure.idealAuth_unpublished_bound` (the third conjunct of `origin_statement`) with the same environment, startup, allowance and frame `frame_idealD`, and the five theorems drop the binder. Before: the same conclusion under the hypotheses listed plus `agreement`; after: the same conclusion under those hypotheses without `agreement`. Every instance of the old statement is an instance of the new one, so the new statements are at least as strong. Guards unchanged (full three axioms).
- CE-KT documentation (semantic lens): the ideal game's two hidden entries are values of a lazily sampled two-entry table keyed by the DH coordinates, shared on the honest pairing, not independent values (module documentation, the CE-KT item above and the analysis).
- CE-OR scope (semantic lens): s2b covers positions from 2; position 1 is CE-A1's (module documentation, the CE-OR item above and the analysis).
- Wording (semantic lens): the events read the experiment's records, not a public view; `F` is any function of the mode-P transcript view and the reductions evaluate events, filters and guesses; the bill conjuncts at `BI_g` are not restated in P1; `min` is in bytes; the CE-APP sum carries `qAccept·qCreate·2^-min`; "CE-12: not claimed" names the later-mode, pin and single-test limits.
- Hygiene lens: `ite_false` replaces the deprecated core lemma `if_false` in `ptc_random_le`; the deprecation suppressions on `ptc_real_draws` and `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineContain.chance_mono`, which suppressed nothing, are removed; the remaining three are disclosed under "CE-12: decisions and deviations".
- No change: the confirmations of the semantic and composition lenses (statements against design-v2, containments, counterpart sets, the exact split, the anomaly reduction, the LOR arithmetic, hygiene counts).

## CE-12: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass (Lean commit `6d13ef8b` and documentation commit `bc86ef4d`) at `bc86ef4d` found every finding of the P1 review at `4365e748` resolved ("CE-12: review fix pass"); none needs further action. It recompiled the four modules the fix pass changed (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineAnomaly`, `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineContain`, `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineKeyTransfer`, `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineOrigin`) without `-o` through the coordination wrapper at the clean head against the recheck prefix `ce12/p1/olean2`, and `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineOrigin` and `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineLor` again against the fix-pass prefix `ce12/p1/olean3` (exit 0, empty logs). Its guard scan of the nine modules finds 116 theorems with 116 exact guards in declaration order, every axiom list within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; its receipt check finds the receipt's hashes of the root, `reviewed-inventory.txt` and `check-inventory.sh`, its commit list, and each module's source hash, theorem list, guard count, axiom profile, imports, last commit and fix-pass recheck record in agreement with the head (both scripts were rerun at `bc86ef4d` for this record: every check passes).

- Nit (optional, analysis): the CE-KT paragraph said that the ideal game's two-entry table is keyed by "their Diffie-Hellman inputs" and that the honest pairing "uses the same Diffie-Hellman inputs", but the table's coordinates are the 128 bytes of Diffie-Hellman outputs that feed the key schedule (item 2's first-touch allocator), as "CE-12: what is proved" and the module documentation say. The paragraph now says "keyed by the 128 bytes of Diffie-Hellman outputs that feed the key schedule" and "reads the same Diffie-Hellman outputs"; the analysis's opening paragraph records the confirming review.

This commit changes no Lean file, inventory row or check. It records the review in this section, the status paragraph, open point 1, the checks, the analysis and the receipt (`scope`, `review.status`, `review.confirming_review`, `review.docs_fix_commit`, the plan record, a new `plan_snapshot` record, `open_points`, the commits and notes), and refreshes the committed plan snapshot `doc/impl/common-deployment-experiment-plan.md` as a wholesale copy of the working plan through its new section 100: against central `ab9986ef`'s snapshot (through section 94), which this branch carried unchanged until now, it adds section 94's integration bullet and sections 95 to 100 (95 CE-11b's main line complete, 96 the CE-12 design adopted, 97 the LQ-F upstream design adopted, 98 P1 built, 99 its review fix pass, 100 its confirming review). The integration merges central if it has moved since `ab9986ef`, central's side first at every anchor (taking this branch's plan snapshot, the newer wholesale copy, if central changed it), recounts the inventory and rehashes it, and runs its checks on the topic tip into a fresh prefix. Once integrated, the nine modules are published and are not edited again.

## CE-12: names for consumers

`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineChain`: `kemLoss`, `slotLoss`, `startupG`, `inputG`, `authLoss`, `recLoss`, `ServerSlots`, `slotted`, `serverSlotted`, `split_named`, `chain_close`, `chain_server`, `view_unslotted`, `auth_le`, `records_close`. `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineAnomaly`: `ptc`, `ptc_bound`, `beta`, `anomaly_beta`, `chain_beta`, `beta_decapsulations`. `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineEvents`: `Direction`, `cpart`, `ForgedAtCE`, `OriginFailure`, `originEvent`, `orClaim`, `CEPartners`, `UksReceiverAt`, `uksReceiverEvent`, `uksReceiverClaim`, `UksSenderAt`, `uksSenderEvent`, `uksSenderClaim`, `AppWin`, `AppWinPeer`, `appEvent`, `appPeerEvent`, `appHandle`, `LorElig`, `LorWin`, `PeerTests`, `lorEligEvent`, `lorWinEvent`, `lorEligPeerEvent`, `lorWinPeerEvent`, `lorClaim`. Headlines: `ce_kt`, `ce_kt_close`, `ce_kt_sum`, `ce_kt_world_sum`; `ce_or_b2s`, `ce_or_s2b`; `ce_uks_b2s`, `ce_uks_sender`; `ce_app`, `ce_app_server`; `ce_lor`, `ce_lor_server`; each with its `*_target` form.

## CE-12: not claimed

- No symbolic-eligibility corollary (design-v2 section 7, unit P2): the events use the public designation, not the ProVerif-shaped `SymServer`/`SymBeacon` eligibility, and no gap, identity-collision or ciphertext-collision term is proved.
- No bills of the headline reductions beyond those of the existing theorems and `ptc`'s decapsulation count (unit P2: `ptc_bills`, `splitStep_uniform`, `HeadlineBills`).
- No later-quantum headline (unit P3). P3 (CE-12-L) is to cover only CE-KT, CE-APP and CE-LOR in the later mode (plan section 19, design.md section 9.1): no later-mode origin, unknown-key-share or authentication headline is planned (the later mode claims confidentiality only; design.md section 9.1 at most carries the classical CE-OR and CE-UKS bounds over to run-phase events of the later game).
- Owner-side designation needs the Beacon's pin to be the experiment's Server identity and key ID `(S, serverKid)` (`designate` requires `binding.2 = serverKid` and a linked build response whose Server identity is the pin); Beacons pinned elsewhere are never designated, and nothing is claimed about them (design-v2 section 15).
- CE-LOR concerns the experiment's single test session (one hidden bit); no multi-session left-or-right claim.
- CE-KT is a transfer to the ideal game, not secrecy of the root (`attacker(root)` stays partial, plan section 19).
- No plaintext-linked UKS, no b2s publisher pin or mode-O plaintext agreement (design-v2 section 15, AG not built), no `INITIAL_SECRET` endpoint, and no claim about raw-byte frames: the origin and UKS events compare decoded frames.
- The CE-LOR advantage is eligibility-weighted: runs whose admitted tests do not all designate the first test's target, or whose first test is not designated, are in neither the win nor the eligibility event.

## CE-12: open points and the rest of CE-12

1. P1 review (semantic, composition and hygiene lenses) and its fix pass: done ("CE-12: review fix pass"); confirming review at `bc86ef4d`: done ("CE-12: confirming review"). Next the integration into central (PROTOCOL section 6), which merges central if it has moved since `ab9986ef` and recompiles into a fresh prefix.
