## CE-12: checks

Every module was compiled with `-o` into the fresh prefix `ce12/p1/olean` (a resolved symlink mirror of the first prefix of the published `ab9986ef` `LEAN_PATH`) through `coord.sh lean claude` (Lean 4.34, `-j1 -M8000`, timeout 180): exit 0 with empty logs, and the root import after each module exit 0 with an empty log; after the documentation-string correction all nine modules and the root were recompiled at `68498a95` into a second fresh prefix `ce12/p1/olean2` (exit 0, empty logs; every module olean byte-identical to its first final except the events module, whose documentation strings changed). Guards: 116 theorems with 116 exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms` in declaration order, every axiom list within `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; no `sorry`, `admit`, custom axiom or disabled gate; no import of `PqxdhBoundary*` (transitive closure checked). Inventory: `coord.sh inv recount` before `coord.sh inv rehash` at every module (handwritten Lean files 1884 to 1893); a focused `check-inventory` under `@nix` after every module (exit 0; the first run for the events module failed only because the not yet finalized sources of later modules were in the working tree, and the rerun with them moved to scratch passed) and at the clean head `68498a95` (exit 0, clean before and after); `git diff --check` on every commit. Review fix pass: development checks of the changed modules, then all nine modules and the root recompiled with `-o` at the clean Lean fix commit `6d13ef8b` into a third fresh prefix `ce12/p1/olean3` (exit 0, empty logs; the module oleans differ from `olean2` exactly for the four changed modules: anomaly, key transfer, containments, origin); a copy of the anomaly module with its deprecation suppressions removed reports only `probEvent` warnings; `coord.sh inv recount` (1893, unchanged) and `inv rehash` of the four rows; focused `check-inventory` at `6d13ef8b` (exit 0, clean before and after); `git diff --check` on both commits. Confirming review: the four changed modules recompiled without `-o` at the clean `bc86ef4d` against `ce12/p1/leanpath2`, and the origin and left-or-right modules against `ce12/p1/leanpath3` (exit 0, empty logs); guard scan and receipt check as in "CE-12: confirming review"; a focused `check-inventory` (`@nix`) on the clean tree right after the fix pass's documentation commit `bc86ef4d` passed (exit 0, clean before and after; receipt `checks.inventories.fix-final-head`). The commit that records it changes no Lean file, inventory row or check; `git diff --check` is clean. The receipt is `doc/impl/proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce12-p1.json`.

## CE-12 P2: the D-ct spike

The task scheduled the 0.5-day spike of design-v2 section 8 first. The statement of `ctgap_world` was compiled at central `b88e3012` as a proposition in a scratch probe (`ce12/p2/spike/CtGapWorld.lean`, `CtGapSpike.CtGapWorld`, exit 0): for a target `g`, the probability over the record world with the ideal environment of `g` (`PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.run … (PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.envGD … (Target.ofCE budget g) ensureInit 0 runtime) …`) that the observation satisfies `CtGapEvent` with the claim `ctClaim` (the Server peer built by the smallest session with a ciphertext gap) designating `g` is at most `ctStat = (qHmac + qAccept + qFinish)/2^256 + H/2^512`, with `H` the BLAKE2b cap. None of the following is on central, and each was estimated in actual days:

- (1) A World invariant that ties the marked build's sequence-one record and commitment to the cached slot material `(key32, nonce12)`. The marked build is `buildCachedEnv`, then `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.buildSplitEnv` and `buildSplitFwd` through `PqxdhServerAuthLoggedSendFactor.factored` and `PqxdhServerAuthCtxMaterial.factor (joinOutput material dummySeed)`, with its BLAKE2b request through the World's `.hash` capability; the invariant must hold across all five branches of `idealStep` and the World's hash cache (1-2 days).
- (2) A Lean lemma for the per-entry `2^-256` step. The step itself is a valid statistical fact, by averaging over the uniform marginal of `key32_0`. `key32_0` is a uniform lazy draw (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealKernel.lazyDummy` queries the material cache), and the Beacon's sequence-one key is a fixed function `f` of the R1 entry, the root: in `concrete.rs:64-106`, `start_initial_ratchet_kdf(root, initialization)` builds its request from the root alone (`SymmetricRatchetKdfRequest::new(*root)`), with no transcript input. For an entry drawn before `key32_0`, `f(entry)` is a fixed value, which a fresh uniform `key32_0` equals with probability `2^-256`. An entry `e` drawn after `key32_0` is a fresh uniform draw, so `Pr[f(e) = key32_0 ∧ e is created] ≤ E_k[Pr_e[f(e) = k]] = 2^-256`, using only that `key32_0` is uniform. Adaptivity therefore costs nothing; no independence of `key32_0` from the adversary's view is needed (it is not independent: it seals the sequence-one record inside the Phase 2 response and enters a BLAKE2b commitment, so `key32_0` cannot be drawn later), no random-oracle or uniformity property of HKDF-Expand is needed, and no hybrid. What is missing is proof engineering: a lemma for this averaged union bound over the World's dynamic R1 table (each entry a fresh uniform draw, at most `qHmac` of them, against one uniform draw that the run may expose), which no Lean tool on central provides for the stateful World (1-2 days).
- (3) `PqxdhRecordFactsCollision`'s counting tools (`run_bound`, `ctxDigest_bound` over a `DrawSpec` client) are generic; its documentation says that their connection to the record interpreters is a separate obligation (0.5-1 day).
- (4) The case `c = k*` goes through `finishChallengeEnv`, `preparationCE` with `Shared.known` and `groupEnv`'s known-root path (`markedRoot setup.hmac 0^32`), another instance of (1) and (2) (0.5-1 day).

The remaining estimate is 3-6 actual days. Items (1), (3) and (4) alone come to 2-4 days, above the stop rule's 1.5 days, so the stop rule fired. D-ct is deferred. The Beacon-side and mixed-side terms of P2 carry the explicit public term `ctGapTerm`, the probability in the experiment of `CtGapEvent` (F-ct2, design-v2 section 8). This is design-v2 section 14's contingency note 4. It is weaker than plan section 9's Beacon-side corollary, and the owner is to be told before publication (open point 1). The design is not affected: its closed-form argument for `ctStat` is sound, and no new idealization is needed to close `ε_ct`. (This section first said that the per-entry step was not a statistical fact and needed a random-oracle hybrid with a 2-4-day item (2) and a 4-8-day total; the review showed the averaging argument above, and a review note's alternative, modelling Expand as a fresh random-oracle output or adding a computational hop, is not needed either.)

## CE-12 P2: what is proved

The setting is the same as for P1 ("CE-12: what is proved"): every statement holds for every setup, codec, checks, router, runtime initializer, fresh-message bounds, observation mode (mode P for CE-APP and CE-LOR), budget-respecting adversary and runtime state, in `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game setup codec checks route ensureInit 0 bounds mode adversary`, under the premises of "CE-12 P2: premises and contracts used". The symbolic events read the outcome: the final transcript, the final native identity keys (`PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple.ownerIdentities`) and the constructed Server's identity (`protectedBinding`). They are never fed through the per-target chain.

- Symbolic eligibility (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineGap`). `SymServer` holds for Server peer `kid` when the session that built it has accept bytes whose candidate the source attached verification accepts with the identity key of an owner `c` among the final identity keys, and `c` made no rejected bundle call in Fresh (ProVerif's `L2SelectedIdentity`). `SymBeacon` holds for owner `c` when it is Established, was created with the binding (constructed Server's identity, protected key ID) and made no rejected bundle call in Fresh (`S2bSelectedIdentity` on `(S, serverKid)` pins).
- Ciphertext gap (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineGap`, public). `CtGap c j` holds when owner `c` accepted, session `j` matches it with its created pin before its acceptance (CE-A1's `PinnedMatchAt`), and the ciphertext read with the Phase 2 reader from `c`'s first successful finish input differs from the one read from `j`'s first successful build response. `CtGapEvent` is `∃ c j, CtGap c j`, and `ctGapTerm` is its probability in the experiment.
- A new run invariant (`ready_game`): every successful finish of the transcript happens while the running summary has its owner created and in InitSent. This is the native fact that `finish_registration` (`beacon.rs`) rejects outside `InitSent`, from `PqxdhDeploymentCompileFootprint.finish_owner` through `PqxdhDeploymentEligibility.Agree`. Hence `finish_accepted`: an owner's first successful finish is its accepting finish, and conversely. Positional sessions: a session handle that an accept returned is below the number of accept attempts, `acceptOf` reads that accept (`accept_positional`), and the session is the reply of that accept ordinal (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineSymbolic.sessionAt_positional`).
- `server_gap`: on every outcome, a `SymServer` peer that is not designated inside the budget witnesses a published-tuple forgery (`OwnerForgery`) at the owner whose identity key the accepted candidate carries, and that owner has no rejected bundle call in Fresh. `beacon_gap`: a `SymBeacon` owner that is not designated inside the budget is a CE-A1 failure (`A1NoPinnedMatch`) or has a ciphertext gap `CtGap c j`. Premises: `Phase1ReadBack`, `Phase2ReadBack` and the budget.
- Gap bounds. `server_gap_bound`: `𝒟[ServerGap] ≤ ε_A2pub`. `beacon_gap_le`: `𝒟[BeaconGap] ≤ 𝒟[∃ c, A1NoPinnedMatch c] + ctGapTerm`. `ce_a1_linked`: `𝒟[BeaconGap] ≤ ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`, with the bills of every CE-A1 reduction; this is CE-A1 with a matching that carries the ciphertext, the pin and the PQ key, because an owner's designation is its smallest linked session. `sym_gap`: `𝒟[ServerGap ∨ BeaconGap] ≤ ε_A2pub + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`.
- Collision terms (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineCollision`). `identity_collision_game`: the probability that two distinct created owners have equal identity public keys is at most `ε_inj`. `pair_cp_le`: `CP ≤ ofReal Adv^IND-CCA(pairAdversary) + 2·ofReal δ_corr + 2^-256`, where `CP` (`pairCollision`) is the probability that two independent encapsulations to a freshly generated ML-KEM key have equal ciphertexts and `pairAdversary` is the fixed encapsulate-and-compare adversary. It makes no pre-challenge query (`pair_pre`) and no decapsulation query after the challenge (`pair_post_decapsulations`). `CtColl` (`CtCollAt k i j`, public): two sessions `i < j` carry one ciphertext (read from their first successful build responses) to the PQ key of owner `k`'s first published bundle. `ctCollTerm` is its probability in the experiment.
- Symbolic events (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineSymbolic`), with identity-based counterpart sets. `SymCpartB2s` (b2s): the owners whose first published bundle carries the identity field of the accepted candidate of the session that built the peer. `SymCpartS2b` (s2b): the Server peers built by a session whose accepted candidate verifies to the owner's identity key. The events: `OriginFailureSym dir` (an accepted raw receive in the direction on a symbolically eligible handle whose decoded frame no symbolic counterpart published before); `UksFailureSym` (an owner with `SymBeacon` returned a frame that a raw receive accepted on a handle outside its symbolic counterparts, the sender-eligible UKSr); `AppWinSym`, `AppWinPeerSym` and `AppWinOwnerSym` (CE-APP's win on a symbolically eligible handle, on a Server peer with `SymServer`, on an owner with `SymBeacon`); `KtSym`, `KtPeerSym` and `KtOwnerSym` (an event whose claim is symbolically eligible, a Server peer with `SymServer`, an owner with `SymBeacon`).
- Containments, on every outcome. `origin_b2s_sym`: a symbolic b2s failure is a CE b2s failure or a Server-side gap, with no term, because a designated peer's CE counterpart (owner `k`) is a symbolic counterpart (`peer_cpart_sym`). `origin_s2b_sym` and `uks_sym`: the symbolic s2b failure (respectively UKS) is the CE event, a CE-A1 failure, a `CtGapEvent` or a `CtCollEvent`. The owner's pinned match `j` is linked unless `CtGap`, the designated session (the smallest linked one) is `j` unless `CtColl`, and `j`'s accepted keys are the owner's published keys, identity included, so the CE counterpart is a symbolic counterpart (`owner_cpart_sym`). `app_sym` and `kt_sym`: the CE event or a gap of either side. `app_peer_sym` and `kt_peer_sym`: the CE event or a Server-side gap. `app_owner_sym` and `kt_owner_sym`: the CE event or a Beacon-side gap.
- Symbolic corollaries with the CE-12 bounds, Server side. `ce_or_b2s_sym` (rows L2-origin, b2s-C2, b2s-C2-chosen-fs, b2s-C4, far-b2s-C4): `𝒟[OriginFailureSym .b2s] ≤ ce_or_b2s's bound + ε_A2pub`. `ce_app_server_sym` (App-s2b, s2b-S1; mode P): `𝒟[AppWinPeerSym] ≤ ce_app_server's bound + ε_A2pub`. `ce_kt_server_sym` (S3-server, and A1-root with a Server-peer claim): `𝒟[KtPeerSym] ≤ ce_kt_sum_le's bound + ε_A2pub`, one-sided, with no CE-A1 premise. None of them carries `ε_A1`, `ctGapTerm` or `ctCollTerm`.
- Symbolic corollaries with the CE-12 bounds, Beacon side. `ce_or_s2b_sym` (S2b-origin, s2b-C1, s2b-C4, far-s2b-C4; positions from 2): `≤ ce_or_s2b's bound + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm + ctCollTerm`. `ce_uks_sym` (b2s-UKSr, sender-eligible): `≤ ce_uks_sender's bound + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm + ctCollTerm`. `ce_app_beacon_sym` (App-b2s, b2s-S2; mode P): `𝒟[AppWinOwnerSym] ≤ ce_app's bound + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`. `ce_kt_beacon_sym` (S3-beacon, and A1-root with an owner claim): `𝒟[KtOwnerSym] ≤ ce_kt_sum_le's bound + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`, one-sided. These are design-v2 section 12's Beacon-side forms with `ctGapTerm` for `ε_ct` and `ctCollTerm` for `ε_ctcoll`.
- Symbolic corollaries with the CE-12 bounds, either side of the claim. `ce_app_sym`: `𝒟[AppWinSym] ≤ ce_app's bound + ε_A2pub + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`. `ce_kt_sym`: `𝒟[KtSym] ≤ ce_kt_sum_le's bound + ε_A2pub + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`. They are for claims whose side is not fixed in advance; a claim known to be on one side uses that side's form. Each corollary of the three groups also has its event form (`*_sym_le`): the symbolic event is at most the CE event plus the gap and collision events.
- `symbolic_lor` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineSymbolicLor`; rows SSX-b2s, SS-b2s, 167, mode P). Symbolic eligibility `LorEligSym` (`e_sym`): a test session exists, the run has no rejected bundle call in Fresh, and every admitted test handle is symbolically eligible. `LorWinSym` adds a correct guess of the test bit. CE-LOR's eligibility is taken with the public filter `nbfFilter` (NoBundleFailure on the mode-P view, `nbfFilter_iff`). `lor_inclusion`: CE eligibility implies symbolic eligibility on every outcome (`designated_sym`: a designated handle on a NoBundleFailure run is symbolically eligible). `lor_difference`: symbolic eligibility without CE eligibility is a gap, an identity collision or a ciphertext collision. Admitted tests share the first test's session at admission (`SameSession`), the ledger's test session starts at the first admissible test (`ledgerInv_game`), and a prefix designation moves only when a smaller owner later publishes the same identity field (`peer_moves`, `field_collision`) or a smaller session later becomes linked (`owner_moves`). `symbolic_lor`: `ofReal |adv_sym − adv_CE(nbfFilter)| ≤ 2⁻¹·(ε_A2pub + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm + ε_inj + ctCollTerm)`, where `adv = Pr[win] − ½Pr[eligible]`. `symbolic_lor_bound`: `ofReal |adv_sym| ≤ ce_lor's bound at nbfFilter + 2⁻¹·(…)`.
- `symbolic_lor_server` (rows SSX-s2b, SS-s2b, mode P): the same with every admitted test handle a Server peer (`LorEligPeerSym`, `LorWinPeerSym`: `e_sym` with CE-LOR's `PeerTests`) against `ce_lor_server`'s events (`lorEligPeerEvent`, `lorWinPeerEvent`) at `nbfFilter`. `lor_difference_peer`: the difference is a Server-side gap or an identity collision; the Beacon-side gap and the ciphertext collision cannot arise, because every admitted test handle is a Server peer, an undesignated admitted peer is `ServerGap`, and a peer's designation moves only by `SameField`. `symbolic_lor_server`: `ofReal |adv_sym_peer − adv_CE_peer(nbfFilter)| ≤ 2⁻¹·(ε_A2pub + ε_inj)`. `symbolic_lor_server_bound`: `ofReal |adv_sym_peer| ≤ ce_lor_server's bound at nbfFilter + 2⁻¹·(ε_A2pub + ε_inj)`, with no `β_g`, no `ε_A1` and no explicit term.
- Injective forms (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineInjective`; the injective L2-origin and S2b-origin rows). The theorems bound the following named failure modes of injectivity. For b2s: a registration replayed under one Beacon identity (CE-R1', `ReplayOwnerEvent`: two successful accepts at distinct positions under the identity key of an owner without a rejected bundle call in Fresh), and a Server peer accepting a sequence number twice (CE-C3, `PeerReplay`). For s2b: two Beacons matched to one session (`SharedMatch`), two distinct owners with equal identity public keys (`IdentityCollision`), and an owner accepting a sequence number twice (`OwnerReplay`). Record replay never happens (`replay_never`, from `record_replay_game`). That these modes are all the ways an injective correspondence of ProVerif can fail in the experiment is argued in prose, not proved: two Server accepts (peers `p1 ≠ p2`) of one Beacon send have that Beacon among both peers' symbolic counterparts, so both sessions accepted candidates under its identity key, which is `ReplayOwnerEvent`; two Beacon accepts (owners `c1 ≠ c2`) of one Server send by peer `p` have `p` among both owners' symbolic counterparts, and `p` is one session because key IDs are unique (`server.rs:434-451`: `server_next_key_id` with the `known_ids` check), so that session's accepted candidate verifies to both identity keys, which is `IdentityCollision`. `ce_or_injective_b2s`: the probability of a symbolic b2s origin failure, a registration replay or a peer record replay is at most `ce_or_b2s`'s bound + `ε_A2pub`, because the Server-side gap and the replay are both published-tuple forgeries at an owner (`server_gap`, `replay_owner_published`). `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`: the probability of a symbolic s2b origin failure, a shared match, an identity collision or an owner record replay is at most `ce_or_s2b`'s bound + `ofReal ε_A1inj` (`ce_a1_injective`'s right side, `ε_A1 + ε_inj` as reals) + `ctGapTerm` + `ctCollTerm`. A shared match is an identity collision (`shared_collision`) and `ε_inj` bounds the identity collision (`identity_collision_game`), so the identity collision adds nothing to the bound. `ce_or_injective_s2b` is the same bound for the event without the identity collision; the full form implies it. Event forms: `injective_b2s_le`, `injective_s2b_le`, `injective_s2b_full_le`.
- Bills (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineBills`). `pair_bills`: `pairAdversary.MakesAtMostQueries 0 0 0 caps.encapsUniform caps.encapsBase 0`, that is, after the challenge it makes only one encapsulation's uniform and base queries (`liftBase_bound`). `auth_bills` and `records_bills`: the bill conjuncts of `record_auth_lift` and `record_lift` at the ideal environment of each target with the frame of `frame_idealD`, at `BI_g = inputG caps qInit budget bounds`. These are the bills behind `authLoss_g` and `recLoss_g` that P1 states without them.

## CE-12 P2: the terms of the bounds

- `ε_A2pub` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineGap.a2pubTerm`): the sum over the `qCreate + 1` generated identity positions of the joint-use weak-EUF advantages of the A2 client's reductions (`PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple.a2pub_owner_bound`; per-owner NoBundleFailure).
- `ε_A1` (`a1Term`, real-valued, entering as `ENNReal.ofReal`): the sum over the `qCreate` creation indices of the Server-authentication bound of `R_c` at the caps transported from the budget (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthBills.ce_a1_billed`, whose bills `ce_a1_linked` restates).
- `ctGapTerm`: F-ct2's explicit public term `𝒟[happens ctGapEvent <$> game]`, a probability in the real experiment. Design-v2's `ε_ct = Σ_g (kemLoss + slotLoss + ctStat)` is not proved ("CE-12 P2: the D-ct spike").
- `ε_A1inj` (the right side of `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjectiveGame.ce_a1_injective`, used by `ce_or_injective_s2b` and `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`): the CE-A1 sum plus the real-valued `ε_inj` sum, entering as `ENNReal.ofReal`; its bills are those of `ce_a1_injective` (every `R_c` and every collision reduction).
- `ε_inj` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineCollision.injTerm`): the sum over the `qCreate + 1` identity indices of the joint-use weak-EUF advantage of the zero-query collision reduction `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective.collider`.
- `ctCollTerm`: the explicit public term `𝒟[happens ctCollEvent <$> game]`, a probability in the real experiment. Design-v2's `ε_ctcoll = (qCreate + 1)·C(qAccept, 2)·(Adv^IND-CCA(pairAdversary) + 2δ_corr + 2^-256)` needs the game-level `ct_collision_game` (`𝒟[CtColl] ≤ (qCreate + 1)·C(qAccept, 2)·CP`), which is not proved; its KEM-level half is `pair_cp_le`.
- `pairAdversary`'s advantage (`pair_cp_le`): one ML-KEM IND-CCA advantage of a fixed adversary independent of the deployment adversary, plus twice the correctness error `correctnessError setup` and `2^-256`.
- Which forms carry which terms. Server side (`ce_or_b2s_sym`, `ce_or_injective_b2s`, `ce_app_server_sym`, `ce_kt_server_sym`): `ε_A2pub` only; `symbolic_lor_server`: `2⁻¹·(ε_A2pub + ε_inj)`. Beacon side: `ε_A1 + ctGapTerm` (`ce_app_beacon_sym`, `ce_kt_beacon_sym`), plus `ctCollTerm` (`ce_or_s2b_sym`, `ce_uks_sym`), or `ε_A1inj + ctGapTerm + ctCollTerm` (`ce_or_injective_s2b`, `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`). Either side of the claim: `ε_A2pub + ε_A1 + ctGapTerm` (`ce_app_sym`, `ce_kt_sym`) and `2⁻¹·(ε_A2pub + ε_A1 + ctGapTerm + ε_inj + ctCollTerm)` (`symbolic_lor`).

## CE-12 P2: premises and contracts used

- Gap lemmas, containments and `symbolic_lor_le`: `Phase1ReadBack codec`, `Phase2ReadBack codec` and `budget.Respects adversary` (`lor_difference` and `lor_difference_peer` need only `Phase1ReadBack`; `origin_b2s_sym`, `app_sym`, `app_peer_sym`, `app_owner_sym`, `kt_sym`, `kt_peer_sym` and `kt_owner_sym` need none).
- `server_gap_bound`, and every bound with `ε_A2pub`: `shortRejects` with `setup.wiring`, the read-back contracts and the budget (`a2pub_owner_bound`). `symbolic_lor_server` needs exactly these; `ce_kt_server_sym` adds `ce_kt_sum_le`'s premises and no CE-A1 premise.
- `ce_a1_linked`, and every bound with `ε_A1`: CE-A1's premises: `KemBound setup caps`, `FrameReadBack`, `ReadWritable codec.frameWrite codec.frameRead`, `X25519ClassLaw`, `X25519ScalarSpread`, `SourceFullCorrect`, `HasAttachedVerificationAdapter`, the read-back contracts and the budget.
- `identity_collision_game`: the read-back contracts and the budget. `pair_cp_le`: `FunctionalDecapsulation setup.kem`.
- The corollaries with the CE-12 bounds add the premises of the P1 headline they use (`FunctionalDecapsulation`, `SourceFullCorrect`, `KemBound`, `initBound`, `FrameReadBack` for CE-OR and CE-UKS; `symbolic_lor_server_bound` adds `ce_lor_server`'s).
- Contracts entering only as advantage terms: those of P1, joint-use weak EUF (A2pub, `ε_inj`), CE-A1's Server-authentication contracts (Gap-DH, the AEAD and schedule contracts of its bound, joint-use signature EUF) and ML-KEM IND-CCA (`pairAdversary`). No new assumption or contract; no import of `PqxdhBoundary*`.

## CE-12 P2: decisions and deviations

- F-ct2 instead of the closed `ε_ct` ("CE-12 P2: the D-ct spike"); to be reported to the owner before publication (design-v2 section 14, note 4).
- `ctCollTerm` is explicit: `ct_collision_game` needs a decomposition of the experiment at two accept ordinals (the later accept's encapsulation is a fresh draw to an honest pool key whose marginal law is key generation), estimated at 2-4 actual days. It is listed under the open points and is part of the owner flag. The KEM-level half (`pair_cp_le`) is proved.
- The gap and symbolic events use the budgeted designation `designateIn`, as P1 does. On the support of a budget-respecting adversary it agrees with `designate` (`PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange.range_game`).
- `SymCpartS2b` names the Server peers built by some session whose accepted candidate verifies to the owner's identity key. It reads the build entries directly rather than `linkOf`, which picks the first build of a key ID, so the containment needs no key-ID or build uniqueness.
- `pairAdversary` forwards its encapsulation's coins and base queries through an explicit `liftBase` handler, not `liftComp`, whose sub-spec instance path through `MlKemCCAOracleSpec` is not the one `simulateQ_add_liftComp_left` needs.
- `lor_difference` takes only `Phase1ReadBack` (the unused `Phase2ReadBack` and budget premises were dropped before the commit).
- Hygiene commit `3b8a4cd3`: a copy of each module with its deprecation suppressions removed showed that only `pair_random_le`, `pair_first_error` and `pair_second_error` need `set_option linter.deprecated false` (VCVio's `probEvent`). The ten others suppressed nothing and were removed; no statement changed.
- Central moved from `b88e3012` to `c031e4e7` (LQ-F upstream unit D) during the first session. It was merged at `a981309b`: conflicts only at the hot spots, resolved with both sides whole and central's first, followed by an inventory recount (1897) and rehash. Central moved again to `adf9d8fc` (LQ-F upstream units C and A1) before the fix pass and was merged at `7c38da80` in the same way (recount 1902 to 1908).
- The injective CE-OR forms are stated with the existing injectivity events: CE-R1' `ReplayOwnerEvent` (b2s), CE-C3 record replay (both directions; probability zero), `SharedMatch` and, in the full s2b form, `IdentityCollision` (s2b). No new uniqueness invariant is needed. The b2s form charges one `ε_A2pub`, because the gap and the replay are both CE-A2pub's per-owner event. The original s2b form `ce_or_injective_s2b` is kept unchanged beside `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`.
- Design-v2 section 12 maps the KT rows to `ce_kt_sym` with "`+ε_A2pub` (server) or `+ε_A1+ε_ct` (beacon)", the App-b2s row to "`+ε_A1+ε_ct`", and the SSX-s2b and SS-s2b rows to `symbolic_lor` with peer-only tests. The first session built only the mixed forms `ce_kt_sym`, `ce_app_sym` and `symbolic_lor`, which charge `ε_A2pub + ε_A1 + ctGapTerm` (and `ctCollTerm` for LOR) whatever the claim's side; that was an unrecorded deviation. The fix pass adds the side forms `ce_kt_server_sym`, `ce_kt_beacon_sym`, `ce_app_beacon_sym` and `symbolic_lor_server`, so every row of section 12 now has a form with exactly its planned terms (with `ctGapTerm` and `ctCollTerm` for `ε_ct` and `ε_ctcoll`). The mixed forms remain for claims whose side is not fixed.
- Bills: only `pairAdversary`'s bill and the record lifts' bills at `BI_g` are new; `guessBill` needs the per-class uniform cost of the anomaly guesser's split handler, including its native record and hash steps, and is open.
- Not built, against design-v2 and the task: `guessBill` with `HeadlineBills`, AG and `ct_collision_game` (open points).

## CE-12 P2: not claimed

- No closed `ε_ct`: the Beacon-side and mixed-side terms carry `ctGapTerm` (F-ct2). No closed `ε_ctcoll`: `ctCollTerm` is explicit. Both are probabilities in the real experiment, not advantages.
- No phase-1 uniform bill for `ptc` (`guessBill` through `splitStep_uniform`) and no aggregate `HeadlineBills`: every headline with `β_g` cites the IND-CCA advantage of `ptc` without a certified phase-1 query bill. No agreement fact AG: the b2s publisher pin and the mode-O (full-reply) plaintext stay symbolic-only (design-v2 section 7.6).
- The injective forms bound the named failure modes; that they are all the ways injectivity can fail is argued in prose ("CE-12 P2: what is proved"), not proved in Lean, and the key-ID uniqueness the s2b argument uses is read from `server.rs`, not proved.
- Beacon-side symbolic eligibility covers only `(S, serverKid)` pins (design-v2 section 15). The symbolic events cover raw receives only (s2b from position 2), as P1's do. No plaintext-linked UKS and no `INITIAL_SECRET` endpoint.
- The symbolic events read the final native identity keys and the constructed Server, so they are not public functions of a view. They are the ProVerif-shaped eligibility conditions. They appear on the left-hand sides of the bound forms; the event forms also show them on the right-hand side inside the gap events `serverGap` and `beaconGap`, which are built from `SymServer` and `SymBeacon`, and `lor_difference`'s conclusion names `ServerGap` and `BeaconGap`.
- No later-quantum symbolic corollary (P3).

## CE-12 P2: open points and the rest of P2

1. Owner, before publication. (a) Two terms of P2 are probabilities in the real experiment, not closed advantages: `ctGapTerm` (F-ct2, the D-ct stop rule fired; the per-entry step is valid and what is missing is proof engineering, "CE-12 P2: the D-ct spike") and `ctCollTerm` (`ct_collision_game` is not proved). `ctGapTerm` appears in `ce_or_s2b_sym`, `ce_uks_sym`, `ce_app_sym`, `ce_app_beacon_sym`, `ce_kt_sym`, `ce_kt_beacon_sym`, `symbolic_lor`, `symbolic_lor_bound`, `ce_or_injective_s2b` and `ce_or_injective_s2b_full` (and in their event forms and in `beacon_gap_le`, `ce_a1_linked`, `sym_gap` and `lor_difference_bound`); `ctCollTerm` appears in `ce_or_s2b_sym`, `ce_uks_sym`, `symbolic_lor`, `symbolic_lor_bound`, `ce_or_injective_s2b` and `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`. Affected VC rows of the CE-13 column: S2b-origin, s2b-C1, s2b-C4, far-s2b-C4 and the injective S2b-origin rows (both terms), b2s-UKSr (both), SSX-b2s, SS-b2s and 167 (both), App-b2s and b2s-S2 (`ctGapTerm`), S3-beacon and A1-root with an owner claim (`ctGapTerm`), and the linked form of S2b-initial (`ce_a1_linked`, `ctGapTerm`). (b) Unaffected: the Server-side rows L2-origin, b2s-C2, b2s-C2-chosen-fs, b2s-C4, far-b2s-C4 and the injective L2-origin rows (`ce_or_b2s_sym`, `ce_or_injective_b2s`), S3-server and A1-root with a Server-peer claim (`ce_kt_server_sym`), App-s2b and s2b-S1 (`ce_app_server_sym`), and SSX-s2b and SS-s2b (`symbolic_lor_server`), which carry neither term. (c) Gating conditions: the headlines with `β_g` (`ce_or_s2b`, `ce_or_s2b_sym`, `ce_uks_sender`, `ce_uks_sym`, `ce_app`, `ce_app_sym`, `ce_app_beacon_sym`, `ce_lor`, `symbolic_lor_bound`, `ce_or_injective_s2b`, `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`) cite the IND-CCA advantage of `ptc`, whose phase-1 uniform bill (`guessBill`) is not proved, so their publication is gated on item 4; the headlines with `ctCollTerm` are gated on item 2, and `pair_cp_le` and `pair_bills` are consumed only once `ct_collision_game` exists.
2. `ct_collision_game` (`𝒟[CtColl] ≤ (qCreate + 1)·C(qAccept, 2)·CP`), estimated at 2-4 actual days. With `pair_cp_le` it closes `ctCollTerm` into `ε_ctcoll`. Every corollary that carries `ctCollTerm` then composes by monotonicity.
3. Injective CE-OR forms: done (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineInjective`, with the identity collision in `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`).
4. Bills, remainder (0.5-1.5 days): `splitStep_uniform` (per-step uniform bound of `handlerWith (idealSplit …)` under `flat`, including the native record and hash steps), `ptc_bills` with `guessBill`, and `HeadlineBills` collecting `kem_transfer_bills`, `allIdeal_bills`, `prf_bills`, `auth_bills`, `records_bills`, `ce_a1_billed`, `a2_bills`, `collider_bills` and `pair_bills` (all nine already proved; what is missing is `ptc_bills` with `guessBill`, through `splitStep_uniform`, and the aggregation itself).
5. AG (2-5 days): `AgreementStatement` in `idealAuth` (seal-context invariant, a public associated-data lemma, injectivity of `build_associated_data`).
6. D-ct (3-6 days, deferred by the stop rule): items (1)-(4) of "CE-12 P2: the D-ct spike".
7. Confirming review of the fix pass: done at `6b9e29f4` ("CE-12 P2: confirming review"). Next the integration into central (PROTOCOL section 6), which merges central if it has moved since `adf9d8fc` and recompiles into a fresh prefix; then P3. (Changed by the commit that records the confirming review: this point said "Confirming review of the fix pass, integration; then P3.")

## CE-12 P2: review fix pass

The P2 review ran three independent lenses (semantic, composition, hygiene) at `757882fa`. The composition lens re-derived every hop and found no hop mismatch, no double-counted term and no central module edited. The findings were fixed as follows; no existing theorem statement changed (the Lean diff of the fix pass changes only documentation comments and one `open` line besides the additions), so nothing was weakened.

- D-ct point (2) (major, semantic): the spike section, the status paragraph, the analysis, plan section 102, the receipt (`spike.missing`, `spike.remaining_estimate_days`) and the owner flag no longer say that the design's per-entry `2^-256` bound is invalid or needs a random-oracle derivation. They say that the bound holds by averaging over the uniform marginal of `key32_0` (the derivation is a fixed function of the root, `concrete.rs:64-106`) and that what is missing is a Lean lemma for this averaged union bound over the World's dynamic R1 table, plus the World invariants (1), (3) and (4). Item (2) is re-estimated at 1-2 days (was 2-4) and the total at 3-6 days (was 4-8); items (1), (3) and (4) alone (2-4 days) exceed the 1.5-day stop rule, so the F-ct2 decision stands. The hygiene lens's optional rewording (random-oracle Expand or a computational hop) is superseded by the same argument.
- Owner notice names only `ctGapTerm` (minor, semantic) and owner flag understates the weakening (major, composition): open point 1, the status paragraph, the analysis and the receipt's first open point now list both explicit terms, every affected headline and VC row, and the unaffected Server-side rows. The Server-side forms below confine both terms to Beacon-side and mixed-side forms.
- Missing Server-side symbolic KT corollary (major, composition): new `KtPeerSym`, `kt_peer_sym`, `ce_kt_server_sym_le` and `ce_kt_server_sym` (`≤ ce_kt_sum_le's bound + ε_A2pub`, without CE-A1's premises); S3-server is mapped to it. With it, `KtOwnerSym`, `kt_owner_sym`, `ce_kt_beacon_sym_le` and `ce_kt_beacon_sym` (`+ ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`) for S3-beacon.
- No peer-only symbolic LOR (minor, composition): new `LorEligPeerSym`, `LorWinPeerSym`, `lor_difference_peer`, `symbolic_lor_server_le`, `lor_difference_server_bound`, `symbolic_lor_server` (`2⁻¹·(ε_A2pub + ε_inj)`) and `symbolic_lor_server_bound` (with `ce_lor_server`'s bound, no `β_g`); SSX-s2b and SS-s2b are mapped to it.
- `injectiveS2b` leaves out the identity collision (minor, semantic): new `injectiveS2bFull` (`injectiveS2b`'s event or `IdentityCollision`), `injective_s2b_full_le` and `ce_or_injective_s2b_full`, whose right side is exactly `ce_or_injective_s2b`'s. Before: `ce_or_injective_s2b` bounds `OriginFailureSym .s2b ∨ SharedMatch ∨ OwnerReplay`. After: the same theorem, unchanged, and `ce_or_injective_s2b_full` bounds that event or `IdentityCollision` by the same quantity; since its event contains the old one, it implies `ce_or_injective_s2b`, so it is at least as strong. The proof routes the shared match through `shared_collision` and bounds `A1NoPinnedMatch ∨ IdentityCollision` by `ce_a1_billed` and `identity_collision_game`. The documentation (module, this guide, the analysis) no longer presents the failure modes as complete; the completeness argument, including the b2s cross-peer case, is stated as prose.
- Owner-only APP claims pay `ε_A2pub` (nit, hygiene): new `AppWinOwnerSym`, `app_owner_sym`, `ce_app_beacon_sym_le` and `ce_app_beacon_sym` (`≤ ce_app's bound + ofReal ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`), design-v2 section 12's App-b2s form.
- Analysis wording (nits, semantic and hygiene): "private-reply-mode plaintext" is now the full-reply-mode (mode O) plaintext; "used only on the left-hand sides" now names the event forms' gap events (also here, "CE-12 P2: not claimed"); the symbolic LOR sentence now says "half of the sum of"; the positive bill sentences moved out of "What is not claimed" into a paragraph on bills, which keeps only the `guessBill` and `HeadlineBills` gap as a non-claim.
- `β_g`'s IND-CCA adversary has no phase-1 bill and `pair_cp_le` is unused by any headline (minor, composition): no Lean change; both are now explicit gating conditions in open point 1 (c).
- No action (composition lens's confirmation of the hops, `ptc`, the bills and the term accounting).

## CE-12 P2: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass (Lean commit `25ca7adc` and documentation commit `6b9e29f4`) at `6b9e29f4` found every finding of the P2 review at `757882fa` resolved ("CE-12 P2: review fix pass"); none needs further action. It recompiled the three modules the fix pass changed (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineSymbolic`, `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineSymbolicLor`, `PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineInjective`) without `-o` through the coordination wrapper at the clean head against the fix-pass `LEAN_PATH` `ce12/p2/fix/leanpath` (exit 0, empty logs). Its guard scan of the six modules finds 120 theorems (30, 14, 34, 29, 7, 6) with 120 exact guards in declaration order, every axiom list within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, and no forbidden token (the scan's only `admit` matches are the words "admitted" and "admits" in documentation and names); the scan was rerun at `6b9e29f4` for this record with the same result.

- Nit (documentation, open point 4): the point said that only "the last four" of the nine component bills that `HeadlineBills` is to collect are proved, but all nine are theorems: `kem_transfer_bills` (`PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer`), `allIdeal_bills` and `prf_bills` (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransferBills`), `ce_a1_billed` (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthBills`), `a2_bills` (`PqxdhDeploymentProvenance`), `collider_bills` (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective`), and `auth_bills`, `records_bills` and `pair_bills` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineBills`). The point now says "(all nine already proved; what is missing is `ptc_bills` with `guessBill`, through `splitStep_uniform`, and the aggregation itself)". Plan sections 102 and 103 do not repeat the nine-component list and are unchanged.
- Nit (documentation, D-ct item (2) of "CE-12 P2: the D-ct spike"): the item said that an entry drawn before `key32_0` "is a fixed value, which a fresh uniform `key32_0` hits with probability `2^-256`", while what is fixed and compared with `key32_0` is the derived key `f(entry)`. The item now says "For an entry drawn before `key32_0`, `f(entry)` is a fixed value, which a fresh uniform `key32_0` equals with probability `2^-256`", and the receipt's `spike.missing` says the same. The analysis already phrases this step through the derived key and is unchanged there; its opening paragraph of the P2 entry records the confirming review.

This commit changes no Lean file, inventory row or check. It records the review in this section, the status paragraph, open point 7, the checks, the analysis and the receipt (`scope`, `topic`, `spike.missing`, `review.status`, `review.fix_commits`, `review.docs_fix_commit`, `review.confirming_review`, the plan record, a new `plan_snapshot` record, `open_points`, the commits), and refreshes the committed plan snapshot `doc/impl/common-deployment-experiment-plan.md` as a wholesale copy of the working plan through its new section 104: against central `adf9d8fc`'s snapshot (through section 100), which this branch carried unchanged until now, it adds section 100's integration bullet and sections 101 to 104 (101 P1 integrated, 102 P2's first session, 103 its review fix pass, 104 its confirming review). The integration merges central if it has moved since `adf9d8fc`, central's side first at every anchor (taking this branch's plan snapshot, the newer wholesale copy, if central changed it), recounts the inventory and rehashes it, and runs its checks on the topic tip into a fresh prefix. Once integrated, the six modules are published and are not edited again. The owner flag of open point 1 is unchanged and goes with the integration report.

## LQ-F upstream unit D: what is proved

Unit D adds two modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/`, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemTargets` (unit D1, 12 guarded theorems) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterGame` (unit D2, 8 guarded theorems); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains two imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound` (`rootBillL_eq` and `later_guess_term` only on `propext`), recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`. The unit proves no security bound. It fixes the definitions that the later units of the lane state their theorems with, states the three targets of the lane as `Prop`-valued definitions, and proves projections, unfoldings, the evaluation of the guessing term, the implication between the two U-L1 targets and the post-phase bridges of the later ideal game.

The targets, as `Prop`-valued definitions (closed statements, quantified over every type and argument):

- `OpaqueBranchSlotsTwoTargetL` (U-L1, generic; to be proved by unit A2 as `opaqueBranchL_slots_two`): for every program `work` of the logged source within a logged source budget (`∀ kind, work.IsQueryBoundP (fun query => laterKind query = some kind) (budget kind)`; the Server read has no kind) and under `SourceFullCorrect setup.source setup.hmac`, `(PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerOpaque.opaqueBranch … work … false).boolDistAdvantage (laterSlotGame … work … 2) ≤ slotLossL … work … budget`. This is the logged counterpart of `PqxdhRegistrationJointBeaconFinishOnce.opaqueBranch_slots_two`.
- `LaterSlotsTwoTarget` (U-L1 at the compiled LQ-F program; unit A2's `later_slots_two`): the same inequality at `work = compileServer setup codec route bounds mode first second`, the per-target decision `LaterServerTransfer.decision … g`, `supplied = none`, `count = budget.create`, the selected KEM `selectedKem budget g`, the raw-accept ordinal `g.1.val` and the logged source budget `laterTransported bounds budget post`, for every `A₁` with `budget.Respects first` and every `A₂` with `post.Respects (second output reveal)` on every input, under `SourceFullCorrect`. `laterSlotsTwo_of_generic` proves that the generic target implies it (through `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompileBudget.compile_server_certified`), and `slotLossL_transported` evaluates the first term of the loss at the transported caps: `(qAccept + qFinish + qHmac + qHmac₂)/2^256`.
- `IdealCompileServerTarget` (U-L2; units B0-B2's `ideal_compile_server`): under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, for every `A₁` with `budget.Respects first` and every `A₂`, `𝒟[laterSlotGame … (compileServer …) runtime budget.create (selectedKem budget g) g.1.val 2] {true} = 𝒟[ceDecideL setup codec budget event claim g <$> (idealLaterGame setup codec route checks budget g ensureInit bounds mode first second).run runtime] {true}`, the later form of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile.ideal_compile`. No post budget is taken (LQ-F design-v2 section 4.7): the post part forwards only coin, HMAC and BLAKE2b commands (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterCompile.postCore`), whose field costs `PqxdhDeploymentCompileBudget.cost (.inr _) (postCore _)` are zero (the fact behind `PqxdhDeploymentLaterCompile.compile_later_reads` and, for the KEM field, the proof of `compile_server_no_kem`), and the compiled program makes no KEM read (`compile_server_no_kem`). `compile_server_reads` bounds only the Server read; the first wording of this sentence cited it for the field reads (corrected in the review fix pass). Without a post budget unit B proves the target with per-kind step bounds (open point 5). (Closed by unit B, which cut inside the run part and needed no per-kind step bound; see open point 5. Note added by unit B's review fix pass.)

| --- | --- | --- |

| The U-L2 target (D2) | 0 | `IdealCompileServerTarget`. |

## LQ-F upstream unit D: decisions and deviations

- Targets as `Prop`-valued definitions. The three target statements are closed propositions, quantified over every type and argument, instead of `sorry`-stubbed theorems: they can be cited, compared and composed now, and no `sorry` is published. The later units prove the theorems LQ-F design-v2 names (`opaqueBranchL_slots_two`, `later_slots_two`, `ideal_compile_server`) with these statements, and each `Prop` then follows by application.
- A generic U-L1 target beside the instance. LQ-F design-v2 section 4.1 freezes the instance `later_slots_two` (as elaborated in `probe/Stmts2.lean`); the unit also freezes the generic form `OpaqueBranchSlotsTwoTargetL`, which has the shape of `opaqueBranch_slots_two` and is the statement unit A2 proves first, and `laterSlotsTwo_of_generic` fixes how the instance follows (the logged source budget is met by `compile_server_certified`).
- `ceDecideL` in `IdealCompileServerTarget`: by definition it is the decision function of the probe statement (`decide (happens event (laterOf o) = true ∧ designated … claim (laterOf o) = some g)`), named so that unit B states its theorem as `ideal_compile` is stated, with a decision on the outcome.
- `rootBillL` is generic in the source budget and equal to the classical `rootBill` (`rootBillL_eq`; LQ-F design-v2 D8). The transported budget `laterTransported bounds budget post` is substituted by the consumers (unit A2's `rootClientL_coins` and `laterSlot_bills`). No bills target statement is frozen: LQ-F design-v2 lists `laterSlot_bills` under unit A2 only. If a later unit needs a different `slotLossL`, `rootBillL` or `laterSlotGame`, the frozen definitions are not edited: the consuming unit adds a primed definition with a comparison lemma (LQ-F design-v2 R9).
- Additions to LQ-F design-v2's list for unit D: `cacheOfL` (the logged `cacheOf`, which `sharedStartL` needs), `hybrid_hybridOf` and `entryDk_entryDkOf` (evidence for the review focus that unit D copies the classical forms: the classical hybrid and distinguisher are instances of the generic ones by `rfl`), `rootBillL_eq`, `slotLossL_transported` (the review focus "first term at `laterTransported`"), `idealReg_hmac` (the ideal instance of `postNative_real`'s premise), `laterOf_real` (`laterWith_real` through `laterOf`) and `laterSlotsTwo_of_generic`.
- `postNative_real`'s premise is the HMAC shape of the handler: the HMAC command is the native HMAC on the deployment state with the rest of the extended state kept (LQ-F design-v2: "with snapshot and log kept"; the backend and the seed table are kept too). Coins and BLAKE2b need no premise, because `handlerWith` runs them itself.
- Guide placement. These sections and the item 2 guide's section "Item 2 over the logged LQ-F source (LQ-F upstream)" carry unit D's guide; LQ-F design-v2 section 4 had named `doc/impl/later-quantum-lqf-spike.md` for units D and A. That guide is not edited, and its open point 3 (item 2 over the logged source) stays open until unit A2.
- Publication: one Lean commit per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block (`inv recount` before `inv rehash`) and with its own root import check and focused inventory check; then one documentation commit. Reviewed at `28230554`; the review fix pass adds one Lean commit (`822bb9d4`, docstrings and one unused `open` name, with `inv recount` and `inv rehash`) and one documentation commit ("LQ-F upstream unit D: review fix pass"); one more documentation commit records the confirming review of `42488632` ("LQ-F upstream unit D: confirming review"). The integration fast-forwards central to the branch's checked tip (status paragraph).
- Olean prefix: a fresh registered prefix `lqf-up/D/olean` (a resolved symlink mirror of the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `ab9986ef`, `ce11b/u7/int/olean`, without the root olean, 6343 links) receives the two module oleans; development oleans went to a separate registered directory `lqf-up/D/devolean`, which no published `LEAN_PATH` names.

## LQ-F upstream unit D: names for consumers

- Units A1 and A2 (item 2 over the logged source; LQ-F design-v2 sections 4.3 and 4.4): `AcceptL`, `GSpecL`, `acceptStepL`, `acceptStepL_fst`, `skelImplL`, `skelImplL_inl`, `skelImplL_accept`, `groupedClientL`, `groupedClientL_inl`, `groupedClientL_accept`, `groupedClientL_read`, `skelResumeL`, `postSkelL`, `cacheOfL`, `sharedStartL`, `sharedClientL`, `rootClientL`, `entryClientL`, `entryDL`, `hybridOf`, `entryDkOf`, `entryDkL`, `laterSlotGame`, `slotLossL`, `rootBillL`, `later_guess_term`, `slotLossL_transported`; the targets `OpaqueBranchSlotsTwoTargetL` and `LaterSlotsTwoTarget` with `laterSlotsTwo_of_generic`.
- Units B0 to B2 (the later second compile lemma): `laterSlotGame`, `idealLaterGame`, `laterOf`, `ceDecideL`, `laterWith_split`; the target `IdealCompileServerTarget`.
- Unit C (CE-11b's later pieces): `idealLaterGame`, `realRegAt_hmac`, `postNative_real`, `idealReg_hmac`, `postNative_ideal`, `laterWith_postNative`, `idealLater_postNative` (for `postNative_split` and `c6_idealGame`). `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterGame.postNative_real` has the same short name as `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentReal.postNative_real`, the real-registration case with a different statement (`postNative … (realReg … created) = realPost … created`), of which it is the generic form; unit C, which is likely to use both, writes the qualified names.
- CE-12-L (P3): `LaterSlotsTwoTarget`, `IdealCompileServerTarget`, `laterOf_real`, `slotLossL_transported`. `slotLossL` is the logged form of `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransfer.allIdealLoss` (term by term, with `rootClientL` and `entryDkL` for `rootClient` and `entryDk`), and CE-12's classical `slotLoss` is `allIdealLoss … (PqxdhDeploymentCompileCaps.transported bounds budget)` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineChain` on `codex/deployment-ce12`), so CE-12-L can mirror it as `slotLossL … (laterTransported bounds budget post)`.

## LQ-F upstream unit D: contracts used

None. The target statements contain, as hypotheses inside the `Prop` definitions, the premises the later units will take: `SourceFullCorrect` and the logged source budget for U-L1 (with the run-phase and post-run budgets at the compiled program), and `Phase1ReadBack`, `Phase2ReadBack` and the run-phase budget for U-L2; no theorem of this unit assumes any of them. `postNative_real` takes an internal hypothesis (the HMAC shape of the registration handler), discharged for the ideal registration by `idealReg_hmac`. No new contract and no new assumption (as LQ-F design-v2 section 10 plans).

## LQ-F upstream unit D: open points

1. Integration. The review of `28230554` and its fix pass are recorded in "LQ-F upstream unit D: review fix pass", the confirming review of the fix pass at `42488632` in "LQ-F upstream unit D: confirming review". The review checked the review focus of LQ-F design-v2 section 4.1: the targets are the logged counterparts of `slotGame` (`laterSlotGame`), of the right side of `opaqueBranch_slots_two` (`slotLossL`), of `rootBill` (`rootBillL`) and of `idealGame` (`idealLaterGame`), and `slotLossL`'s first term at `laterTransported` is `(qAccept + qFinish + qHmac + qHmac₂)/2^256` (`slotLossL_transported`). The concurrent CE-12 track (`codex/deployment-ce12`) appends at the same anchors (the root import block, the inventory rows and module blocks, the end of the analysis and this guide's status paragraphs); whichever is integrated second merges the other first, central's side first at every anchor. The commit that records the confirming review replaced the "Not integrated" sentences of the status paragraph and of the analysis entry with the integration's route, and the integration message records its outcome (updated by that commit; this point first said that the integration replaces them with its record).
2. The proofs of the targets: `OpaqueBranchSlotsTwoTargetL` and `LaterSlotsTwoTarget` by units A1 and A2, `IdealCompileServerTarget` by units B0 to B2 (B0 is the spike with the stop rule of LQ-F design-v2 section 4.5). Units B0, B1 and A1 can start against this branch's oleans before its integration (LQ-F design-v2 section 6, lanes).
3. Unit C's `postNative_split` and `c6_idealGame` use `postNative_real`, `postNative_ideal` and `idealLater_postNative`; `c6_idealGame` waits for this unit's integration.
4. The working plan's record of this unit is left to the plan's owner (the orchestrator); this unit did not edit the plan.
5. Unit B's budget route for `IdealCompileServerTarget` (added by the review fix pass). The target takes no post budget, so unit B cannot reuse the classical cut lemmas behind `ideal_compile`, which take a `certified` bound in all fourteen source kinds together with `unreadIdentity : budget (.inr 0) = 0` and `unreadKem : budget (.inr 1) = 0` (the route `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBranch.slotGameAt_cut`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut.item2_prefix`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource.kemCommands_stopped`, then `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemCommands.creations` and `PqxdhRegistrationJointOpenCosts.command_program`). Without a post budget the logged scan, which contains the whole post part when the selected accept is never reached, has no finite coin, HMAC or BLAKE2b cap, and at LQ-F the identity-read cap is `laterTransported bounds budget post (.inr 0) = budget.create`, not 0 (checked by `rfl` in the review's scratch probe). Unit B therefore proves the target with per-kind step bounds, as the LQ-F chain already does (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerReserve.creations`, `compile_server_creates`, `compile_server_no_kem`). If that turns out infeasible, the R9 fallback of LQ-F design-v2 section 8 applies: unit B adds a primed target with `post` and `postRespects` beside the frozen one, which is not edited. That costs CE-12-L nothing, because `LaterSlotsTwoTarget` already takes `post` and `postRespects`: the review's scratch composition probe (`lqf-up/D/review-semantic/probe/ComposeL.lean`, not a committed check) composes `kem_transfer_server`, `LaterSlotsTwoTarget` and `IdealCompileServerTarget` into the CE-KT-L transfer form (`chain_ideal_later`, allowed axioms only). LQ-F design-v2 section 4.7 gives the incomplete reason of this guide's first wording; the design file is not edited by this unit. Closed by unit B (note added by unit B's review fix pass): unit B proved the target as frozen without per-kind step bounds and without the R9 primed target. It cuts inside the run part: the compiled program is its embedded run part followed by the rest (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterCut.compileServer_split`, `restOf_quiet`, `compile_scanServer`); the logged copies of the classical cut lemmas (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterBranch.laterSlotGameAt_cut`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterCut.item2_prefixL`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterPool.kemCommands_stoppedL`) take the run part's budget `runBudget`, finite in all fourteen kinds with identity and KEM caps 0 (`run_certified`, `scan_certifiedL`, `runBudget_caps`); and the rest needs no cap (discarded in the dead branches, `itemRestL_dead`; no KEM read and no creation in the active branch, `restOf_allowed`, `restOf_costly`). See "LQ-F upstream unit B: decisions and deviations".

## LQ-F upstream unit D: re-estimate

Figures in nominal agent-days. LQ-F design-v2 section 6 estimated unit D at 0.5-0.75 (build 0.25, review to integration 0.25-0.5) and 20-32 theorems. The build took about 0.1 with 20 theorems: the probes of LQ-F design-v2 section 9 (`SkelL.lean`, `Stmts2.lean`, `DeferL.lean`, `Hmac.lean`) carried every definition and three of the proofs. Review to integration is unchanged at 0.25-0.5; the review fix pass took about 0.1. No other unit's estimate changes here. Whether the per-kind budget route of open point 5 changes unit B's figure is not yet known, and B0's spike as LQ-F design-v2 section 4.5 scopes it does not test that route (the spike covers (i) `stepAtC_ghost`, (ii) the non-selected logged accept step and (iii) `step_serverRead`), so its stop rule cannot tell. The orchestrator should add a per-kind budget step to B0's spike scope (a first try of open point 5's per-kind step bounds where the classical cut lemmas take the `certified` budget); otherwise unit B1 re-estimates once the budget route is tried, before any branch commit. LQ-F design-v2 stays unedited. (Corrected after the confirming review: this paragraph first said that B0's spike and its stop rule would tell.) Closed by unit B (note added by unit B's review fix pass): B0's spike kept LQ-F design-v2's scope (no budget step was added), and no re-estimate was made before unit B's first branch commit; the per-kind route was never needed, because unit B cut inside the run part, whose budget the run-phase budget gives in all fourteen kinds (open point 5, "LQ-F upstream unit B: decisions and deviations"). Units B0 to B2 took about 0.3 to build ("LQ-F upstream unit B: re-estimate").

- The contamination scan and the closure check are scratch metaprograms, recorded here and in the receipt (probe and log hashes), not committed checks, as item 2's are (`doc/impl/extract-composition.md`). The design probes cited above (`probe/Stmts2.lean` and the others of LQ-F design-v2 section 9) are scratch too.

The commit that records the confirming review changes only files under `doc/`, which `check-inventory` does not read; the integration's checks of the branch's tip are in its checks receipt (`lqf-up/D/int/checks-receipt.json`), which repeats these runs.

## LQ-F upstream unit D: review fix pass

An independent review of `28230554` in two parts (semantic and hygiene) found no error in the Lean statements or proofs; both parts approved with nits. The semantic part recompiled both modules without `-o` through the coordination wrapper (exit 0, empty logs), compared every definition of D1 and D2 with its classical counterpart at `ab9986ef` (`slotGame`, the right side of `opaqueBranch_slots_two` and `allIdealLoss`, `rootBill`, `sharedStart`, `sharedClient`, `cacheOf`, `postSkel`, `skelResume`, `rootClient`, `entryClient`, `entryD`, `hybrid`, `entryDk`, `idealGame`, `ideal_compile`, `ceDecide`) and with the LQ-F objects it uses (`LaterServerCommands.impl`, `accept` and `ordered`, `LaterServerCut.opaqueResume` and `tagged`, `LaterServerOpaque.OpaqueContext`, `sourcePreOpaque` and `opaqueBranch`, `laterWith`, `laterWith_real`, `realPost`, `join_later`, `kem_transfer_server`, `laterTransported`) and found them faithful, and composed the frozen targets with `kem_transfer_server` into the CE-KT-L transfer form in a scratch probe (`chain_ideal_later`, allowed axioms only). The hygiene part scanned both modules (12 and 8 theorems with as many exact guards in declaration order, allowed axioms only, no forbidden token, `set_option` only `autoImplicit false`), checked the inventory digests and counts at `ab9986ef`, `5d580bab`, `727d8835` and `28230554` (handwritten Lean files 1884, 1885, 1886, 1886), the receipt's hashes and lists, the footers, the registry, every cited name and the per-commit scopes, rebuilt both modules and the root at the clean `28230554` with byte-identical oleans, reran the contamination probe with the recorded result, and ran the focused `check-inventory` on the clean tree at `28230554` (exit 0). They raised two minor findings and eight nits. This fix pass resolves all ten with one Lean commit (`822bb9d4`: docstrings and one unused `open` name, with `inv recount` and `inv rehash`) and one documentation commit.

- Minor (semantic): `IdealCompileServerTarget` takes no post budget, and the documented reason cited `compile_server_reads` for "the post part issues no field read", although that lemma bounds only the Server read; the reason also left out that unit B cannot reuse the classical cut lemmas, which take a `certified` bound in all fourteen kinds with `unreadIdentity` (at LQ-F the identity-read cap is `budget.create`, and without a post budget the logged scan has no finite coin, HMAC or BLAKE2b cap). No statement changes. The docstrings of `IdealCompileServerTarget` and of the D2 module, and "LQ-F upstream unit D: what is proved" now cite the zero field costs of the post commands (`PqxdhDeploymentCompileBudget.cost (.inr _) (postCore _)`, the fact behind `PqxdhDeploymentLaterCompile.compile_later_reads` and, for the KEM field, the proof of `compile_server_no_kem`), and the new open point 5 records that unit B proves the target with per-kind step bounds (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerReserve.creations`, `compile_server_creates`, `compile_server_no_kem`) and that, if that is infeasible, the R9 fallback (a primed target with `post` and `postRespects`) costs CE-12-L nothing, as the review's composition probe confirms. The analysis entry gives the corrected reason in plain words.
- Nit (semantic): `slotLossL` is, term by term, the logged form of the existing classical `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransfer.allIdealLoss` (with `rootClientL` and `entryDkL` for `rootClient` and `entryDk`), whereas the docstring and the item 2 guide's table called it only the right side of `opaqueBranch_slots_two`, and LQ-F design-v2 section 4.1 says that it has no classical name. The docstrings of `slotLossL` and of the D1 module, "what is proved", the item 2 guide's correspondence table and "names for consumers" now name `allIdealLoss`, and the CE-12-L bullet says that CE-12-L can mirror CE-12's `slotLoss` (`allIdealLoss` at `PqxdhDeploymentCompileCaps.transported bounds budget`) as `slotLossL … (laterTransported bounds budget post)`.
- Nit (semantic): the status texts "Not yet reviewed and not integrated" (status paragraph) and "The unit is not yet reviewed or integrated" (analysis) must be updated. The status paragraph, the publication bullet and the analysis now record the review and this fix pass; the "not integrated" sentences remain, and open point 1 says that the integration replaces them with its record.
- Minor (hygiene): a bare "design-v2" named two designs, CE-11b's design-v2 (cited by this guide's CE-11b sections and 33 existing Lean modules at `ab9986ef`; corrected after the confirming review: this bullet first repeated the review's count of 35, which included this unit's two modules) and the scratch LQ-F upstream design-v2, and some section numbers exist in both. The status paragraph now names the LQ-F upstream design-v2 and its file and introduces the short form "LQ-F design-v2", which every unit D section, the item 2 guide's section and the docstrings of both modules now use; no bare "design-v2" is left in unit D's text.
- Nit (hygiene): the implementer's root and inventory checks all ran on trees with uncommitted changes. "LQ-F upstream unit D: checks" and the receipt (`review.reviewer_checks`) now record the hygiene review's clean-tree checks at `28230554` (`check-inventory` under lease 00000444, exit 0, clean before and after; the root olean `3ecd462c`, byte-identical to the D2 commit's; the module oleans `34202d1a` and `70f17b6e`, byte-identical to the implementer's), and this fix pass's own checks below ran on the clean tree of its Lean commit.
- Nit (hygiene): the contamination scan, the closure check and the design probes are scratch files under `/tmp`, which the analysis and the guide did not say. The checks section now says that they are scratch metaprograms, recorded there and in the receipt, not committed checks (as item 2's precedent says), and the analysis entry says the same in a clause.
- Nit (hygiene): `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterGame.postNative_real` has the same short name as `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentReal.postNative_real`, which has a different statement (the real registration). Its docstring and the D2 module docstring now call it the generic form of that lemma, and "names for consumers" says that unit C writes the qualified names.
- Nit (hygiene): the selective `open PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure (… xreveal)` of D2 named `xreveal`, which the module never uses. It is dropped.
- Nit (hygiene): "A2" named both unit A2 and theorem A2 in the same texts. The D1 module docstring, "LQ-F upstream unit D: checks" and the item 2 guide's section now write "theorem A2" for the theorem and "unit A2" (or "units A1 and A2") for the unit; the analysis names no theorem A2.
- Nit (hygiene): the D1 module docstring said that no definition "imports" a DH-keyed component, although the imports pull DH modules in transitively.
