## LQ-F upstream unit C: what is proved

Unit C adds four modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 53 guarded theorems: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLater` (unit C1, 18), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterEnv` (unit C2, 16), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSlots` (unit C3, 13) and `PqxdhDeploymentLaterTargetRange` (unit C4, 6); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains four imports). In the unit C sections, "unit C1" to "unit C4" name these four parts (LQ-F design-v2 section 4.2), and the bare labels C1 to C7 name CE-11's hand-off obligations (for example C1, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv.c1_split` for the ideal environment; C4, the budget obligation `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.c4_later`; C6, `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.join_later`). Every theorem depends on exactly `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`. The new definitions are the predicates `QuietQuery`, `CoinEffect` and `HmacCoins` (unit C3) and the projection `eligibleL` (unit C4). The statements are equalities of programs, equalities in law (`LawEq`), support facts and bounds by the probability of a public event; they charge nothing beyond the terms of the existing theorems they compose, and LQ-F claims confidentiality only.

The main statements:

- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterEnv.ideal_to_split_later` (CE-11b's split chain for the later game, pointwise in the target `t` and the challenger's draws `p`): `LawEq (outcomeMap splitView <$> (laterWith … idealInit (idealReg … t p) (realPost …) …).run runtime) (outcomeMap splitView <$> (laterWith … splitInit (idealSplit … t p) (realPost …) …).run runtime)`, through `erase_ideal_later`, `represent_ideal_later`, `seed_move_later`, `ideal_to_splitX_later`, `ideal_to_splitEager_later` and `split_later`, each the later form of the CE-11b step of the same name.
- `c6_split` (C6 for the ideal environment of a target at the full reassembled state): `LawEq (drawParams setup count >>= fun p => (·.map fun v => (v.1, v.2.1, v.2.2.1, withX p v.2.2.2)) <$> (laterWith … splitInit (idealSplit … t p) (realPost …) …).run' runtime) (joined <$> PqxdhRecordLiftLater.run … (envGD … t …) setup.serverKeyId bounds mode envReveal first second)`.
- `c6_ideal`, `c6_ideal_public` and `c6_idealGame`: the draw followed by the later game with the ideal registration and the real post phase (for `c6_idealGame`, unit D's `IL_g` itself at `Target.ofCE budget g` and identity index 0) has the law of the later record world with `envGD` and the environment's reveal at the observed projection (the post-run output, the run-phase ledger and the produced frames) and, for `c6_ideal_public`, at the public projection (also the Server's public data, the record view and the Beacon-side reveal with the Server ephemeral log). `c6_idealGame_decide`: `𝒟[ceDecideL … g <$> (idealLaterGame … budget g …).run runtime] {true} = 𝒟[(fun result => (PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.observed result).any (decisionOn … g)) <$> PqxdhRecordLiftLater.run … (envGD … (Target.ofCE budget g) ensureInit 0 runtime) … envReveal first second] {true}`, so the right side of unit D's `IdealCompileServerTarget` is the probability of CE-11b's hand-off decision on the later record world.
- `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSlots.post_noinstall` (U-L4): under every private interpreter, the compiled post phase of `envGD`, from every environment state, control and private state, keeps the record control on every outcome, so the handle-to-slot map after the post phase is the one after the run phase. `later_slots`: under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, on every outcome of the all-real later chain world `laterRealChains` with `envGD`, for every reveal and post-run adversary, the view's handle-to-slot map satisfies `IdealSlots` for the run-phase transcript, or that transcript has the Beacon-side `Anomaly`. `later_view_slots_le`: for every event `D` of the view, `𝒟[D on laterRealChains] {true} ≤ 𝒟[D ∧ IdealSlots on laterRealChains] {true} + 𝒟[Anomaly on PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.run … first] {true}`, with the anomaly bounded by `ctGuessByCca` of the anomaly guesser of the first adversary (`later_view_slots_pk`) or by `ctGuess` (`later_view_slots_ct`).
- `PqxdhDeploymentLaterTargetRange.range_game_create_later`: on every outcome of the LQ-F game of a run-phase budget-respecting adversary, with every post-run adversary, every designation `(session, k)` of any handle has `session < qAccept` and `k < qCreate`. `kem_transfer_server_create_two_sided`: the probability that the LQ-F game ends with the event and the claim designated at all (plain eligibility `eligibleL`) and the sum over `g` in `Fin qAccept × Fin qCreate` of the logged opaque KEM-ideal branch of `(g.1, g.2.castSucc)` differ by at most the sum over the same targets of one IND-CCA advantage and one correctness error, the terms of `PqxdhDeploymentLaterKemTwoSided.kem_transfer_server_two_sided`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLater` (unit C1, generic) | 18 | Transport of the later game along a map of the extended state: `laterTail_map` (the post phase from a mapped run result), `laterWith_map` (exact, the run phases related command by command) and `laterWith_proj` (in law, the run phases related in law per constructed Server). The represent step with the real post phase: `xreveal_mapBackend` (`rfl`), `realPost_mapBackend`, `represent_run` (the run-phase step of `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.represent`), `represent_later`, `represent_split_later`. Seed relocation: `realPost_moveSeeds`, `game_moveSeeds_later`. The parameter in the backend: `postNative_param`, `laterWith_param`. C6 with drawn parameters: `run_split_from` (`PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.run_split` from any World state), `join_later_from` (`join_later` from a World state whose counts alone differ from the initial World), `later_program_draw`, `join_later_draw`, `join_later_param`. The seed deferral with lists of points: `defer_seeds_later` (from the LQ-F design-v2 probe `DeferL.lean`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterEnv` (unit C2, instances) | 16 | The split chain for the later game (`erase_ideal_later`, `represent_ideal_later`, `seed_move_later`, `ideal_to_splitX_later`, `ideal_to_splitEager_later`, `split_later`, `ideal_to_split_later`); the post phase of the final split form (`idealSplit_hmac`, `postNative_split`); C6 (`c6_split`, `c6_ideal`, `c6_ideal_public`, `c6_idealGame`); the decision of the later second compile lemma on the observed projection (`ceDecideL_observed`, the later form of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff.ceDecide_observed`) and `c6_idealGame_decide`; U-L3's reveal constraint `envReveal_blind` (`rfl`: `envReveal` reads neither the backend nor the shared native state, hence neither the hidden-slot material nor item 2's R1 slice). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSlots` (unit C3, U-L4) | 13 | Quiet World requests (coin effects and the shared BLAKE2b capability) keep the record control (`handler_quiet`, `schedule_quiet`); coins and lifted coin effects are quiet (`coins_coinEffect`, `effectLift_quiet`); the post phase of an environment whose HMAC step draws coins only (`HmacCoins`) is quiet (`post_quiet`), and `envGD` is such an environment (`envGD_hmacCoins`); `post_noinstall`, `continueWith_noinstall` (the E-only continuation of `PqxdhRecordLiftLaterWorlds` keeps the control, the Server's public data and the run-phase ledger), `later_slots`, `later_anomaly_eq` (the anomaly on the later view has the probability of the anomaly on the run-phase record world of the first adversary, through `PqxdhRecordLiftLater.run_event`), `later_view_slots_le`, `later_view_slots_pk`, `later_view_slots_ct`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentLaterTargetRange` (unit C4, F15) | 6 | `range_game_create_later` (the event "some designation is out of range" reads only the ledger, so `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.game_classical_event` moves it to the classical experiment, where it is impossible by `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange.range_game_create`), `range_game_later`, `eligibleL` with `eligible_designated_later`, `designated_create_later`, `split_create_later` and `kem_transfer_server_create_two_sided` (the LQ-F forms of `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange.eligible_designated`, `designated_create`, `PqxdhDeploymentKemTwoSided.split_create` and `kem_transfer_create_two_sided`). |

## LQ-F upstream unit C: decisions and deviations

- The fixed real post phase (LQ-F design-v2 D5). Every later game of the split chain runs with `realPost`, which runs on the deployment state only, so it commutes with every map of the backend, the seed table or the CTX cache that the chain uses (`realPost_mapBackend`, `realPost_moveSeeds`, `realPost_commutes`, `realPost_commutes_seed`), and the C6 statements are bridged to the `postNative` form of `join_later` by `postNative_ideal` (unit D) and `postNative_split`. `c6_split` is stated with `realPost`, not with `postNative` of the final split form.
- Generic transport lemmas. LQ-F design-v2 lists the later steps one by one; the unit proves them through three transport lemmas (`laterTail_map`, `laterWith_map`, `laterWith_proj`), so each later step is the classical run-phase step plus the commutation of the post handler and the blindness of the reveal. `represent_run` exposes the run-phase step that `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.represent` proves inline.
- C6 from a counted World state. `join_later_draw` needs `join_later` from a World state whose counts differ from the initial World (the draw's coins are counted), which `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.join_later` does not state; `join_later_from` is its proof with that World state (as `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw.join_from` is for `join`), with `run_split_from`, and `later_program_draw` splits the later program of an environment that draws first. LQ-F design-v2's `join_later_draw` takes the startup equation `born : env.start = draw >>= …`, as `join_draw` does.
- U-L4's route. LQ-F design-v2 section 4.2 builds `post_noinstall` from `PqxdhRecordLiftLaterWorlds.laterChains_split`, `post_effects` and `effects_post`. Those show that the compiled post phase requests only seed allocation, coin, hash and charge services, which does not exclude a change of the handle-to-slot map (an `installServer` capability may allocate a seed and install a hidden kernel). The unit proves the stronger fact for `envGD`: its post phase makes only quiet requests, because its HMAC step is the real HMAC step, which draws coins only, so the record control is unchanged. `laterChains_split` is used in `later_slots`; `laterReal_event` is not needed for the support statement, and `later_anomaly_eq` uses `PqxdhRecordLiftLater.run_event` with `laterReal_source` instead.
- Additions to LQ-F design-v2's list: in unit C1, `laterTail_map`, `laterWith_map`, `laterWith_proj`, `represent_run`, `realPost_moveSeeds`, `postNative_param`, `run_split_from`, `join_later_from` and `later_program_draw`; in unit C2, `idealSplit_hmac` (the premise of `postNative_real` for the final split form), `ceDecideL_observed` and `c6_idealGame_decide` (the decision form that the later `ideal_world` composition needs); in unit C3, the quiet-request lemmas and `HmacCoins`, `continueWith_noinstall`, `later_anomaly_eq` and the three `later_view_slots_*` bounds (the later forms of `ideal_world_view_slots`'s split and of its two anomaly bounds); in unit C4, `range_game_later`, `eligibleL`, `eligible_designated_later` and `split_create_later`. `kem_transfer_server_create_two_sided` is stated with plain eligibility, as `kem_transfer_create_two_sided` is.
- Imports. Unit C2 imports `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff` for `decisionOn`; unit C3 imports `PqxdhDeploymentIdealHandoff`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` and `PqxdhRecordLiftLaterWorlds` and does not depend on units C1 or C2; unit C4 imports `PqxdhDeploymentLaterKemTwoSided` and `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange` and depends on no other module of the unit.
- No item 2 content. Unit C states nothing about item 2 over the logged source, so the item 2 guide `doc/impl/extract-composition.md` gets no section from this unit; LQ-F design-v2 places C's guide sections here (CE-11b-L).
- Publication: one Lean commit per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block (`inv recount` before `inv rehash`), its own root import check and its own focused inventory check; then one documentation commit. Olean prefix: a fresh registered prefix `lqf-up/C/olean` (a resolved symlink mirror of the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `c031e4e7`, `lqf-up/D/int/olean`, without the root olean, 6354 links) receives the four module oleans. The review fix pass added one Lean commit and one documentation commit (section "LQ-F upstream unit C: review fix pass"), with its own fresh registered olean prefix `lqf-up/C/fix/olean` (the same kind of mirror); `lqf-up/C/olean` keeps the unit's oleans. One more documentation commit records the confirming review of `e9a9d3da` ("LQ-F upstream unit C: confirming review"). The integration fast-forwards central to the branch's checked tip (status paragraph).

## LQ-F upstream unit C: names for consumers

- CE-12-L (P3): `c6_idealGame_decide` (with unit B2's `ideal_compile_server`, the target `IdealCompileServerTarget`, it gives the later `ideal_world`: item 2's logged two-slot world of the compiled LQ-F program and the later record world with `envGD` give the per-target decision the same probability; the composition goes into P3's chain module, as U7 did for the classical chain), `c6_idealGame`, `c6_ideal`, `c6_ideal_public`, `post_noinstall`, `later_slots`, `later_anomaly_eq`, `later_view_slots_le`, `later_view_slots_pk`, `later_view_slots_ct` (with the record lift's later forms `later_record_lift`, `later_idealRecords_fresh`, `later_idealRecords_lor` for CE-APP-L and CE-LOR-L), `kem_transfer_server_create_two_sided`, `range_game_create_later`, `designated_create_later`, `eligibleL`, `eligible_designated_later`, `split_create_later` (CE-KT-L over the tight target domain).
- Units B1 and B2 and later work on the later game: the generic tools `laterTail_map`, `laterWith_map`, `laterWith_proj`, `join_later_from`, `join_later_draw`, `join_later_param`, `defer_seeds_later`, and `ideal_to_split_later` with `postNative_split`.
- Same short names with different statements in other namespaces: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterEnv.idealSplit_hmac` (the final split form's HMAC is the native HMAC on the deployment state with the rest of the extended state kept) beside `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvStep.idealSplit_hmac` (it is `realRegAt`'s HMAC; the unit C2 lemma is that lemma composed with unit D's `realRegAt_hmac`, proved directly); `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSlots.handler_quiet` beside `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.handler_quiet` (and two older `handler_quiet` lemmas); `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSlots.post_quiet` beside `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompile.post_quiet`. Consumers that open several of these namespaces write qualified names. The other names of the unit are new.

## LQ-F upstream unit C: contracts used

`Phase1ReadBack codec` and `Phase2ReadBack codec`, approved on 2026-10-06: in `later_slots` and the three `later_view_slots_*` bounds (through `ideal_view_slots` and the anomaly bounds) and in all of unit C4 (through `range_game_create`). `FunctionalDecapsulation setup.kem`, the existing contract of the KEM hop that the owner allowed in the top-level statement: in `kem_transfer_server_create_two_sided` only (through `kem_transfer_server_two_sided`). The run-phase budget `budget.Respects first`: in unit C4. Units C1 and C2 and the rest of unit C3 take no premise beyond their arguments' shapes. No new contract and no new assumption (LQ-F design-v2 section 10).

## LQ-F upstream unit C: open points

1. Review and integration. Reviewed at `f7d26887` by a semantic and a hygiene review (focus: the later steps are the classical steps with the post-handler commutation and the reveal's blindness; `post_noinstall` uses the quiet-request route instead of LQ-F design-v2's `post_effects`; the C6 statements' projections; unit C4's use of `game_classical_event`), both approving with nits; every finding is resolved by the review fix pass (section "LQ-F upstream unit C: review fix pass"). The confirming review of the fix pass at `e9a9d3da` is recorded in "LQ-F upstream unit C: confirming review". The commit that records it replaced the "not integrated" sentences of the status paragraph and of the analysis entry with the integration's route, and the integration message records its outcome (updated by that commit; this point first said that the integration replaces them with its record). Central was `c031e4e7` throughout, so no merge was needed at the time of the fix pass.
2. The later `ideal_world` (unit B2's `ideal_compile_server` composed with `c6_idealGame_decide`) and the CE-12-L headlines are P3's; they wait for units A2 and B2.
3. The contamination scan reports one direct reference that item 2's list excludes: `kem_transfer_server_create_two_sided` takes `PqxdhChallengeSecretRouting.FunctionalDecapsulation`, the existing contract of the KEM hop (a functional decapsulation interface, not a DH-keyed component), exactly as `kem_transfer_server_two_sided` does. Item 2's list excludes the whole `PqxdhChallengeSecretRouting` namespace because item 2's own proofs must not use it; unit C4 is the KEM transfer, not item 2. No other excluded or DH, authentication, T5 or T8 name is referenced.
4. The working plan's record of this unit is left to the plan's owner (the orchestrator); this unit did not edit the plan or LQ-F design-v2.

## LQ-F upstream unit C: re-estimate

Figures in nominal agent-days. LQ-F design-v2 section 6 estimated unit C at 2-2.75 (build 1-1.5, review to integration 1-1.25) and 45-70 theorems. The build took about 0.1 with 53 theorems: every step is a classical CE-11b or CE-9b proof with the post phase added, the probe `DeferL.lean` carried `defer_seeds_later` and `split_later`, and the four modules compiled in a few seconds each. Review to integration is estimated at 0.25-0.5, as unit D's took. No other unit's estimate changes.

## LQ-F upstream unit C: checks

- Each module, after its guards were added, through `coord.sh lean` (Lean 4.34, `-j1 -M8000`, `timeout 180`, `-o` into `lqf-up/C/olean`) against `lqf-up/C/leanpath` (that prefix, then the published `LEAN_PATH` of central `c031e4e7`): exit 0 and an empty log each (oleans `712bb89d`, `5c3305d0`, `98b84495`, `dc580ccd`). Guards 53 of 53 (18, 16, 13 and 6), generated from a `#print axioms` probe of every theorem; every theorem depends on exactly `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.
- At each Lean commit's tree (the commit's changes uncommitted, the rest equal to the previous commit): the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` through `coord.sh lean` against the same `LEAN_PATH` (exit 0, empty log each); `coord.sh inv recount` (1895 to 1896, 1896 to 1897, 1897 to 1898, 1898 to 1899) before `coord.sh inv rehash` (the root and `check-inventory.sh` each time; `trusted-boundary.md` unchanged); the focused `check-inventory` in the locked proof shell under a `@nix` lease (leases 00000454, 00000456, 00000460, 00000463; exit 0 each); `git diff --cached --check` (clean). ## LQ-F upstream unit C: review fix pass

An independent review of `f7d26887` in two parts (semantic and hygiene) found no error in the Lean statements or proofs; both parts approved with nits. The semantic part recompiled the four modules without `-o` through the coordination wrapper (exit 0, empty logs); checked that the statements of unit C1 equal the LQ-F design-v2 probes (`Stmts.lean`, `Stmts2.lean`, `DeferL.lean`) up to binder names, that unit C2's chain mirrors CE-11b's `ideal_to_split` with the same views (`fullX`, `mapBackend`, `moveSeeds`, `splitView`, `seedFree`) and that the C6 statements mirror `c1_split`, `c1_ideal` and `c1_idealGame` with the later run and `envReveal` (`c6_ideal_public` with the projection of central's `join_later_public`, the left side of `c6_idealGame_decide` literally the right side of unit D's `IdealCompileServerTarget`); found unit C3's quiet-request route justified and stronger than LQ-F design-v2's `post_effects` route, and unit C4's use of `game_classical_event` at a ledger-only event and of `FunctionalDecapsulation` only in the transfer correct; and found that all 57 qualified names cited in the module docstrings exist. The hygiene part rebuilt the four modules and the root at the clean `f7d26887` with oleans byte-identical to the implementer's; scanned the modules (18, 16, 13 and 6 theorems with as many exact guards in declaration order, allowed axioms only, `set_option` only `autoImplicit false`); checked the inventory at every commit of the range (handwritten Lean files 1895 to 1899), the receipt's hashes and lists (114 checks, no issue), the footers, the registry, the per-commit scopes and the short-name collisions; reran the closure check with the recorded result; ran its own module-wide contamination scan; and ran the focused `check-inventory` on the clean tree at `f7d26887` (exit 0). They raised one minor finding and seven nits. This fix pass resolves all eight with one Lean commit (`6e03082b`: three unused `open` names and one clause of the unit C1 module docstring, with `inv recount` and `inv rehash`) and one documentation commit.

- Minor (semantic): the analysis entry said that, because the environment's reveal reads neither the hidden-slot material nor the shared HMAC cache, "the post-run adversary learns nothing from item 2's idealized random-oracle slice through the reveal". `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterEnv.envReveal_blind` is an `rfl` fact about what `envReveal` reads (`envReveal { e with backend := b, native := { e.native with shared := shared } } = envReveal e`), not a statistical independence, and LQ-F design-v2 section 5 (2) says that the reveal may depend on the candidate through hidden-read answers (whether owner k's challenge finish succeeds decides whether its one-time scalar is captured in `snap`, which `revealOf` reads). No statement changes. The analysis now says that the reveal function does not read the hidden-slot material or the shared HMAC cache, so item 2's idealized slice is never handed over directly, and that the slice can still affect the reveal through the course of the run (whether a finish succeeds), which item 2's argument allows. The docstring of `envReveal_blind` and the table of "LQ-F upstream unit C: what is proved" state only the read-independence fact and are unchanged.
- Nit (semantic): the analysis entry said that each rewrite of the chain changes only hidden state and that the post-run phase runs on the deployment state, "which the rewrites do not touch". The C7 step (`erase_ideal_later`, through `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure.erase_later_ctx`) erases an entry of the CTX cache, which `ctxLens` (`baseLens.comp nativeCtxLens`) focuses inside the deployment state; the proof covers that step with `realPost_commutes` (the real post phase commutes with completions of the CTX cache) and `xreveal_ctx` (the reveal does not see the CTX cache), not with disjointness. The analysis now says that the rewrites either leave the deployment state alone or change only its CTX cache, and that completing an entry of that cache before or after the post-run phase gives the same result and the reveal does not see it. "LQ-F upstream unit C: decisions and deviations" already said this and is unchanged.
- Nit (semantic): the analysis entry said that the two sides "agree on the Server's public data and on what is revealed". `c6_ideal_public` projects to the Server's public data, the post-run output, the run-phase ledger, the record view (produced frames and raw-attempt counters) and the Beacon-side reveal with the Server ephemeral log (`revealOf owners snap` and `log`); the Server-identity-seed component `some created.seed` of the full reveal is not in the projection, and the Server's public data (`ClientCreated`) does not contain the seed. This is the projection of central's `join_later_public`, whose docstring says that the second adversary's view of the seed is covered inside `join_later`; the docstring of `c6_ideal_public` and "what is proved" already name the projection exactly. The analysis now names the record view, the Beacon-side reveal and the Server's ephemeral log, says that the seed is not among these components, and says that the equal laws of the post-run adversary's output already account for the seed it receives. No statement changes; a consumer that needs the seed component in the public projection gets a new statement beside `c6_ideal_public` in a later unit.
- Nit (hygiene): three opened names were never used: `paramStep` in unit C1's `open PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw (…)`, `RegImpl` in unit C2's `open PqxdhDeploymentBackend (…)` and `Capability` in unit C3's `open PqxdhRecordLiftInterface (…)` (each name occurred only in its `open` line; unit C4 had none). They are dropped (`6e03082b`), the three modules and the root are rechecked, `inv recount` and `inv rehash` are run and the focused `check-inventory` passes (checks below).
- Nit (hygiene): the bare labels C1 to C4 named both the four parts of this unit and CE-11's hand-off obligations (C1 `join` and `c1_split`, C3 `c3_shared_cache`, C4 the budget obligation `c4_later`, C6 `join_later`) in the same texts. Every unit C section of this guide now writes "unit C1" to "unit C4" for the parts (the intro of "what is proved", the table, the additions and imports bullets of the decisions, names for consumers, contracts used and open points 1 and 3) and keeps bare C1, C5, C6 and C7 for the obligations, and the intro of "what is proved" now says so; the receipt's `scope` and `assumptions` fields do the same. The unit C1 module docstring already wrote "unit C1" for the part; it now writes "the hand-off obligation C1" and "the obligation C1" where it means the obligation (`6e03082b`). The receipt's per-module field `unit` keeps its values `C1` to `C4`, which the field name qualifies, and the subjects of the four Lean commits already say "unit C1" to "unit C4".
- Nit (hygiene): every implementer `check-inventory` and root run was on a tree with the commit's changes uncommitted (four dirty paths at each Lean commit, three on the documentation tree). "LQ-F upstream unit C: checks" and the receipt (`review.reviewer_checks`) now record the hygiene review's clean-tree runs at `f7d26887` (`check-inventory` under lease 00000468, 06:12:37 to 06:14:26 UTC, exit 0, empty working-tree status before and after; the four module oleans and the root olean `a42a653a` byte-identical to the implementer's) and its scripted per-commit inventory check, and this fix pass's own checks below ran on the clean tree of its Lean commit.
- Nit (hygiene): the source-hygiene claim listed `opaque` among the absent tokens, but the word occurs in one unit C4 docstring ("the logged opaque KEM-ideal branch") and inside names such as `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerOpaque.opaqueBranch`; what is absent is an `opaque` declaration. "LQ-F upstream unit C: checks" and the receipt (`checks.source_hygiene`) now say "no `opaque` declaration" and record that the policy regex of the Makefile's `check-lean-policy` finds nothing in the four modules or the root.
- Nit (hygiene, no change needed): the implementer's contamination list (item 2's fixed-HKDF list) is narrower than the wording of item 2's risk 9, which forbids the programming lemmas of `PqxdhHiddenRoot` and `PqxdhJointKdfGame`; the hygiene review's module-wide scan gives the same result (its only other hit, `PqxdhJointKdfGame.FixedHkdfSha512NoSaltSource.crypto`, is the first field of the type of `Setup.source`, used throughout central, not a programming lemma). The optional note is taken: "LQ-F upstream unit C: checks" and the receipt (`checks.contamination`) now record the narrower list and the module-wide scan's result, and this fix pass reran that scan against its own oleans with a log identical to the review's.

Statements before and after. None changed. The Lean diff `f7d26887..6e03082b` drops one name from each of three selective `open` commands, which no declaration uses, and changes one clause of the unit C1 module docstring in two places ("of C1 with drawn parameters" to "of the hand-off obligation C1 with drawn parameters", and "the parameter in the backend and C1 for an environment" to "the parameter in the backend and the obligation C1 for an environment"). Every theorem statement, definition body and proof is byte-identical, no theorem or definition was added or removed, and the 53 theorems keep their exact guards (18, 16, 13 and 6; the guard text is unchanged and passes, each allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`). ## LQ-F upstream unit A1: what is proved

Unit A1 carries item 2's stages S0a, S0b, S1b and S1c to the logged source of LQ-F, on unit D1's frozen definitions. The logged opaque KEM-ideal branch (theorem T6 for the logged source, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerOpaque.opaqueBranch`) equals the router-free logged branch (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemFree.opaqueBranchL_freeRouter`), is G1's shared world on `sharedStartL` and `sharedClientL` (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemShared.opaqueBranchL_shared`, the Lean form of the T7 condition for LQ-F), and is within `(qAccept + qFinish + qHmac)/2^256` of G1's split world under the logged source budget (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemSplit.opaqueBranchL_split`; the compiled LQ-F program meets that budget at the transported caps, whose HMAC cap is `qHmac + qHmac₂` (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompileBudget.compile_server_certified`), and at these caps the three caps add up to `qAccept + qFinish + (qHmac + qHmac₂)` (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemTargets.later_guess_term`), so post-run HMAC queries are charged with coefficient one). The statements, the proof structure and the post-run HMAC accounting are in the item 2 guide, section "Unit A1: stages S0a to S1c over the logged source". The classical per-request lemmas of item 2 are applied unchanged through a generic lifting lemma (`logClient_step`); the only new per-request facts are those of the logged accept. No assumption, contract or premise is added.

## LQ-F upstream unit A1: decisions, deviations and checks

The deviations from LQ-F design-v2 (49 theorems against an estimate of 90 to 115, a few names, `commandsL_cut` on the logged command run, and the post-run HMAC queries of a run whose selected accept is never reached or rejects before encapsulation, for which G1's charge is zero instead of counting them as prefix keys; the design's case of a reached accept leaves out an active accept with another KEM key, where the branch stops without running the post phase) and the checks (per-module finals with `-o`, root import checks, focused `check-inventory` under `@nix` leases 00000457, 00000459 and 00000462, exact axiom guards on all 49 theorems, the contamination scan, the candidate-freedom closure check and a short-name scan over the root import) are recorded in the item 2 guide, sections "Unit A1: deviations from LQ-F design-v2" and "Unit A1: checks", and in the receipt; the review of `312451cb` and its fix pass (finals into `lqf-up/A1/fix/olean`, focused `check-inventory` under lease 00000475 on the clean Lean commit `658828a3`) are in "Unit A1: review fix pass" there, and the confirming review of `6cd90022` (rebuilds byte-identical to the fix pass's, focused `check-inventory` under lease 00000480 on the clean tip `6cd90022`) in "Unit A1: confirming review". Open points: the integration (status paragraph); then unit A2 (S2 to S8), which closes open point 3 of `doc/impl/later-quantum-lqf-spike.md` (item 2 over the logged source).

## LQ-F upstream unit A2: what is proved

Unit A2 carries item 2's stages S2, T, S3b, S7 and S8 to the logged source of LQ-F, on unit D1's frozen definitions, and so proves the U-L1 targets. For every logged-source program within a logged source budget and under `SourceFullCorrect`, the logged opaque KEM-ideal branch and the logged two-slot world `laterSlotGame … 2` are distinguishable with advantage at most `slotLossL … budget` (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemKeys.opaqueBranchL_slots_two`, which proves `OpaqueBranchSlotsTwoTargetL`); at the compiled LQ-F program, for a run-phase adversary within its budget and a post-run adversary within its post budget, the same holds at the transported caps (`later_slots_two`, which proves `LaterSlotsTwoTarget` through `laterSlotsTwo_of_generic`), where the first term of `slotLossL` is `(qAccept + qFinish + qHmac + qHmac₂)/2^256` (unit D1's `slotLossL_transported`). The other terms are two one-query HMAC-SHA-512 PRF advantages of `rootClientL`'s reductions (stage S2 with stage T's two root requests, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemOnce.rootClientL_two`, which needs no premise) and G2's four key-schedule advantages on each of `entryDkL 0` and `entryDkL 1` (stage S3b's `uniformGameL_entry` and the generic hybrid of stage S7, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemHybrid.slotOf_telescope`). The unit also proves theorem T7 over the logged source (`opaqueBranchL_idealKeys_two`, whose bound has the four key-schedule advantages of the first hidden output only) and the bills of every reduction, built from unit D1's frozen coin bill `rootBillL` plus fixed constants, with the instance at the compiled program and the transported caps (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemBills.laterSlot_bills_compiled`; the Server read weighs nothing, and its cap enters no bill). The statements, the proof structure, the bills and the post-run HMAC accounting are in the item 2 guide, section "Unit A2: stages S2 to S8 over the logged source". The classical per-request facts of item 2 are applied unchanged on source14 requests; the new cases are the logged accept and the Server read. No assumption, contract or premise is added. The sharpened R1 caveat (LQ-F design-v2 section 5) is in the docstrings of the targets' proofs.

## LQ-F upstream unit A2: decisions, deviations and checks

The deviations from LQ-F design-v2 (87 theorems against an estimate of 101 to 135; the `1 + qFinish` per-request hidden counts of the Root module not built, the root-count lemmas stated for any bound on the hidden reads instead; no new game definition for the ideal-key and all-ideal worlds; the frozen `Prop` targets proved by name besides `opaqueBranchL_slots_two` and `later_slots_two`; a logged grouped weight under which the Server read weighs 0; additional names; the Hybrid commit's message counts 10 theorems where the module has 9) and the checks (per-module finals with `-o`, root import checks, focused `check-inventory` under `@nix` leases 00000490, 00000491, 00000492, 00000495, 00000497 and 00000499, exact axiom guards on all 87 theorems, the contamination scan, the candidate-freedom closure check, a short-name scan over the root import and a scan of the selectively opened names) are recorded in the item 2 guide, sections "Unit A2: deviations from LQ-F design-v2" and "Unit A2: checks", and in the receipt; the review of `57d046ed` and its fix pass (finals into `lqf-up/A2/fix/olean`, focused `check-inventory` under lease 00000507 on the clean Lean commit `d2fe186e`) are in "Unit A2: review fix pass" there, with the integration notes, and the confirming review of `5cf0a1af` (focused `check-inventory` under lease 00000511 on the clean tip `5cf0a1af`) in "Unit A2: confirming review". Open points: the integration (status paragraph), at which the merge of central `f0dc46bb` must be followed by `inv recount` (1914 handwritten Lean files; the count line merges without a conflict at 1908) and `inv rehash`; open point 3 of `doc/impl/later-quantum-lqf-spike.md` (item 2 over the logged source) is settled by units A1 and A2 for LQ-F, which needs the R1 split, T7, the key-schedule hops and stage S7 but not T8 (confidentiality only: T8 (its `authenticated_*` forms, the composites with T5) and the key-first stage S6 are not carried over), and can be closed there when unit A2 is integrated.

## LQ-F upstream unit B: what is proved

Unit B adds eight modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 145 guarded theorems: unit B1 `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterParams` (3), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterPool` (29), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterCut` (24) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterBranch` (21); unit B2 `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterMid` (27), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSteps` (28), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterPost` (12) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterCompile` (1). No existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains eight imports). Every theorem depends on axioms within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`: 144 theorems on all three, and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterPool.onBase_view` on `propext` and `Quot.sound` only (the first wording of this sentence said "exactly" the three; corrected in the review fix pass). The statements are program equalities, equalities in law (`LawEq`), support facts and query bounds; they charge nothing, and LQ-F claims confidentiality only.

The main statement is `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterCompile.ideal_compile_server : PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterGame.IdealCompileServerTarget`, unit D2's frozen target unchanged: under `Phase1ReadBack codec` and `Phase2ReadBack codec`, for every run-phase adversary `first` with `budget.Respects first` and every post-run adversary `second`, `𝒟[laterSlotGame … (PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerTransfer.decision … g) (compileServer … first second) runtime budget.create (selectedKem budget g) g.1.val 2] {true} = 𝒟[ceDecideL … g <$> (idealLaterGame … budget g ensureInit bounds mode first second).run runtime] {true}`. No post budget is taken.

The route is CE-11b unit U6's, carried to the logged source and the later game:

- The draws (`…Params`): `laterSlotGame_pool`, item 2's logged two-slot world has the law of the challenger's draws `drawParams` followed by the same world at the draws (`draws_swapL`, `preOpaque_poolL`).
- The pool (`…Pool`): the logged handlers with the KEM creations reading the challenger's pool on every layer of the chain (deployment side `handlerPoolL`, `snapPoolL`; source14 side `nativePoolL`, `compiledPoolL`; annotated side `sourcePoolL`), the logged pool compile lemma `compile_simulation_poolL` (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompile.server_run` with the pool create), item 2's logged commands under the reserved services (`program_reservedL`, `kemCommands_stoppedL`), the ideal registration before the cut on the LQ-F view (`preStepL`, `preScanL`, the ghosts kept), and the stopped router with identity reveals allowed (`stopped_programL`).
- The cut (`…Cut`): the logged scan of the compiled LQ-F program is the embedded compiled CE scan of the run part followed by the rest (`compile_scanServer`, with `runPart`, `restOf` and `compileServer_split`); the CE side cut at the selected raw accept with the LQ-F ghosts (`ideal_cutL`, `ideal_prefixL`, with `cutStateL`, `markedL`, `resumeL` and `commonPrefixL`); item 2's side cut at the same accept (`item2_prefixL`, with `opaqueAfterL`); and the relation at the cut `CutRelL` (`cut_relL`).
- The branches (`…Branch`): the later decision reads the mark (`keymatch_later`, `game_markedL`, through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeymatch.ideal_keymatch` and `laterWith_split`); the branches without an active cut accept nothing on both sides (`itemPostL_failed`, `itemRestL_dead`, `resumeL_dead`); the selected-key branch begins with the native steps before encapsulation on both sides (`itemRestL_selected`, `resumeL_selected`); and `ideal_compile_server_of_active` proves the target's equation from the active branch `ActiveBranchL` at every draw (through `ideal_compile_server_of_selected` and `selected_of_activeL`).
- The CE layer of the middle run (`…Mid`): the logged middle source handler `sourceMidL` and the compiled handler over it `compiledMidL`; the LQ-F ghosts of the handler with the ideal registration (`idealStep_ghost`: every request but a raw accept writes the finish snapshot and keeps the log, and reads neither; `handler_acceptOther`: a raw accept other than the selected one appends its logged entry); `step_mergeMidL` and `compile_simulationMidL` (the merge image `mergeXL`, with the snapshot and the log, of the compiled middle run is the handler's run; CE-11b's `step_mergeMid` with the ghosts, and the logged accept through `accept_merge`), with the invariant `GoodMidL` kept by every compiled step (`goodMidL_step`); and the rest of the compiled LQ-F program (`restOf_middle`, `laterTail_middle`: the terminal reads, the Server read and the post part under the logged middle source handler are the real post phase on the reveal of the merge image).
- Item 2's side (`…Steps`): item 2's logged layers over the logged middle source handler (`item_simL`, from `step_allL` and `keep_allL`: CE-11b's per-request steps lifted with the log kept, the Server read, raw accepts other than the selected one and identity field reads, the last through `lowerE_poolL`, CE-11b's `lowerE_pool` with identity reveals allowed), the logged skeleton with both erasures (`itemPostL_clean`), and `itemPost_middleL`: item 2's logged post-challenge phase on an active cut is the logged middle run from the state after the selected accept, with the log at the cut extended by the accept's ephemeral.
- The active branch (`…Post`): `activeCE_middleL`, the CE side of the active branch is a function of the same logged middle run, read off through the merge image with the mark replaced by whether the selected accept returned a session; `decision_mergeL`, the two decisions agree on the merge image; the bounds of the logged continuation (`restOf_allowed`, `restOf_costly`, with the run part's bounds from `CutRelL.residual`); the start after the selected accept (`start_goodL`, `start_itemL`, `run_slotL`, `acceptAfter_logged`); and `active_branchL`. `…Compile` composes `ideal_compile_server_of_active` with `active_branchL`.

## LQ-F upstream unit B: decisions and deviations

- The spike B0 (LQ-F design-v2 section 4.5) closed all three items in under half an hour in scratch (`lqf-up/B/probe/Spike.lean`), so the stop rule held and B proceeded on the U6-reuse route at the design's budget. Item (i) came out differently from the design's wording: item 2's view has no ghosts at all (the log is a component of the logged grouped client's state, beside the owners), so the generic lemma is `stepAtCL_of_stepAtC`, which lifts every CE-11b per-request step of a source14 request other than a raw accept to the logged view whatever the log, and the snapshot moves only on the CE side; the CE side's ghosts are handled by `idealStep_ghost` in `…Mid`. Item (ii) is `step_acceptL` and `step_acceptCL`; item (iii) is `step_serverRead`, proved by `rfl` without `PoolOwned` (both sides reveal the Server's identity slot of the same pool; the slot's value matters only on the CE side, through `serverRead_mid` and the premise of `restOf_middle`).
- An added module. `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterPool` (registered before creation) carries the pool create through the logged layers; LQ-F design-v2 lists only `…Params`, `…Branch` and `…Cut` for unit B1, with the logged pool handlers inside `…Cut`.
- The CE side keeps the ghosts exactly. LQ-F design-v2's fallback F-B and its R4 note kept the ghosts in the middle state; the unit's merge image `mergeXL` carries the compiled program's snapshot and the source log, and `step_mergeMidL` relates the compiled middle step to the handler's step ghosts included, so the real post phase after the run (`laterTail`) is related to the rest of the compiled program directly (`laterTail_middle`), with no state-form split.
- Names. LQ-F design-v2's names that changed: `ideal_cutMarkedL` and `ideal_prefixMarkedL` are `ideal_cutL` and `ideal_prefixL` (for any decision on the LQ-F outcome and the mark), `slotGameAtL_cut` is `laterSlotGameAt_cut`, `itemRest_deadL` and `resumeMarked_deadL` are `itemRestL_dead` and `resumeL_dead`, `CutRelIndexedL` is `CutRelL`, `step_realL` is the pair `stepAtCL_of_stepAtC` and CE-11b's `step_all` (inside `step_allL`), and `server_continuationMid` is `restOf_middle` with `laterTail_middle`. `middleInvL` and `activeBranchL_of_middle` are not separate theorems: `GoodMidL` is CE-11b's `GoodMid` on the compiled program's local copies and the middle state, kept by `goodMidL_step`, and `active_branchL` is proved directly from `activeCE_middleL` and `itemPost_middleL`.
- The Server slot on the CE side. `laterTail_middle` needs the Server's identity slot to hold its seed at the end of the run part (the Server read of the compiled program returns the slot). `GoodMidL` does not carry the identity pool, so `run_slotL` obtains the slot from item 2's invariant `ItemInvL` along the run part (`simulate_keeps` with `keep_allL`, under the run part's bounds from `CutRelL.residual`).
- The logged entry of the selected accept. The CE side appends the scalar `acceptAfter` is given (the second component of the native steps before encapsulation), item 2's resumption appends the ephemeral's secret; `acceptAfter_logged` and `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerSource.beforeLogged_some` show that they agree on every outcome of an active cut.
- The budget route (unit D open point 5). Unit D predicted that unit B could not reuse the classical cut lemmas, which take a `certified` bound in all fourteen source kinds together with `unreadIdentity` and `unreadKem`, and that it would prove the target with per-kind step bounds as the LQ-F chain does (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerReserve.creations`, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompileBudget.compile_server_creates`, `compile_server_no_kem`), with the primed target of LQ-F design-v2's R9 in reserve: in the frozen unit D2 docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterGame.IdealCompileServerTarget`, in unit D's "what is proved", open point 5 and re-estimate here, and in unit D's analysis entry. Unit B took another route, which needs neither: the cut lies in the run part. The compiled LQ-F program is its embedded run part followed by the rest (`compileServer_split`, by `rfl`; the rest issues no raw accept, `restOf_quiet`), so its logged scan is the embedded compiled CE scan of the run part followed by the rest (`compile_scanServer`). The logged copies of the classical cut lemmas (`laterSlotGameAt_cut`, `item2_prefixL`, `kemCommands_stoppedL`; the last calls `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemCommands.creations` and `PqxdhRegistrationJointOpenCosts.command_program` as the classical `kemCommands_stopped` does) are applied only to the compiled scan of the run part, at the run part's budget `runBudget`, which is finite in all fourteen kinds by `budget.Respects first` (`run_certified`, through `FinitePartitionQueryBounds.weighted_bound`, and `scan_certifiedL`), with identity and KEM caps 0 and the creation cap `budget.create` (`runBudget_caps`). The rest needs no cap: in a dead branch it is discarded on both sides (`itemRestL_dead`, through `LazyOracleDiscard.lawEq_drop`), and in the active branch it only has to read no KEM field and create nothing (`restOf_allowed`, `restOf_costly`). Per-kind step bounds and the R9 primed target were not needed; `compile_server_creates`, `compile_server_no_kem` and `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerReserve.creations` are not used (the `…Cut` module docstring says so: "A budget of the whole compiled LQ-F program is not used"). The route sentence of the frozen unit D2 docstring ("The proof therefore cannot reuse the classical cut lemmas … it uses per-kind step bounds, as the LQ-F chain does") is superseded; it is not edited, because the module is frozen. Unit D's open point 5, re-estimate and "what is proved" carry a closing note, and unit B's analysis entry corrects unit D's (added by the review fix pass).
- One review cycle for units B1 and B2. LQ-F design-v2 section 4.7 placed unit B2 on the same branch with a second review cycle; units B1 and B2 were built in one session and reviewed together, at `66e5c888`, in one cycle (the re-estimate below counts B1's and B2's planned review time in that cycle; added by the review fix pass).
- No post budget, as frozen: the rest of the compiled program reads no KEM field and creates nothing for every post-run adversary (`restOf_allowed`, `restOf_costly`, through `post_zero` and `post_zeroK`, which use only that the post part compiles coin, HMAC and BLAKE2b commands), and the identity field reads of the terminal reads and the Server read are simulated requests of item 2's side (`AllowedL`).
- Imports. `…Mid` imports `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterBranch` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile` (CE-11b unit U6, for the classical middle run); `…Steps`, `…Post` and `…Compile` each import the previous one.
- Publication: one Lean commit per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block (`inv recount` before `inv rehash`), its own root import check and its own focused inventory check (the later, still uncommitted modules moved aside to the unit's scratch `stash` during each check); then one documentation commit. Olean prefix: a fresh registered prefix `lqf-up/B/olean` (a resolved symlink mirror of the first prefix of the published `LEAN_PATH` at `adf9d8fc`, `lqf-up/A1/int/olean`, without the root olean, 6361 links) receives the eight module oleans. Development used a separate registered scratch prefix `lqf-up/B/devolean`.
- Process records. A scratch copy of the Branch module (`lqf-up/B/dev/BranchT.lean`, created during development) was deleted with `rm -f` without `coord.sh disk check-delete`, against PROTOCOL's deletion rule; it was the unit's own unpublished scratch file. A backup copy `lqf-up/B/dev/Branch.backup.lean` remains in the unit's scratch. The first focused inventory check of `…Params` failed (exit 2, "unreviewed handwritten-lean file-set drift") because a later module file was present in the tree; it was rerun with the later modules moved aside (exit 0, lease 00000494).

- Same short names with different statements in other namespaces: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSteps.step_identity` (an identity field read at CE-11b's middle state) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSteps.step_serverRead`; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterMid.post_zero` (registration kinds) beside `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterPost.post_zeroK` (every source kind) and `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompile.post_quiet`; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterSteps.simulate_keeps` beside `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSlots.simulateQ_keeps` (no budget).

## LQ-F upstream unit B: contracts used

`Phase1ReadBack codec` and `Phase2ReadBack codec`, approved on 2026-10-06: in `ideal_compile_server` (through the hypotheses of the target `IdealCompileServerTarget`) and as explicit arguments of `…Pool`'s `good_step_poolL` and `compile_simulation_poolL`; `…Cut`'s `pool_scan_sourceL`, `ideal_prefixL`, `cut_goodL` and `cut_relL`; `…Branch`'s `keymatch_later` and `game_markedL` (`Phase1ReadBack` only), `ideal_compile_server_of_selected` and `ideal_compile_server_of_active`; `…Mid`'s `goodMidL_step`, `compile_simulationMidL`, `post_mid`, `restOf_middle` and `laterTail_middle`; and `…Post`'s `activeCE_middleL` and `active_branchL`. The run-phase budget `budget.Respects first`: in `ideal_compile_server` (through the target's hypotheses) and as an explicit argument of `…Branch`'s `run_certified`, `scan_certifiedL`, `ideal_compile_server_of_selected` and `ideal_compile_server_of_active`. No other premise; no new assumption or contract; no post budget. (The review fix pass completed these lists, which first named only the users reached through `ideal_compile_server_of_active` and the middle run.)

## LQ-F upstream unit B: open points

1. Review and integration. Reviewed at `66e5c888` (semantic and hygiene), fixed by the review fix pass ("LQ-F upstream unit B: review fix pass") and confirmed by a confirming review of `409ed4d4` ("LQ-F upstream unit B: confirming review"); the integration (PROTOCOL section 6, status paragraph) is the next step. Central moved to `c27b4533` during the unit (units A2 and CE-12 P2 and item 7 increment A integrated; the first wording of this point said `c72db84a`, which misses item 7 increment A); the branch is based on `adf9d8fc`, so the integration merges `c27b4533` or a newer central first. Central since `adf9d8fc` only adds Lean modules (no existing module changed, no namespace clash with unit B), but it also changes the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean`, `check-inventory.sh`, `reviewed-inventory.txt`, the analysis, this guide, the working plan and the item 2 guide (item 7 increment A alone adds five `PqxdhBoundary*` modules with their root imports, inventory rows and `check-inventory.sh` blocks). These files need the union of both sides, and the handwritten Lean count is recounted after the merge (it will differ from 1910).
2. The later `ideal_world` (`ideal_compile_server` composed with `c6_idealGame_decide`) and the CE-12-L headlines are P3's; with unit A2 integrated, they wait only for this unit's integration.
3. The contamination scan (scratch, `lqf-up/B/probe/Contamination.lean`, item 2's fixed-HKDF list over all 395 declarations of the eight modules) finds no forbidden direct reference; its report list of DH, authentication and T5/T8 infixes finds only the types `MlKem768PublicKey`, `PqxdhJointGapDh.ScalarBytes` and `Ed25519PublicKey` and the field `Phase1KeyTuple.pqKey`, which unit D1 already uses. No T5, theorem A2 or T8 is cited. The hygiene review's module-level reference probe (`lqf-up/B/review-hygiene/probe/Modules.lean`) shows the only other direct references into DH- or KDF-named modules: `PqxdhJointDhExtractFamily.State` and its field `State.cache` (the Extract-table cache record, allowed as in unit A1's review) and `PqxdhJointKdfGame.FixedHkdfSha512NoSaltSource.crypto` through `setup.source.crypto` (in 19 declarations), both allowed by item 2's plan (risk 9: `FixedHkdfSha512NoSaltSource` legitimately appears through `setup.source`) and present in the frozen targets of units D1 and D2 and in CE-11b units U5 and U6; `PqxdhSecretInputKdf.HmacOracle`, `PqxdhSecretInputKdf.RootOutput` and `PqxdhSecretInputKdf.HmacBlock` (the HMAC oracle spec and byte types, allowed); and `PqxdhFullSecretInputKdf.FullOutput` (the 76-byte output type of the full expand, allowed). The first wording of this point left out the last four (the confirming review found the first three in the probe's watch of `PqxdhSecretInputKdf`; the fourth comes from a module the probe's watch list does not cover and was found by the probe of the commit that records the confirming review, `lqf-up/B/int/probe/DhKdfRefs.lean`, which lists every constant of a module whose name contains `Dh`, `Kdf` or `Hkdf` referenced by a declaration of the eight modules: exactly these eight and `PqxdhJointGapDh.ScalarBytes`). The module-level probe also lists the types `PqxdhServerRelatedRootConsumer.Returned` and `Auxiliary`, which the review found allowed. The hygiene review's closure comparison (`ClosureDiff.lean`) finds 29448 constants in the closure of `ideal_compile_server` against 29215 in that of the frozen target together with the classical `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCompile.ideal_compile`, none of those only in unit B's closure from an authentication-, DH- or KDF-named module, and no constant from the T5, T8, RawOpaque, SigningHistory, Provenance or Origins modules. (Added by the review fix pass.)
4. The working plan's record of this unit is left to the plan's owner (the orchestrator); this unit did not edit the plan or LQ-F design-v2.

## LQ-F upstream unit B: re-estimate

Figures in nominal agent-days. LQ-F design-v2 section 6 estimated B0 at 0.5, unit B1 at 1.5-2 (build 1-1.5, 77-113 theorems) and unit B2 at 2.5-4 (build 1.75-3, 122-174 theorems). The build of B0, B1 and B2 took about 2 hours in one session (about 0.3), with 77 theorems in B1 and 68 in B2: B2 reuses CE-11b unit U6's middle run as it stands (the per-request steps, the invariants and the hidden steps), and the new proofs are the ghosts, the logged accept, the identity and Server reads and the rest of the compiled program. Review to integration is estimated at 0.5-1 (B1's and B2's planned 0.5 and 0.75-1 in one review cycle). The review fix pass took about 0.1. No other unit's estimate changes.

## LQ-F upstream unit B: checks

- Each module, after its guards were added, through `coord.sh lean` (Lean 4.34, `-j1 -M8000`, `timeout 180`, `-o` into `lqf-up/B/olean`) against `lqf-up/B/leanpath` (that prefix, then the published `LEAN_PATH` of central `adf9d8fc`): exit 0 and an empty log each (oleans `8e771b14`, `a8848cf9`, `eaec7590`, `285d00bf`, `c33bc39f`, `ac881857`, `37f027b2`, `822ef5a3` for `…Params`, `…Pool`, `…Cut`, `…Branch`, `…Mid`, `…Steps`, `…Post`, `…Compile`). Guards 145 of 145 (3, 29, 24, 21, 27, 28, 12 and 1), generated from a `#print axioms` probe of every theorem; every theorem's axioms are within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound` (144 on all three; `…Pool.onBase_view` on `propext` and `Quot.sound` only; per module in the receipt's `axiom_profiles`; the first wording said "exactly" the three).

## CE-12 P3: what is proved

Every later headline holds for every setup, codec, checks, router, runtime initializer `ensureInit`, fresh-message bounds, observation mode (mode P for CE-APP-L and CE-LOR-L, every mode for CE-KT-L), run-phase adversary `first` within a budget `B` (`B.Respects first`), post-run adversary `second : X → Reveal → OracleComp PostSpec Out` within a post budget `post` on every input (`∀ output reveal, post.Respects (second output reveal)`; `post` caps the post coins, HMAC and BLAKE2b queries `qCoin₂`, `qHmac₂`, `qHash₂`) and runtime state, under the premises of "CE-12 P3: premises and contracts used" (CE-APP-L and CE-LOR-L also need `KemBound setup caps` and a total query bound `qInit` of the runtime initializer, `initBound`), in the LQ-F game `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.game setup codec checks route ensureInit 0 bounds mode first second`: the Server constructor with a generated seed (identity index 0, `supplied = none`), the run phase of `first`, the post-run reveal (`Reveal`: the Beacon-side reveal of the final owners with the snapshot, and the Server's identity seed with the log of every Server ephemeral), and the post phase of `second` on the run's output and the reveal. Events read the LQ-F observation (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerTransfer.observeGame`): the post adversary's output, the run-phase ledger (transcript, test session with the hidden bit, fresh messages) and the produced frames. As in CE-12 P1, these are the experiment's own records in plan section 9's sense, not a public view: the transcript stores mode-O replies (raw-receive plaintexts a mode-P adversary does not see) and `produced` includes frames whose writer failed and that were never published. The reductions behind `kemLossL`, `slotLossL` and `recLossL` run the LQ-F game, including the post adversary `second`, and evaluate the event and the claim (for CE-LOR-L also the filter `F` and the guess); the APP claim and the LOR guess are read from `second`'s output. These advantages are therefore small under the contracts only when the event, the claim, the filter and the guess are efficiently computable and `second` is efficient. For a claim, the target `g = (i, k)` is the budgeted public designation of the claimed handle on the run's transcript, as in CE-9; `D_g` is the event with the claim designating `g`, `d_g` the per-target decision of the compiled LQ-F program (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerTransfer.decision`). Sums run over `Fin qAccept × Fin qCreate` with coefficient one (the column `k = qCreate` is never designated, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterTargetRange.split_create_later`). The events of P1 are used unchanged at the post output type (`X := Out`): the APP claim and the LOR guess read the post adversary's output, produced after the reveal.

- CE-KT-L (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineKeyTransfer`; passive-quantum rows with the key transfer): for an arbitrary event and claim, `ce_kt_later_close`: `|Pr_LQF[D_g] − Pr_{IL_g}[D_g]| ≤ kemLossL g d_g + slotLossL g d_g`, where `IL_g` is the later ideal game `PqxdhDeploymentIdealLaterGame.idealLaterGame` (the challenger's draws, then the later game with CE-11b's ideal registration of `Target.ofCE B g`, in which item 2's two hidden entries are uniform values of a lazily sampled two-entry table keyed by their 128-byte DH coordinates, and the real post phase) with the same decision (`ceDecideL`); one-sided `ce_kt_later`. Summed: the probability that the LQ-F game ends with the event and the claim designated at all (`eligibleL`), and the sum over `g` of the later ideal-game probabilities, differ by at most `Σ_g (kemLossL_g + slotLossL_g)` (`ce_kt_later_sum`; one-sided `ce_kt_later_sum_le`). Later World forms with the later record world of the target's ideal environment and the environment's reveal (`PqxdhRecordLiftLater.run … (envGD …) … envReveal first second`) in place of `IL_g`: `ce_kt_later_world`, `ce_kt_later_world_close`, `ce_kt_later_world_sum`.
- CE-APP-L (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineApp`; passive-quantum rows 169-172), mode P: `ce_app_later`: the probability that the post adversary's output claims index `i` and guess `m` (`appClaim : Out → Option (Nat × Bytes)`), the `i`-th admitted fresh message of the run was sent on a designated handle, and it equals `m` (`AppWin` at `Out`), is at most `Σ_g (kemLossL_g + slotLossL_g + β_g(first) + recLossL_g(dec_g ∧ IS_g) + 2^-min)`. `ce_app_server_later`: the same for a fresh message on a Server peer (`AppWinPeer`), with `ISs_g` and no anomaly term. Per-target forms `ce_app_later_target`, `ce_app_server_later_target`.
- CE-LOR-L (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineLor`; passive-quantum row 173 and the later forms of SSX-b2s, SSX-s2b, SS-b2s, SS-s2b), mode P: for every filter `F` on the mode-P transcript view and every guess `guess : Out → Bool` of the post adversary's output, with the eligibility `LorElig` and the win `LorWin` of P1 at `Out`, `ofReal |Pr_LQF[win] − ½·Pr_LQF[eligible]| ≤ Σ_g (kemLossL_g(W) + slotLossL_g(W) + recLossL_g(dec_g(W) ∧ IS_g) + ½·(kemLossL_g(E) + slotLossL_g(E) + recLossL_g(dec_g(E) ∧ IS_g)) + ½·β_g(first))` (`ce_lor_later`), two transfers per target. `ce_lor_server_later`: every admitted test handle a Server peer (`PeerTests`), `ISs_g`, no anomaly term. Per-target forms `ce_lor_later_target`, `ce_lor_server_later_target`.
- Symbolic corollaries (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineSymbolic`, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineSymbolicLor`): CE-12 P2's symbolic events at `Out`, read on the classical projection `asClassical` of the LQ-F outcome (the Server's public data, the post output, the run-phase ledger and the final deployment state). `ce_app_later_sym`: CE-APP-L's bound + `ε_A2pub + ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`; `ce_app_server_later_sym`: CE-APP-L Server's bound + `ε_A2pub`; `ce_app_beacon_later_sym`: CE-APP-L's bound + `ε_A1 + ctGapTerm`; `ce_kt_later_sym`, `ce_kt_server_later_sym`, `ce_kt_beacon_later_sym`: CE-KT-L's one-sided summed bound + the same terms by side; `symbolic_lor_later`: `|adv_sym − adv_CE| ≤ ½(ε_A2pub + ε_A1 + ctGapTerm + ε_inj + ctCollTerm)` with CE-LOR-L at the NoBundleFailure filter, `symbolic_lor_later_bound` with CE-LOR-L's bound; `symbolic_lor_server_later` (`½(ε_A2pub + ε_inj)`) and `symbolic_lor_server_later_bound`. `ε_A2pub`, `ε_A1`, `ctGapTerm` and `ctCollTerm` are classical terms of the run-phase adversary `first` (on `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game … first`); `ε_inj` is the fixed collider advantage of the setup at `qCreate`, independent of the adversary, which bounds the identity-collision event of that experiment. The gap and collision events are carried to the classical experiment of `first` by `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.game_classical_event`.
- The per-target chain (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineChain`), in the later world: C1-C2 `chain_slot_later`, `chain_slot_later_close` (`kem_transfer_server`, `kem_transfer_server_two_sided`, `later_slots_two`); C3 `chain_ideal_later`, `chain_ideal_later_close` (`ideal_compile_server`); C3-C5 `ideal_world_later` (`ideal_compile_server` with `c6_idealGame_decide`), `ideal_world_view_later` (`laterReal_game`), `chain_view_later`, `chain_close_later`; C6 `later_real_slots_server` (new: the Server-side conjuncts `ServerSlots` on every outcome of the later chain world, from `real_slots_server` through `laterChains_split` and `continueWith_noinstall`), `later_view_unslotted` (`later_slots`, `later_anomaly_eq`), `chain_server_later`, `chain_slots_later` (`later_view_slots_le`), `chain_beta_later` (`anomaly_beta` at `first`); C7 `records_close_later`, `records_le_later` (`later_record_lift` at the frame of `frame_idealD` with `envReveal` and `post`); `split_named_later`.
- Bills (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineBills`): `slotLossL_eq` (the closed form of `slotLossL`), `kem_bills_later`, `slot_bills_later`, `records_bills_later`, `records_hash_later`, `hash_route_later`, `beta_decapsulations_later`, `later_target_bills` (their conjunction); see "CE-12 P3: the terms of the bounds".

## CE-12 P3: the terms of the bounds

- `kemLossL_g` (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineChain.kemLossL`): one ML-KEM IND-CCA advantage of the logged per-target proof-carrying adversary `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerKemHop.certified … (scanned … d_g (compileServer …) g.1)` of `kem_transfer_server`, plus the ML-KEM correctness error. Bills (`kem_bills_later`, from `kem_transfer_server_bills` at the transported caps `laterTransported B post`): prechallenge `(qInit + 32) + (keygenCost·qCreate + V)` uniform, `decapsBase·qFinish` base and `qFinish` logical decapsulation queries, postchallenge `V`, `decapsBase·qFinish` and `qFinish`, with `V = uniformCap caps qCreate qFinish (OpenUniform.total caps (laterTransported B post)) qAccept`; the post budget enters only `V`.
- `slotLossL_g` (`slotLossL`): item 2 over the logged source at two slots, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemTargets.slotLossL` at `laterTransported B post`; in closed form (`slotLossL_eq`) `(qAccept + qFinish + (qHmac + qHmac₂))/2^256` (the R1 guessing term; the post-run HMAC budget enters with coefficient one) plus the two HMAC-SHA-512 PRF advantages of the indexed reductions of `rootClientL` and the key-schedule bounds of the two entry distinguishers `entryDkL` (each four Extract-on-uniform32 and HMAC-PRF advantages). Bills (`slot_bills_later`, from `laterSlot_bills_compiled`): `PrfBills` at `rootBillL caps qInit none qCreate (laterTransported B post)` and `EntryComponentBills` at that bill plus 216.
- `β_g(first)` (`PqxdhDeploymentHeadlineAnomaly.beta` at the run-phase adversary): CE-12 P1's anomaly term unchanged: one IND-CCA advantage of the predict-then-compare adversary built from the anomaly guesser of `first`, the correctness error and `2^-256`. The anomaly reads only the run-phase transcript, which the post phase does not change (`later_anomaly_eq`), so it is bounded on `first`'s run (`anomaly_le_pk`, `ptc_bound`). Phase 1 makes at most `qFinish + 1` decapsulations (`beta_decapsulations_later`).
- `recLossL_g e` (`recLossL`): the later record-lift loss `PqxdhRecordLiftLaterTargets.laterRecordLoss` of the explicit later chain, integrity and (mode P) privacy reductions of `later_record_lift` at the ideal environment `envGD` of the target, the environment's reveal `envReveal`, the post adversary and the post budget, for the view event `e`, with zero aliasing and refusal terms, at the later bill input `inputL = ⟨BI_g, post⟩`. The post phase makes no record request, so the hops are the run-phase hops. Bills (`records_bills_later`): `LaterRecordBounded` with `laterChainBills`, `laterCtxBills`, `laterPrivBills` at `inputL`; their hash bills are `sharedHashTotal BI_g + postHash inputL` (`records_hash_later`: the `qHash₂` term, the post BLAKE2b requests and the environment's class-six allowance per post HMAC request). The CTX hash bill (`laterCtxBills.hash inputL`) is also part of the loss: `laterRecordLoss` passes it to every integrity term (`PqxdhRecordLiftIdeal.integrityLoss`: the hash count of the validation reduction and the term `qHash/2^128`), so the post budget's hash work enters `recLossL_g` itself. The BLAKE2b routes of every later record-world outcome are within `qHash + S + R + qBuild + qFinish + qHash₂` (`hash_route_later`, `c4_ideal_later`).
- Final terms: `2^-min` per target in CE-APP-L (`later_idealRecords_fresh`), none in CE-LOR-L (an exact half in the later record-ideal world, `later_idealRecords_lor`) and in CE-KT-L (the ideal probability stays on the right).
- Symbolic terms: `ε_A2pub = a2pubTerm`, `ε_A1 = a1Term` (at the caps transported from `B`), `ctGapTerm` (F-ct2, explicit) and `ctCollTerm` (explicit; CE-12 P2b's `ct_collision_game` closes it into `ε_ctcoll` on its own branch, not on central) are terms of the classical experiment of `first`. `ε_inj = injTerm setup B` is the fixed collider advantage of the setup at `qCreate`: the sum over the `qCreate + 1` identity indices of the joint weak-EUF advantage of the zero-query collider reductions. It depends only on the setup and `qCreate`, not on the adversary; `identity_collision_game` bounds the identity-collision event of the classical experiment of `first` by it.

## CE-12 P3: premises and contracts used

- CE-KT-L: `FunctionalDecapsulation setup.kem`, `Phase1ReadBack codec`, `Phase2ReadBack codec`, `SourceFullCorrect setup.source setup.hmac`, `B.Respects first` and the post budget of `second` (`slotLossL` is evaluated at the transported caps of the compiled program).
- CE-APP-L, CE-LOR-L: CE-KT-L's plus `KemBound setup caps` and the total query bound `qInit` of the runtime initializer (the frame `frame_idealD` of the later record lift).
- Symbolic corollaries: the premises of the headline and of the P2 terms at `first`. The Server-side forms (`ce_app_server_later_sym`, `ce_kt_server_later_sym`, `symbolic_lor_server_later`, `symbolic_lor_server_later_bound`) add `shortRejects` (for `ε_A2pub`). The Beacon-side forms (`ce_app_beacon_later_sym`, `ce_kt_beacon_later_sym`) add CE-A1's premises (for `ε_A1` and `ctGapTerm`): `KemBound setup caps` (new for the KT forms, since CE-KT-L itself does not need it), `FrameReadBack`, `ReadWritable`, `X25519ClassLaw`, `X25519ScalarSpread` and `HasAttachedVerificationAdapter`. The combined forms (`ce_app_later_sym`, `ce_kt_later_sym`) and the symbolic LOR forms (`symbolic_lor_later`, `symbolic_lor_later_bound`) need both sets. The difference forms `symbolic_lor_later` and `symbolic_lor_server_later` need neither `FunctionalDecapsulation` nor a post budget (any `second`).
- Contracts entering only as advantage terms: ML-KEM IND-CCA, HMAC-SHA-512 PRF, Extract-on-uniform32, the R1 Extract-ROM (statistical term; sharpened caveat: after the reveal the post adversary knows both hidden entries' DH coordinates exactly, so their unpredictability and independence rest on R1 alone), modified-nonce AEAD IND$ and INT-CTXT, the BLAKE2b random-oracle model, functional AEAD correctness; for the symbolic terms, joint Ed25519/X25519 weak EUF and Gap-DH through CE-A1 and CE-A2pub at `first`. No new assumption or contract; no theorem A2, T5 or T8 is cited on the later compiled program: CE-A2pub, CE-A1 and the collision bounds are applied only to the classical experiment of `first` (direct-reference scan in "CE-12 P3: checks").

## CE-12 P3: decisions and deviations

- Modules. design.md section 12 named two modules (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineChain`, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlines`); P3 follows design-v2 section 4's one-module-per-headline layout of the classical headlines: Chain, KeyTransfer, App, Lor, Symbolic, Bills and SymbolicLor (all names registered before creation). Size: 91 theorems against design-v2 section 11's estimate of 40 to 80 for P3. The chain, the three headlines with their World and Server-peer forms, and the bills take 43 (Chain 16, KeyTransfer 7, App 5, Lor 7, Bills 8); the symbolic modules take 48 (Symbolic 24, SymbolicLor 24). The excess comes from the forms added beyond design.md section 9.1 (the item on additions below), chiefly the Server-side and Beacon-side symbolic forms and the later symbolic LOR with its support transfer.
- The anomaly (design-v2 section 10). It is carried to the later world by unit C's `later_anomaly_eq` (inside `later_view_slots_le`), which uses `PqxdhRecordLiftLater.run_event` with `laterReal_source`, the fact `PqxdhRecordLiftLaterWorlds.laterReal_event` is built from, rather than by `laterReal_event` itself; the bound is `anomaly_le_pk` on `first`'s run with `ptc_bound`, so `β_g` is the classical `beta` of `first`.
- U-L4 is unit C's `post_noinstall`, `continueWith_noinstall` and `later_slots`. The Server-side form `later_real_slots_server` is new in P3 (unit C states only `IdealSlots ∨ Anomaly`); it is `later_slots`'s proof with P1's `real_slots_server`.
- The reveal is `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.envReveal`, fixed by `c6_idealGame_decide`; unit C's `envReveal_blind` shows it reads neither the backend nor the shared native state, so it excludes the R1 cache and the hidden slot material (the C6 constraint of design-v2 section 10).
- Events and containments are P1's at the post output type (`X := Out`); no new event is defined for the headlines. The later `ideal_world` is a composition with no new proof (`ideal_world_later`, as unit B's and unit C's guides predicted).
- Additions beyond design.md section 9.1: the later World forms of CE-KT-L (for parity with P1), the Server-peer forms of CE-APP-L and CE-LOR-L (no `β_g`), the Server-side and Beacon-side symbolic forms, and the later symbolic LOR (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineSymbolicLor`).
- Symbolic corollaries. The APP and KT forms need only the probability of the gap events, which read the constructed Server, the run-phase ledger and the owners' identity keys and ignore the caches; `classical_later` moves them to the classical experiment of `first` (`game_classical_event`). The LOR form also needs CE-12 P2's support facts (`lor_inclusion`, `lor_difference`, `lor_difference_peer`) on the LQ-F game: the new support transfer `support_later` (from `game_frame` and `runPhase_classical`) relates every LQ-F outcome to an outcome of the classical experiment of `first` with the same Server, the same ledger and a deployment state equal up to the caches (`Kin`), and each event of the corollary is invariant under that relation (`kin_*`).
- Same short name: `PqxdhDeploymentLaterHeadlineChain.slotLossL` (the per-target abbreviation at `laterTransported B post`) beside unit D's generic `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerItemTargets.slotLossL`, as P1's `slotLoss` abbreviates `allIdealLoss`; consumers that open both namespaces write qualified names.
- Not restated: the uniform and base-query bill of `β_g`'s phase 1 (`ptc_bills` with `guessBill`), which CE-12 P2b proves on `codex/deployment-ce12` (not on central); it applies unchanged to `β_g(first)`.
- The finalize script of the first module stopped at the commit step on a relative message path after every check had passed; `c88029f4` was committed by hand from the checked staged tree and the script was fixed.

## CE-12 P3: not claimed

- Confidentiality only: no later CE-OR, CE-UKS, origin, binding or authentication headline. `game_classical_event` (like the record lift's `laterReal_event`) carries run-phase events to the later game: the symbolic terms are classical run-phase probabilities of `first`, not later authentication claims. No theorem A2, T5 or T8 is cited on the later compiled program (direct-reference scan in "CE-12 P3: checks").
- The adversary is a classical oracle algorithm with a post-run reveal and classical post queries; no QPT or QROM claim and no soundness transfer from the ProVerif passive-quantum rows.
- The classical limits stay: owner-side designation needs the `(S, serverKid)` pin; CE-LOR-L concerns the single test session and is eligibility-weighted; CE-KT-L is a transfer, not `attacker(root)` secrecy; no plaintext-linked UKS, no `INITIAL_SECRET` endpoint.
- `ctGapTerm` and `ctCollTerm` stay explicit probabilities of the classical experiment of `first` (P2's owner flag); `β_g`'s phase-1 query bill is P2b's.

CE-12 P2b (`codex/deployment-ce12`, not integrated) adds `ptc_bills` and `ct_collision_game`; once both lines are on central, billed later forms (with `ptc_bills` at `first`) and closed later symbolic forms (with `ε_ctcoll` at `first`) follow by composition, as P2b's Gated and CtClosed modules do classically.

Each committed module source equals the source of its final check. Guards: 91 theorems with 91 exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms` in declaration order, every axiom list within `propext`, `Classical.choice`, `Quot.sound`; no `sorry`, `admit`, custom axiom, `native_decide`, `implemented_by` or `extern`; no deprecation suppression; no `PqxdhBoundary*` among the 1579 BeaconcryptCore modules of the transitive import closure (the first scan reported 1617, which also counted 38 external top-level import names that it does not expand). Direct references (fix pass; scratch probe `ce12/p3/fix/probe/Refs.lean` through `coord.sh lean claude`, imports only, no `-o`, exit 0): over the 113 constants of the seven modules there is no direct reference to theorem A2 (any constant of `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance`), T5 (`authenticated_*`, `PqxdhRegistrationJointAuthenticatedSelectedKem`) or T8 (a theorem of a `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransfer*` module), and liveness cases for all three are flagged. The only references into modules whose names suggest authentication, provenance, injectivity, EUF, Gap-DH or the KEM key transfer are the types `Ed25519PublicKey` and `PqxdhJointGapDh.ScalarBytes`, the premise `HasAttachedVerificationAdapter`, the identity-collision definitions `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective.Collision` and `identities`, and the bill predicate `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemKeyTransfer.PrfBills`. The symbolic modules cite CE-12 P2's `server_gap_bound`, `sym_gap`, `ce_a1_linked` and `identity_collision_game`, each applied to `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game … first` after `gaps_later`, `serverGap_later`, `beaconGap_later`, `collisions_later` or `identity_later` has moved the event there. Inventory: `coord.sh inv recount` before `coord.sh inv rehash` at every module (handwritten Lean files 1927 to 1934); a focused `check-inventory` in the locked proof shell under a `@nix` lease before every Lean commit (exit 0; the dirt was the three unstaged modified paths `check-inventory.sh`, `BeaconcryptCore.lean` and `reviewed-inventory.txt` and the untracked new module, and each of these seven runs printed three `comm: ... not in sorted order` warnings, which come from the untracked module meeting the locale-sorted `comm -23` of `check-inventory.sh`), on the documentation tree and at the clean head `049f1d5b` (exit 0, no dirt; neither run printed a `comm` warning, and the hygiene review's `LC_ALL=C` check of every commit found no unreviewed tracked proof file); `git diff --check` on every commit.
